# The Letters of John of Dalyatha (La collection des lettres de Jean de Dalyatha)

**Author**: Jean de Dalyatha  
**Series**: PO Tome 39  
**Columns**: pp. 304–522  

[p. 304] {#po.39.p304}
ܬܘܒ ܐܓܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܘܣܒܐ ܪܒܐ ܆ ܕܐܫܬܕܪ̈ܝ ܡܢܗ ܠܐܢܫ ܐܢܫ. ܕܡܢ ܩܪ̈ܝܢܗܝܢ ܡܣܬܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܘܢܝܼܫܗܝܢ.
(Letters of the Saint, the great Elder, sent by him to one person or another. By reading them one will know the purpose.)
ܐ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ :ܕܠܘܬ ܣܒܐ ܚܕ ܪܒܐ ܒܪ ܥܢܝܿܢܗ
FIRST LETTER [To one of his intimate great elders]
ܠܕܒܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܕܒܢܦܫܗ ܐܫܝܕ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ ܒܚܪ ܘܪ̈ܐܙܝ ܪܘܚܐ ܒܝܕ ܪܘܚܐ ܝܺܠܦ ܆ ܘܒܝܕ ܟܘܟܒܐ ܣܓܝ ܨܡ̈ܚܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܒܗ ܕܢܿܚ ܒܢܘܗܪܐ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܡܬܚ̇ܝܕ ܇ ܘܣܘܦ̈ܝ ܬܐܒܝܠ ܘܫܘܘ̈ܟܢܝ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܒܗ ܕ ܐܬܡܼܠܝܘ. ܣܐܘ ܕܪ̈ܝܼܚ ܚܳܝܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܚ̇ܝܐ ܆ ܘܡܬܐܠܨ ܒܣܪܝܘܬ ܡܿܘܬܐ ܠܫܘܘܕܥܐ ܀ ܡܪܝ ܕܝܠܝ ܘܐܒܐ ܕܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ. ܗܐ ܡܢ ܙܒܢܐ ܡܣ̇ܿܟܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܦܓܥ ܀ ܒܒܪ̈ܢܫܟ ܡܠܐ ܩܘܕܫ̈ܐ ܘܠܒܝܼܫ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܘܚܓܝܣܘܬ ܕ̈ܓܠܝ ܥܽܘܦܝܢܝ ܕܫܥܬܐ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܆ ܘܥܠ ܗܢܐ ܡܿܦܩ ܒܪܘܚܐ ܪܘܚܐ ܕܒܟ ܢܣܣܗܕ ܠܟ ܀ ܢܬܼܠ ܠܝ ܡܪܢ ܕܐܚܙܐ ܐܦܟ ܘܢܼܪܘܙ ܠܒܝ ܡܢ ܘܐܥܣܬܝ ܘܐܩܕܡ ܫܥܬܐ ܕܡܥܪ̈ܒܝ ܫܡܫܐ ܕܚܙܘܩܝܟ ܕܠܐܬܪܐ ܢܗܝܪܐ ܕܟܠ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܆ ܘܥܠܡܐ ܥܠܡ ܥܠܡ̈ܝܢ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܬܡܢ ܡܢܝ ܒܥܪ̈ܦܠܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܗ ܕܡܪܟ ܬܬܚܿܦܐ ܆ ܘܠܚܙܬܟ ܠܐ ܐܫܬܘܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܡܥܠ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܗܽܘ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܠܐ ܝܼܠܦ ܢܣܝܢܗ ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ. ܓܗܪ̈ܢ ܓܝܪ ܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܡܕܥܗ ܡܢ ܙܠܺܝܩܝ ܢܘܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܢ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܘܡܬܟܠܐ. ܘܡܕܥܐ ܕܐܙܠܓ ܒܗ ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܨܡܚܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܒܘܣܡܗ ܥܡ
1 To him who, in the divine light spread in his soul, examines hidden things and learns by the Spirit the mysteries of the Spirit; who by the Star with multiple splendors that rises from his heart is, in the light, united to the light; in whom the Spirit has operated gifts that have filled the ends of the earth; who breathes the odor of Life by the Living One (Himself) and who is pressed to make it known by the stench of death! 2 My master, Father of saints! It has been some time since I have been waiting to meet your humanity full of holy things and bearer of God; but I limp, and that has prevented me until now. May the Spirit who is in you guarantee to you the truth of this excuse! 3 May Our Lord grant me to see your face and may my heart rejoice, making me forget my pains, before the hour of the sunset of your departure for the luminous place of all the luminous ones, the eternal world. For perhaps I will be hidden from you there by the clouds of the glory of your Lord, and I will not be worthy to see you, since entry into this place is impossible to one who has not learned to experience it from here below, for the eyes of his intelligence are (then) blinded by the rays of light of the clouds that are outside of there, and it is prevented (from seeing). But the intelligence in which the splendor of Life has shone from here below for its delight, that one, as soon as this sun sets for the pupil


[p. 306] {#po.39.p306}
ܕܢܸܥܪܲܒ ܡܢ ܒܒܬ̇ ܥܝܢܗ ܫܡܫܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܦܠܓ ܝܪܬܘܬܗ ܘܢܸܙܘܩ ܢܦܓܥ ܒܕܢܚܗ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܢܘܗܖܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܥܠ̈ܝܐ ܀
of its eye, distributes its inheritance and departs to meet the apparition of the great Sun which is the light of the worlds from Above.
ܕܢܐܡܪ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟܢܝܘܬ ܡܬܓܠܝܢܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܒܡ̈ܕܥܐ ܡܩ̈ܕܫܐ ܆ ܠܠܫܢܝܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܦܼܣܣ. ܐܠܐ ܣܝܼܡ ܦܘܫܩ ܖܿܐܙܐ ܪܒܐ ܒܡ̈ܕܥܐ ܕܟ̈ܝܐ ܘܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ܆ ܘܛܕܝܼܥ ܒܫܬܩܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܒܐܬܪܐ ܕܬܗܪܐ ܡܬܓܠܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܪܚܿܡܘܗܝ ܆ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܡܝܬܗܪ ܇ ܘܒܕܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܡܫ̇ܬܩ ܙܘܥ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܒܚܙܘܐ ܕܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ ܬܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܗܘ ܕܬܡܗܐ ܆ ܐܦ ܣܝܓܐ ܟܪܟ ܠܗ ܕܫܼܠܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܨܿܒܐ ܡܕܥܐ ܕܢܬܚܪ̈ܐ ܠܡܿܦܩܘ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ ܪܐܙܐ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܩ̈ܠܐ ܘܢܒ̇ܕܩ ܆ ܢܦܓܥ ܒܗ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܫܠܝܐ ܘܢܫܼܬܘܩ ܀
4 As for stating the modality of the revelation of God in sanctified intelligences, that is not permitted to the tongue. But the explanation of (this) great mystery is deposited in pure and luminous intelligences, and it is plunged (there) into silence. For it is in the place of wonder that God reveals Himself to those who love Him: He amazes them by His beauty, and by admiration || reduces to silence their movements at the sight of His mysteries. And as this place of wonderful visions is that of stupor, it is also surrounded by a fence of silence: if the intelligence wants to try to bring the mystery out of it to the place of voices and expose it, the border of silence presents itself to it, and it becomes silent.
ܘܐܬܪܐ ܕܫܼܬܩܐ ܗܘ ܠܫܢܗ ܆ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܒܡܢܝܐ ܡܬܒܿܕܩܝܢ. ܘܐܢ ܠܥܡܘܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܐܬܪܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܡܿܬܡܗܝܢ ܆ ܠܠܐ ܡܥ̈ܝܕܐ ܟܝ ܐܢ ܡܬܓܠܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ. ܒܐܝܢܐ ܒܘܠܗܝܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܩܝܡܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܇ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܙܘܥܐ ܘܕ̈ܓܫܬܐ. ܘܐܬܪܐ ܕܫܡܗ ܕܘܡܪܖܐ ܗܘ ܆ ܐܦ ܦܘܫܩ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܬܡܗܐ ܗܘ. ܘܐܢ ܙܕܩ ܕܡܡܠܠܐ ܢܫܿ̇ܡܗ ܠܗ ܆ ܫܬܩܐ ܗܘ ܕܠܐ ܙܘܥܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܟܘܢ̇ܝ. ܡܒܠܗܐ ܠܥܡܘܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܒܫܘܒܚܗ܆ ܘܡܩܝܡ ܕܝܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܒܬܗܪܗ ܘܨܿܒܥ ܚܶܙܝܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܦܘܫܩ. ܡܕܝܢ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܢܬܟܠܐ ܡܪܚܐ ܆ ܘܒܩܐܡܐ ܕܫܠܝܐ ܢܬܒܠܡ. ܘܡܥܝܠ ܕܠܫܢܝ ܥܓܘܕ ܒܩܘܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܒܫ̈ܬܝ ܆ ܘܕܢܒܿܕܩ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܆ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܡܬܓ̇ܘܣ ܐܢܐ. ܕܐܬ̇ܿܚܢܢ ܡܝܟ ܓܝܙܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܫܸܪܵܐ ܒܐܬܪܐ ܕܫܠܝܐ ܇ ܘܙܗܐ ܒܨܡܚ̈ܢ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܝܿܗܒܬ̇ ܚܙܘܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܗܿ ܠܪ̈ܚܿܡܝܗܿ ܀ ܫܿܟܢ ܠܝ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܼܡܐ. ܕܐܦܢ ܠܛܘܒܐ ܡܫܿ̇ܟܪ̈ܝ ܐܢܐ ܡܢ ܛܘܪܐ ܕܫܘܘܙܒܐ ܠܐ ܐܬܿܪ̈ܚܩ ܆ ܘܐ̇ܗܘܐ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܠܢܘܪܐ ܗܿܝ ܣܕܘܡܝܬܐ ܡܛܲܠܩܬ̇ ܥܘܠ̈ܐ. ܐܒܘܢ ܨܿܠܐ ܥܠܝ ܐ܀ ܡܦܝܼܣ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܕ ܡܛܠ ܕܕܝܠܝ ܘܡܿܪܟܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܝ ܒܒܗܬܬܐ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ. ܘܒܠܝܡ
5 The place whose language is silence, by what would its mysteries be exposed? And since the mysteries of (this) place strike its inhabitants with stupor in silence, if they are revealed to those who are not accustomed to it, in what seizure does it not hold them, depriving them of all movement and all perception? The place whose name is Admiration, the explanation of its mysteries is also stupor; and if it is fitting to name it language, this is a silence without movement and without appellation. (This place) throws its inhabitants into astonishment by its glory; it makes those who dwell there stand in wonder and bathes those who see it in its inexplicable sweetness. Let the audacious one therefore be hindered by silence; let the muzzle of silence make him mute! And since my tongue is numbed by the cold of my evil actions and it cannot expose the beatitudes of the sons of the mysteries of God, I take refuge in mercy in order to obtain your pity, O steward of His mysteries who dwell in the place of silence and shine with the splendors of the hidden Being who gives the vision of His glory to those who love Him. 6 Grant me (your) merciful prayer so that, although a stranger to beatitude, I may not move away from the mountain of salvation and not become the food of this fire of Sodom that consumes the impious. Our father, pray for me, I conjure you with tears, for I bow my head in shame because of (all) this. The tongue of my intelligence is mute for this


[p. 308] {#po.39.p308}
ܠܫܢ ܡܕܥܝ ܡܢ ܦܝܣܐ ܕܬܘܟܠܝܢܐ ܘܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܘܚܓܝ̈ܣܢ ܕ̈ܓܠܝ ܡܢ ܗܠܟܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܛܠܠ. ܐܬܕܡ̈ܡܝ ܒܬܘܩܠܬܐ ܕܒܝܫ̈ܐ ܘܐܬܬܒܪ̈ܝ ܒܦܚ̈ܐ ܕܨܝ̈ܕܝ ܥܘܠܐ. ܒܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܘܫܥܝ ܡܢܝ ܐܬܪܚܩ. ܘܠܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܐܫܟܚܬ ܠܡܚܙܐ. ܡܟܢܫ ܠܝ ܚܢܢܝܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܟ. ܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܠܚܒܪ̈ܬܝ. ܘܡܕܥܟ ܕܢܗܝܪ̈ ܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܒܝܕ ܗܙ̈ܓܐ ܚܳܙܐ ܠܟܠ ܢܣ̣ܥܪܲܢܝ ܒܥܩܬܝ ܕܢܬܠ ܠܝ ܡܪܢ ܕܐܿܫܬܘܐ ܠܛܘܒܐ ܕܚܙܬܟ ܒܪ̈ܓܫܬܐ ܆ ܐܦܢ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܐܚܪ̈ܬܐ ܣܼܡܐ ܐܢܐ ܀ ܕܶܐܬܠܠܬ ܐܟ ܣܟܠܐ ܠܐ ܬܥܼܕܠܢܝ ܆ ܛܒܘܬ ܥܝܢܐ ܕܠܒܟ ܕܝ̇ܩܬ̇ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ ܬܚܿܦܐ ܠܒܝܫ̈ܬܝ ܀ ܒ ܕܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܣܒܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܐ ܠܡܬܕܡܪܘ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܕܚܘܒܟ ܡܟ̇ܣܐ ܡܨܥܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܼܕܬ̇ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܗܘ ܕܥܠܝܡܐ ܢܕܝܕܐ ܐܬܢܿܕܕ, ܘܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܒܪܐ ܕܚܙܬܗ ܘܗܘܦܟ̈ܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܫܝܥܐ ܆ ܙܢܩ ܫܕܝܗܝ ܡܢܗ ܠܪܘܚܩܐ ܕܒܗ ܠܐ ܢܬܛܿܲܢܦ. ܐܠܐ ܕܠܡܐ ܗܘܢܟ ܠܐ ܐܬܢܿܚܬ ܡܢ ܐܬܪܐ ܡܪ̈ܝܡܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܪܡܐ ܠܡܡܫ ܒܐܬܪܖ̈ܐ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܠܡܕܥ ܗܘܦܟ̈ܝ ܚܫܘ̈ܟܐ. ܐܘ̇ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܢܣܒ ܠܗ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܟܠ ܥܠܡܐ ܠܡܕܢܚܘ ܨܡܢܚܺܝ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܥܠ ܝܬܒ̈ܝ ܚܫܘܟܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܶܦܪܘܥ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܟ ܣܿܥܪܐ ܠܘܬ ܒܝܫܘܬܝ ܆ ܢܦܪ̈ܥܟ ܛܒܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܕ ܒܫ̈ܘܘܟܢܐ ܟܠ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܢܦܫܟ ܕܙ̈ܓܬܗܝܢ ܠܐ̇ ܥܿܒܪܐ ܐܡܝܢ ܀ ܡܥܗܕ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ ܕܠܐ ܬܛܥܝܢܝ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܐܛܥܐ ܙ ܘܐܢ ܠܟ ܛܲܥܐ ܐܢܐ. ܛܥܝܐ ܐܺܝܬܝ ܚܬܝܬܐ. ܐܠܐ ܬܚܘܒܓܘܣܟ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܘܠܫ̈ܒܝܐ ܕܒܝܼܪ ܗܘܢܗ ܒܫܒܝܬܐ. ܘܠܘ ܠܫܒܐ ܠܕܝܠܗ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܐܟ
Which is of the confident and free supplication of those who love. My feet limp from having walked (on a path) without shade; they have been bloodied on the stumbling blocks of the Wicked; they have been broken by the snares (laid by) the hunters of the Impious. Since the light of my eyes has been removed from me by my iniquities and I have not been able to see these things, prepare for me the compassion of your prayer as a remedy for my wounds, and may your intelligence, whose movements are illuminated by the meditation that sees all, visit me in my affliction. May Our Lord grant me to be worthy of the happiness of seeing you through the senses, even though I am blind to seeing you otherwise. 7 Do not blame me for having behaved foolishly, like a madman; but may the kindness of the eye of your heart, which looks upon hidden things, veil my evil actions. 2 SECOND LETTER [To another Elder] 1 Admirable is the beauty of your love which hides what is vile! It has not loathed him whom the loathsome world has loathed and whom it has cast and thrown far from itself, like someone whose sight and impious manners it cannot bear, so as not to be defiled by him. But perhaps it is that your spirit has not lowered itself, from the high place that is near the Most High, to grope in a dark place to know my dark manners! Or has it taken the likeness of the great Sun of all worlds, to make the splendors of its grace shine upon those who stand in darkness? As for me, who have nothing with which to repay you for what your grace does for my malice, (I pray) the All-Good to repay you with his visits (to your soul), granting you all that it desires: those things whose desire does not pass. Amen. 2 You remind me: "Do not forget me, so that I do not forget you"; and (you add): "If I were to forget you, I would be a true wanderer." But you have there a (very) weak refuge, whose spirit is led into captivity by


[p. 310] {#po.39.p310}
ܕܐܼܡܪܬ. ܐܠܐ ܡܗܝܡܢ ܐܢܐ ܕܡܬܝܗܒܐ ܠܝ ܪܓܬܟ ܗܕܐ ܆ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܿܘ ܕܐܫܬܘܕܝ ܕܠܐ ܛܿܥܡ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܕܪܐܓ ܬܩ̈ܢܬܐ ܀
a ravisher who is not, as you say, He who ravishes by his beauty that which belongs to him. However, I have faith that what you desire (there) will be given to me by Him who promised that He would not disappoint the will of
ܕ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚܐ ܝܡܝܢܝ ܠܡܨܪ ܨܵܐܪ̈ ܟܠ ܠܚܙܘܐ ܒܨܘܪ̈ܬܐ. ܐܪ̈ܝ̣ܼܡ ܟܠ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܟ ܆ ܕܗܘ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܟܼܣܐ ܠܚܘܕ ܢܫܬܼܟܚ ܠܚܙܬܟ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡ̇ܶܢ ܕܐܬܬܪ̈ܝܡܬ̇ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܚܙܬܗ ܐܪܥܐ ܝܿܠܕܬܗ ܆ ܘܒܝ̇ܠܘܕܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ ܠܥܦܝܦܘܬܗ ܦܫܝܛܐܝܬ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܡܪ̈ܿܕܝܬܐ ܀
one who desires what is good. 3 My hand cannot figure Him who has given figure to all, in such a way that He is seen by figures. Remove everything from before you, so that He who is hidden inside everything may present Himself alone to your sight. Happy is he from before whose sight the earth, his mother, has been removed, and for whose duality there will be, in the Father of his soul, a journeying in simplicity!
ܐܘ̄ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܡܩܝܼܡ ܬܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܒܒܘܠܗܝܐ ܡܗ݀ܠܺ̈ܟܝ ܒܢܘܗܪܐ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܫܦܝܐ ܘܫ̇ܦܝܪ̈ܐ. ܠܐܝܟܐ ܐܙܼܠ ܟܠ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝ ܆ ܕܡܪܐ ܟܠ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܚܙܐ. ܘܐܝܟܐ ܐܺܝܬܝ ܠܐ ܕܐ ܢܕܥ ܐܝܿܐ. ܕܫܒܼܐ ܠܒܝ ܒܫܘܦܪ̈ܗ ܕܫܦܝܪܐ ܀ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܥܰܦܩܟ ܘܕܡܸܟ ܘܣܼܩܗ ܠܪܝܚܟ ܗܢܝܐܐ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܝܼܬܒ ܘܚܼܙܐ ܨܡܥܟܝܰܟ ܕܡܥܿܝܕܝܢ ܒܗ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܙܠܝܢܶܩܝ ܫܡܫܐ ܒܐܣܦܝܪܗ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܚܼܙܝܗܝ ܠܐܘܟܠܗ ܕܡܫܿܚܠܦ ܠܕܡܘܬܟ ÷ ܘܛܥܼܡܗ ܒܚ̣ܟܗ ܘܐܫ̇ܬܚܠܦ ܠܒܘܣܡ ܚܠܝܘܬܟ ܝ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܥܡ ܫܩܝܗ ܚܼܙܐ ܕܚܠܝܼܛ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܘܐܫܼܬܝ ܘܪܘܼܙ ܐ ܠܒܗ ܒܪܚܡܬܟ ܝ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡ̇ܢ ܕܥܼܠ ܠܥܘܡܩܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ ܘܚܼܙܟ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܕܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܘܬܼܗܪ ܒܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܙܝܟ ܕܬܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܿܒܥܝܢ ܡܢܓܘܗ ܕ܀
4 O mystery! Wonder casts into stupor those who walk in the light that is the Lord, a clear and splendid Place: where (then) have all things gone from before me, so that only the Lord of all things is visible? Where I am, I do not know, for my heart is captivated by the beauty of the Beautiful. 5 Happy is he who embraces You, and who, (even) after having fallen asleep, breathes in your delicious odor! Happy is he who sits and sees your resplendences united to him as the rays of the sun to its disk! Happy is he who sees his food changed into your likeness, who tastes it on his palate and (for whom) it changes into the delectation of your sweetness! Happy is he who sees You mixed into his drink, who drinks and whose heart (then) exults with your love! Happy is he who enters inside himself, who sees You with an admirable vision and marvels at the beauty of your mysteries which well up from within him!
ܐܬܥܿܙܙ ܠܒܝ ܗܫܐ ܥܠ ܩܢܝܐ ܐܬܿܒܪ̈ܝܘܗܝ ܆ ܥܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܢܼܨܘܪ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܬܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܕܚ̇ܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܢܗܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܿܒܘܥ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ. ܘܟܕ ܕܢܿܚܝܢ ܡܟ̇ܣܝܢ ܠܫܬܩܐ ܘܓܢܝܼܙܝܢ. ܠܐ ܬܥܼܕܘܠ ܕܫܢܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܕܪ̈ܟܬܢܝ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܬܚܗ ܕܚܡܪ̈ܐ ܡܥܿܕܐ ܟܠ ܘܛܥܝ̈ܬ ܠܕܝܠܝ. ܟܠ ܗܘܢܐ ܣܒܝܼܣ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܥܼܠ ܠܐܬܪܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܐܬܒܼܠܡ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܡܢ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܘܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܒܬܗܖ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܐܙܐ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܡܥܢ̇ܘܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܗ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܚܿܙܝܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܠܩܢܘܡܗ̇ ܆ ܘܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܕܢܝܼܚ ܒܗ̇ ܘܡܪܘܙ ܠܗ̇ ܒܚܙܬܗ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܚܿܙܝܐ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܬܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ ܕܩ̇ܝܡܝܢ ܒܬܗܖ̈ܐ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܚ̇ܝ ܥܠ̈ܡܐ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܒܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܡܬܚ̇ܘܝܐ ܆
6 My heart has just flared up against (my) pen: I am going to break it because it cannot depict wonderful beauties here; for I see rivers of living water flowing from the Source of beatitudes, and although they appear, they are covered and hidden by silence. Do not blame me that here madness has seized me under the blow of the fervor of the Wine that rejoices all, and that I have forgotten what is mine. Every spirit abundant in words that enters this place is gagged by silence, (deprived) of words and movements by the wonder that the mysteries provoke. There, God shows his beauty to those who love Him. There, the soul sees its being and Christ who appears in it and rejoices it by his sight. There, it sees the wonderful beauties of the angels who stand in wonder at the glory of Him who lives eternally. There, the Holy Trinity shows itself


[p. 312] {#po.39.p312}
ܘܠܗܘܢܐ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܝܗ̇ ܡܬܚܼܙܝܢ. ܪܒ ܗܘ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܗܢܐ ܘܠܗܘܢܐ ܕܕ ܫܦܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܥܠܡܐ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܠܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܡܫܝܚܐ ܒܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܡܬܚܿ̇ܘܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܡܬܓ݂ܠܐ ܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ. ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܠܒܪܝܼܬܗ ܡܠܝܼܠܬܐ. ܡܠܬܐ ܐܘܟܠܗܿ. ܘܫܩܝܗ ܚܕܘܬܗܿ. ܫܡܫܗ ܫܡܫܗܿ. ܠܒܘܫܗ ܠܒܘܫܗܿ. ܚܙܬܗ ܚܙܘܗܿ. ܝܼܕܥܬܗ ܡܬܝܕܥܢܗܿ. ܕܡܘܼܬܗ ܕܡܘܼܬܗ̇ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܐ. ܘܥܒܝܼܕܬܐ ܒܥܿܒܘܕܗܿ ܚܳܙܝܐ ܠܩܢܘܡܗܿ ܘܠܥܒܘܕܗ̇. ܟܠ ܕܐܫܼܟܚ ܗܠܝܢ ܆ ܐܫܼܟܚ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܡܩܿܕܡܬܐ. ܘܟܠ ܕܠܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܚܼܙܘ ܆ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܒܝܬ ܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܀
mysteriously, and its Hypostases appear to the naked spirit: great is this mystery, and it is seen only by a limpid spirit. There, the world destined for the dead in Christ appears mysteriously, that is to say that God reveals Himself, (He) the World of worlds, the new World, the Creator of worlds who is a place for his rational creature. The Word is its food; Drink, He is its joy; Sun, He is its sun; Clothing, He is its clothing; His vision is its sight; His knowledge is the object of its knowledge. By grace, His likeness is its likeness; the creature in its Creator sees itself, its Creator. 7 Whoever finds these things finds the anticipated resurrection, and all those who do not see them are still among the dead.
ܓ ܕܬܠܬ ܆ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܆ ܕܒܙܒܢ ܡܚܒܢܗ ܗܘܐ ܆ ܘܒܬܪ̈ܟܢ ܐܫ̣ܼܬܡܥ ܠܒܝܼܫܐ ܘܗܼܦܟ ܡܨܲܚܐ ܠܗ.
3 THIRD LETTER [To a Brother who was once his friend, then obeyed the Evil One and turned against him by insulting him]
ܛܒ ܚܿܕܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܓܘܢ̈ܝܐ. ܘܠܨܥܪ̈ܐ ܝܘܬܪ̈ܢܐ ܚ̇ܫܒ ܐܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܬ ܕܡܕܝܢ ܡܢܐ ܥܿܕܠ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ ܕܡܨܲܚܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ. ܫܼܡܥ ܐܦ ܐܢܬ ܘܐܡܪ ܠܟ. ܡܪ̈ܢ ܓܝܪ ܐܡܼܪ ܕܡܥܡܘܕܝܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܿܥܡܕ ܆ ܘܐܠܝܨ ܐܢܐ ܠܫܘܼܡܠܝܗܿ. ܗ̇ܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܿܡܘܬ ܡܛܿܝܒ ܐܢܐ ܘܡܣܿܟܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܩܒܘܖ̈ܬܐ. ܒܪܡ ܠܘ ܠܝܗܘܕܐ ܪ̈ܚܿܡܗ ܐܙܕܩ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܫܠܡܝܘܗܝ.
1 I rejoice greatly in invectives and I hold outrages as gains. But you will say to me: "Why then do you blame me for insulting you?". Listen to me also say this to you. Our Lord declared: There is a baptism with which I must be baptized, and I am anxious for it to be accomplished. That is to say: "I am ready to die and I await the burial". However, it was not fitting for Judas, his friend, to betray Him.


[p. 314] {#po.39.p314}
ܕ ܕܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܆ ܕܠܘܬ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܐܚ̈ܐ ܝܚܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܆
4 FOURTH LETTER [To one of the hermit Brothers]
ܫܘܡܗܟ ܐܚܝ ܥܠܝ ܗܘ ܡܢ ܛܘܗܡܟ. ܘܐܦ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܟ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܡܿܣܒܗ ܠܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܐܬܟܢܼܝܬ. ܕܢܗܼܘܐ ܨܡܚ ܥܢܟ ܒܢܩܕܘܬܗ ܕ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܙܗܼܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܕܟܝܘܬܗܘܢ. ܘܢܦܫܟ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܡܨܿܠܠܐ ܡܢ ܚܘܫܒ̈ܝ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܢܘܪܐ ܝ̇ܩܕܬܐ ܇ ܒܚ̇ܘܪ̈ܪܐ ܐܡܝܢܐ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܡܝܢ ܕܚܝܪ̈ܟ ܐܦ ܕܕܝܠܟ ܠܘܬܗ ܥܡ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܗ ܕܒܠܒܟ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܩ ܆ ܕܬܣܼܒܝ ܢܦܫܟ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܀
1 Your title, my Brother, is higher than your kinship. May your conduct also be similar to that of those whose name you bear, so that your body may shine with its integrity as they themselves shine by virtue of their purity, and that your soul may be clarified (from the mud) of passionate thoughts. And just as they are a fire that burns with their gaze continually fixed on God, so may your gaze also be directed continually toward Him, accompanied by His incessant praise in your heart, so that your soul may acquire their likeness.
ܐܠܘܨ ܢܦܫܟ ܩܠܝܠ ܡܢ ܣܝܒܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܡܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܢ ܥܢܝ̇ܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܆ ܘܝܬܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܟܫܝܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܕܬܕܥܟܝ ܡܟ ܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܡܿܣܠܝܬܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܩܝܣ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܒܥܠܕܪ̈ܢ. ܕܫ̈ܓܪ ܒܗܘܢ ܠܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܗܕܐ ܝܿܩܕ݁ܬ݁ ܥܿܘܠܐܝܬ. ܥܢܝ̇ܢܐ ܕܥܡ ܥܠ̈ܡܝܐ. ܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܥܡ ܢܫ̈ܐ. ܚܘܒܐ ܕܥܡ ܛܠ̈ܝܐ. ܐܢ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܘܐܢ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܝܬܝܖ̈ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܝܼܫ̈ܢ ܆ ܗܕܐ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܠܦܠܚܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ. ܥܡ ܚܒܪܖ̈ܬܗ̇ ܪ̈ܫܝܥܬܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܬܐ ܕܥܡ ܒܢ̈ܬ ܩܝ̇ܡܐ. ܘܐܡܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܝ̈ܠ̈ܕܐ ܚ̈ܘܘܬܐ ܪܒܘܬ ܟܪ̈ܣܐ. ܥܡ ܚܒܪܖ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܐܚ̈ܐ ܒܛܝܿܠܝ ܡܢ ܦܘܠܚܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܟܕ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܬܫܿܬܠܚܝ ܢܦܫܢ ܆ ܒܡܨ̈ܝܕܬܗ ܕܒܥܠܕܪ̈ܐ ܠܝܡܦܠ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ̇. ܘܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܡܫܟܚܐ ܦܪ̈ܚܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܒܗ ܬܬܦ̈ܨܐ. ܘܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܦܓܪܐ ܛܥܐ ܠܢܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܛܡܐܬܐ ܆ ܟܕ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܚܣܝܟ. ܘܐܦܢ ܢ̇ܒܗܐ ܒܗ ܟܝܢܐܝܬ ܆ ܕܠܐ
2 Constrain yourself (to deprive yourself) a little of food, and totally of the company of men, especially those who are not fervent, so that the loathsome desire may be extinguished in you. For here are the logs with which our enemy maintains this fire that burns in an impious manner: the frequenting of seculars, conversation with women, affection maintained with young boys, whether they be of the world or whether they be religious. And this (last) thing is worse for the servant of Christ than all (other) evils, it and its perverse companion, that is to say, friendship with the Daughters of the Covenant. As for the mother of all these offspring of serpents, it is gluttony, associated with the company of Brothers whose life is devoid of asceticism. When our soul has stripped itself of these things, it will (no longer) be able to fall into the nets of the Enemy; it will be able to fly lightly toward God in whom it will be saved. The body itself forgets this impure desire when it is deprived of these things; and although it arises in it by virtue of nature, it is without difficulty that it vanishes.
ܥܡܠܐ ܚܿܡܝܐ ܀ ܛܪ ܐܚܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܙܘܗܪ̈ܝ ܪܚܿܡܟ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܕܫܓܡܐ ܗܘ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܢ ܐܠܐ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܗܘ ܕܥܝܪ̈ܝ ܪܘܡܐ. ܫܟܬܐ ܣܓܝܐܬܐ ܡ̈ܚܫܟܐ ܠܗ ܠܗܘܢܝܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܢܦܼܪܘܫ. ܥܝܖܪܘܬܐ ܫ̇ܘܬܦܐ ܠܣܪ̈ܦܐ ܥܿܒܕܐ ܠܗ ܆ ܘܡܢ̇ܟܪܝܐ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܚܘܠܛܢ ܐܢܫܐ. ܘܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ ܒܐܡܝܢܢܘܬܗ ܡܬܢܒܪ̈ܫܐ ܒܗ ܀
3 I beg you, my Brother, follow these recommendations of your friend. Our way of life, indeed, is not vain, for it is similar to that of the Watchers of the Height. Sleeping much darkens the spirit, so that it does not discern these things; but the vigil makes him the associate of the Seraphim and makes him a stranger to the commerce of men, while the desire for God is continually inflamed in him.


[p. 316] {#po.39.p316}
ܟܡܐܓܝܪ̈ ܕܚܫ̈ܐ ܒܢܦܫܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܐܘ̈ ܐܚܐ ܆ ܡܕܝܢܝܬܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܠܩܢܐܘܗܝ ܇ ܙܕ ܘܫܓܘܫܝܐ ܕܥܡܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ ܟܠ ܟܪ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕ ܘܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܕܘܝܕ ܩܪܝܬܐ ܡܫܡܗ ܆ ܘܠܦܘܨܝܐ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܒܡܕܒܪܐ ܡܬܓܘܣ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܒܚܪܘܪܐ ܕܡܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ. ܡܐ ܕܐܫܬܘܚܕ ܠܗ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܡܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܀ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܩܪܝܬܐ ܆ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܡܫܟܚ ܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܇ ܕܡܟܝܠ ܡܢܘ ܣܒܪܝ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܐܢܬ ܡܪܝܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܚܙܝܗܝ ܛܒܐ ܕܐܫܬܘܚܕ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܙܘܥ ܆ ܡܫܡܥ ܠܗ ܩܠܐ ܡܥܢܕܝܢܐ ܘܡܠܒܒܢܐ ܐܬܚܝܠ ܐܬܚܝܠ, ܠܐ ܐܫܒܩܟ. ܘܠܐ ܐܪܦܐ ܒܟ ܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ. ܘܠܟܠ ܟܪ ܕܬܐܙܠ ܥܡܟ ܐܝܬܝ. ܘܡܐ ܕܫܡܥ ܗܠܝܢ ܗܘ ܕܝܬܒ ܗܘܐ ܒܝܬ ܒܟܐ ܆ ܫܒܪܐܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܝܠܘܕܐ ܕܐܙܓܫ ܒܝܠܘܕܗ ܩܥܐ ܘܐܡܪ. ܐܒܐ ܐܒܐ ܐܒܐ. ܗܕܐ ܠܚܘܕ ܡܫܬܟܚܐ ܠܗ ܒܥܕܢܐ
4 Brother, as long as the passions are in the soul, man, wherever he may be, is for himself a city (filled) with the tumult of a numerous people. This is what David calls the city from which he delivers himself by taking refuge in the desert, that is to say, in freedom from the passions. When, therefore, man has separated himself from the passions as from the city, he finds with God the confident freedom that makes him say: Who will henceforth be my hope, if not You, Lord? And when the All-Good sees that he has separated himself from everything, He makes him hear a voice that rejoices and comforts (him): "Courage! Courage! I will no longer leave you, I will no longer abandon you; and wherever you go, I will be with you". When he who stood in the midst of tears hears that, he cries out in the manner of a child, like the very little one who has perceived (the presence of) the one who begot him: Abba! Abba! Abba! finding no other words at those moments.
ܗܠܝܢ ܀ && ܘܡܟܐ ܘܠܗܠ ܫܦܝܘܬܐ ܒܬܡܗܐ ܡܩܝܡܐ ܠܗ ܆ ܘܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܝܗܒܝ ܐܝܕܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܙܝܥܐ ܒܗ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܩ. ܘܣܟܐ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܆ ܢܒ̈ܗܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܘܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܘܓܠ̈ܝܢܐ ܘܣܘܟ̈ܠܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܠܡܓܠܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܐ ܡܦܣ. ܠܐ ܡܥܝܠ ܥܗܕ ܗܘܝܗ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ. ܘܝܠܝܕܘܬܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܘܚܒܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܐܢܫܝܬܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܕܝܠܘܕܗ ܐܒܐ ܕܟܠ ܆ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܒܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܘ̈ܥܘܗܝ ܝܗܒܬ ܠܗ. ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܕ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܐܬܚܝܕܘ ܆ ܟܢܝܟܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠܐ. ܗܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܚܒܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܥܡܗܘܢ. ܐܘ ܡܐ ܬܡܝܗܐ ܪܚܡܬܐ ܕܡܢܘܝܢ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܗܘ ܕܗܟܢܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܐܬܚܠܛ ܆ ܘܗܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܛܘܗܡܐ ܡܫܬܘܕܥ ܀
5 For limpidity makes him from then on stand in stupor, and grace does not cease to stir in him movements that do not lend themselves to composition, that is to say, to be concise, motions of the new world, mysteries, revelations, intellections concerning the divine Essence: things that it is not permitted to reveal. He no longer remembers the existence he received from the world, nor any bodily birth, nor any human company: the love of Him who begot him, the Father of all, has given him the forgetting of everything by the transformation of his movements. Those who love have been united to those who love; ineffable gravity is that of the likeness to the angels of light and of their company... Oh! how stunning is the love they show toward him who has been thus mingled with them and has recognized in them parents!
ܝܩܕ ܠܗ ܩܢܝܐ ܡܢ ܥܘܙܗ ܘܕܢܘܪܟ ܝܫܘܥ ܆ ܘܦܫܬ ܝܡܝܢܝ ܡܢ ܕܠܡܟܬܒ. ܐܣܬܝܛܝ ܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܡܢ ܙܠܝܩܝ ܫܘܦܪܟ ܆ ܘܐܫܬܢܝܬ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܒܗ̇ ܪܕܐ ܗܘܝܬ ܆ ܐܬܒܠܗܝ ܠܗ ܡܕܥܝ ܒܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܟ ܆ ܘܐܢܐ ܠܝ ܡܥܝܠ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܝܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܚܒܬ ܫܠܝܗܒܝܬܐ ܒܓܪ̈ܡܝ܆
6 The pen burns from the violence of your fire, O Jesus! My hand has stopped writing, and my eyes have been set on fire by the rays of your beauty. The earth upon which I was advancing has been taken from before me. My intelligence has been struck with stupor by the wonder that You provoke, and henceforth I know myself as no longer existing. A flame has been lit in my bones and springs have gushed forth to bathe


[p. 318] {#po.39.p318}
ܘܐܬܬܪ̈ܥܝ ܡ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܕܢܫܩܝܢ ܠܒܣܪ̈ܝ ܟܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܚܪܟ ܆ ܐܘ ܟܘܪܐ ܡܕܟܝܢܐ ܕܒܗ ܡܪܩ ܓܒܝܠܬܗ ܐܘܡܢܐ. ܐܘ ܐܣܛܠ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܐܫܠܚ ܡܢܢ ܨܒܝܢܢ ܆ ܕܠܒܝܫܝܢܢ ܠܗ ܗܫܐ ܀ ܡܢ ܓܘ ܢܘܪܐ ܐܦܣ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ ܕܐܬܠ ܩܘܕܫܐ ܠܒܢ̈ܝܟ ܆ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܥܠܒܐ ܝܗܒ ܐܢܐ. ܡܙ̈ܓܢܝܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܘܒܟ ܝܡܐ ܫܒܝܚܐ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܡܣܩ ܐܢܐ ܆ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܕܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܠܪ̈ܓܠܐ ܚܙܝܪ̈ܬܢܐ ܠܗܝܢ ܪܡܐ ܐܢܐ ܀ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܟ ܕܟܡܐ ܬܡܝܗܝܢ ܪ̈ܐܙܝܟ ܛܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܕܒܫܘܦܪܟ ܟܠܫܥ ܡܙܕܗܝܢ ܘܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܘܗܒܬܐ ܝܗܒ ܐܢܬ. ܘܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܡܩܕܡܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܡܫܝܚܐ ܕܐܡܪ ܛܘܒܢܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ. ܛܘܒܝܟܘܢ ܐܘ̈ ܝܚܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܆ ܕܗܘܝܬܘܢ ܠܟܘܢ ܥܡ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܚܕ ܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܒܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܡܗ. ܘܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܕܐܒܐ ܠܟܘܢ ܡܬܓܠܝܢ ܘܦܪ̈ܪ̈ܝܣܝܐܝܬ ܐܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܆ ܕܡܕܥܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܘܗܘ ܕܢܚ ܒܠܒܢ ܘܢܗܪ ܒܫܘܒܚܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܘܝܠܝ ܕܒܨܒܝܢܝ ܓܠܙܬ ܠܩܢܘܡܝ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܆ ܘܝܪ̈ܬܘܬܝ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܐܘ ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܐܘ
my entire flesh so that it may not be consumed. O purifying furnace in which the Artisan has removed every stain from his work! O mantle of light that has stripped us of our will so that we may now clothe ourselves in it inside the fire! Permit me, Lord, to give what is holy to your sons; it is not to the dogs that I give (it); but it is to those who love You that I offer the pearls that I bring up from your bosom, O glorious Sea, and I do not throw them to the swine so that they may trample them with their feet. 7 Glory to You! How admirable are your mysteries! Happy are those who love You, for at every moment they shine in your beauty and You grant them Yourself! It is the anticipated resurrection of the dead in Christ of which the blessed Paul spoke. Happy are you, O solitaries, for you have become with the only (Son) one single Son of God by union with Him! That is why the mysteries of the Father are revealed to you, and you say with confident freedom: The intelligence of Christ is ours; He has appeared in our hearts and they have been illuminated by the glory of God. Woe to me who, voluntarily, have deprived myself of these things and have prepared for myself from here below my dark inheritance!
ܛܝܒܬ ܠܝ ܀ ܐܠܗܝ ܐܫܠܚ ܡܢܝ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܟ ܠܟܘܬܝܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܓܝܗܢܐ ܚܫܘܟܬܐ ܆ ܘܐܠܒܫܝܢܝ ܐܣܛܠ ܢܘܗܪܟ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܗܝ ܗܝ ܩܢܘܡܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܆ ܩܕܡ ܕܐܦܘܩ ܡܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ. ܗܒ ܠܝ ܡܪܝ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܙܬܟ ܠܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܘܓܠܝܢ̈ܝ ܪ̈ܐܙܝܟ ܕܟܣܝܢ ܒܥܘܒܗ̇ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܟ ܠܫܩܝܐ ܡܢܕܝܢܐ. ܥܒܕܝܢܝ ܡܪܝ ܗܕܡܐ ܙܕܝܩܐ ܒܓܘܫܡܗ ܕܚܝܕܝܟ ܘܐܙܪܓܫ ܒܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܚܘܝܕܗ ܕܥܡܟ. ܐܟܡܐ ܕܣܦܩ ܟܝܢܝ ܚܠܫܐ ܀
8 My God, strip me by your grace of the tunic of the passions, this dark Gehenna, and clothe me with the mantle of your holy light—which is the new World itself—before I depart from the body. Give me, Lord, the beauty of your vision as food, and the revelations of your mysteries hidden in the bosom of your Essence, as drink that rejoices. Make of me, Lord, a member in the body of your only (Son), that I may perceive the mystery of the union with You, as much as my weak nature can.
ܐܬܚܦܛ ܐܚܝ ܠܡܫܐܠ ܐܘ ܕܘ ܒܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܐܝܡܡܐ :ܕ ܪܚܡܬܗ ܕܡܪܟ ܘܘܗܟܢ ܐܡܼܪ. ܐܒܐ ܛܒܐ ܫܟܢ ܠܝ ܪܚܡܬܟ ܘܐܦܢ ܠܐ ܫܿܘܐ ܐܢܐ ܙ ܒܗܿܓܝܪ̈ ܡܬܛܠܩܐ ܖܪܚܡܬܿ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ :ܘ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܐ ܙܘ ܗܝ ܐ ܗܝ ܣܿܘܦܢܐ ܕܒܥܠܕܒ̈ܒܐ ܘܡܨܿܝܒܢܝܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܡܘܗ̈ܒܢ ܕܡܢ ܥܘܼܒܗ ܕܐܒܐ. ܐܡܐ ܗܝ ܓܝܪ ܝ̈ܠܕܬ݁ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܚ̈ܕܬܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܕܬܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ
9 Apply yourself, my Brother, to asking night and day for the love of your Lord and say this: "(Very) good Father, give me your love, even though I am unworthy of it". By it, indeed, every passionate love is eliminated from the soul; it is it that causes (our) enemies to perish and procures all the gifts that come from the bosom of the Father. For it is the mother of the new mysteries of the new World; that is to say that by it Christ appears


[p. 320] {#po.39.p320}
ܒܗܕܐ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܕ݁ܢ̇ܚ ܒܟ ܆ ܘܦܪ̈ܨܘܦ ܢܦܫܟ ܒܗ ܡܙܕܗܐ. ܘܐܘܘܢܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܗ ܩܕܝܫܐ :ܡܢ ܟܕܘ ܘ ܥ̇ܒܕ ܠܟ. ܡܫܟܢܝܐ ܗܝܓܝܖ̈ ܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܒܡܫܝܚܐ :ܙ ܒܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܐܘ̄ ܐ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܕܬܥܩܘܪ̈ܝ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܐ ܪ̈ܓܬܿ ܥܠܡܐ ܕ ܘܥܘܗ̈ܕܢܘܗܝ ܇ ܘܒܐܠܗܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܬܬܐܣܪܝ. ܕܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܪ̈ܚܿܡܘܗܝ ܘܠܢ ܢܚܿܕܐ ܒܪܚܡܬ ܗ ܐܡܝܢ ܀ ܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܚܡܫ ܐܧ
in you and the face of your soul shines in Him: from now on He makes of you his dwelling, with his Father and his holy Spirit; for the desire for Christ, O my Brother, has the power to tear vigorously from the soul the desire and the memories of the world so that it attaches itself only to God alone. To Him be glory, from all those who love Him, and may He rejoice us by his love. Amen. 5 FIFTH LETTER To him who hears and obeys, the peace of the Sanctifier!
ܠܕܫ̇ܡܥ ܘܡܫܬܡܥ ܪ ܫܠܡܗ ܕܡܩܕܫܢܐ › ܀ ܐܘ̄ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܫܸܡܥ ܠܡ̈ܠܝ ܪܚܡܿܟ. ܠܐ ܐܚܝ ܚܿܒܝܒܐ ّ ܆ ܠܐ ܢܥܼܨܐ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܠܨܒܝܢܢ ܇ ܐܠܐ ܚܢܢ ܢܫ̇ܬܥܒܕ ܠܨܒܝܢܗ ܀ ܨ̈ܒܐ ܐܢܬ ܀ ܕܢܬܚܼܙܐ ܠܟ ܀ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܒܨܠܘܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܪ̈ܚܿܡܗ :ܙ ܪܚܡܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܿܩ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܒܟ. :ܙ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܕܗܕܐ ܒܐܡܢܢܘ ܬܫܬܠܝܗܒ ܒܢܦܫܟ ܐܦܼܩ ܡܢܗ̇ ܐ ܪܚܡܬ ܥܠܡܐ ܐ ܕܲܓܝܓ ܐܢܬ ܕܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܬ݂ܪܶܐ ܕܙ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܙ ܙ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܥܘܡܙܪܵܟ :ܙ ܦܘܼܩ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܟܪ̈ܣܐ. ܘܗܐ ܡܥܝܠ ܚܼܙܝܬܝܗܝ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܫܪ̈ܝܪܪܐ. ܠܐ ܙܝܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܠܡܥܡܪ ܥܡ ܥܠܡܐ. ܙ ܒܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ. ܫܸܡܥܝܗܝ ܟܕ ܡܚ̇ܘܐ ܠܟ ܘܐܿܡܪ. ܕܐܢܿܐ ܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ « ܡܛܠܗܢܐ ܪܕܦ ܠܝ ܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܿܥܡܪ :ܙ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܿܥܡܪ ܥܡܗ ܡܛܠ ܕܣܿܥܐ ܠܝ ܐܡܝܢܐܝܬ ܡܿܐܓܢ ܨܝܕ ܙ ܙ ܢܦܫܐ ܘܣܿܥܪ ܠܗ̇ ܆ ܕܐܢ « ܝ
1 Man of God, hear the words of your friend. No, my beloved Brother, let us not impose our will on Christ, but let us, on the contrary, submit ourselves to his! Do you want Christ to appear to you in prayer as to his friend? May his love be in you without ceasing. Do you desire that this be always inflamed in your soul? Make the love of the world go out from it. Do you aspire that your dwelling be in the Place without place, that is to say in God? Come out of the world as (the newborn comes out) of the womb, and immediately you will see the true (World). (For) Christ cannot dwell with (this) world. I beg you, hear Him warn you of it with these words: "I am not of the world, that is why it chases me from where I dwell. And I cannot dwell with it because it hates me". (But) always He rests upon the soul and visits it, in order to dwell in it if it is empty of what is of the world.
ܣܦܝܩܐ ܡܢ :ܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܢ̣ܥܡܖܿ ܒܗܿ ܐܐ ܀ ܪܐ̈̇ܓܥܐ ܠܟ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܨܡ̈ܥܝܐ ܐ ܕܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܒܢܦܫܟ ܆ ܛܪ ܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܘܗܝ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ. ܐܼܡܪܓܝܪ̈ ܕܗܝܕܝܢ ܬܫܬܟܚ ܠܘܬܟ ܪܚܡܬܝ ܟܕ ܬܸܛܪ ܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܝ :ܝ ܘܒܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܒܢܦܫܐ ܐܸܡܪ ܕܗܼܘ
2 Do you desire to see the resplendences of the beauty of the Holy Trinity in your soul? Keep the commandments of Christ. He said indeed: "When you keep my commandments, my love is found with you"; and when these are perfectly observed in the soul, He comes, just as He declared, to make his dwelling there with his Father and his Spirit;


[p. 322] {#po.39.p322}
ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ ܘܪܘܚܗ ܐܬܐ ܘܥܿܒܕ ܐܘܘܢܐ ܆ ܘܬܡܢ ܥܿܡܪ ܘܡܬܚܼܙܐ. ܐܦܐ ܪ̈ܚܶܡܘܗܝ ܐܸܡܪ ܕܠܐ ܗܘܘ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ :ܘ ܘܣܼܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܠܡܐ :ܐ ܫܘܠܡܐ ܘ ܕܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܘܗܝ .ܐܧ ܙܩܝܦܐ ܘܐ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܛܶܥܝܘܬܐ ܘܡܬܢܫܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܪܲܓܬ̇ ܙ ܝ ܥܠܡܐ. ܘܝ̈ܐܝܒܘܬܐ ܘܣܘܘܚܐ ܒܫܘܠܗܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܠܫܘܢܝܐ. ܐܝܟ ܗܺܝ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ. ܘܒܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐܐ :ܕ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܬܘܟܠܢܝܐ ܐܿܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܫܪܖ̈ܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܐܕ ܆ ܕܥܡܗ ܕܕ ܕܫܿܠܚ ܡܕܥܐ :ܕ ܠܥܠܡܐ :ܕ ܠܒܫ ܠܡܫܝܚܐ. ܥܡܗ ܕܢ̇ܦܩ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܕ ܦܿܓܥ ܒܐܠܗܐ ܕ ܥܡܗ ܕܦ̇ܣܩܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܡܥܗ̇ ܕ ܥܢܝ̈ܢܝ ܥܠܡܐ ܆ ܙܡܪ ܒܗ̇ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܡܬܡ̈ܠܠܢܐ. ܪܐܙܐ ܗܘ ܠܝ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܩܼܢܛܐ ܗܘ. ܐܠܐ ܠܫܖ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ ܙܕ ܫܪ̈ܪܐܓ݂ܠܐ », ܐܘ̄ ܒܒ̈ܬܐ ܐܢ :܀ ܬܙܝ̈ܥܢ ܧ+܀܀ ܠܫܼܩܝܐ ܆ ܢܫܦ̈ܥܢ َّ܀ ܬܦ̈ܐ ܒܫܦܥܝ̈ܗܝܢ ܕܡܢܝܐ ܒܡܢܝܐ ܡܢܿܠܦܝܢܢ. ܘܪ :ܘܝ ܠܝ ܪ ܀
it is there that He dwells and is seen. (Now) of his friends also He said that they were not of the world and that the world hated them. (And this, because) the perfect observance of his commandments is the Cross: in other words, the lack of knowledge and the forgetting of the lusts of the world and the ardent desire to emigrate from it that the burning of love provokes, as was the case for Saint Paul. It is with confident freedom toward my God and with assurance that I say in all truth that at the very moment when the intelligence strips itself of the world, it clothes itself in Christ; that immediately withdrawn from the preoccupation of worldly affairs, it meets God; that from the moment when the soul breaks with the companies of the world, the Spirit psalmodizes in it his ineffable mysteries. This is for me a mystery that inspires fear in me, but for the truthful it is a manifest truth. O pupils, if you made (your tears) flow to irrigate (the earth), the overflowing canals would flood! For what indeed are we exchanging this! Woe to me! Woe to me!
܀ ܡܫܝܚܐ :܀ ܕܦܼܪܥ ܚܽܘܒܬܐ ܐ ܕܚܼܒ ܨܒܝܢܢ ܣܟܠܐ ܒܕܡܗ ܙܟܝܐ ܘ ܦܼܬܚ ܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܡܕܥܢ ܕܢܼܕܥ ܠܐܝܟܐ ܪܕܝܿܢܢ. ܢܘܗܪ̈ܟ ܕܗܼܘ ܡܿܢܗܖܪ ܫܡܫܢܐܝܬ ܠܬܓܡ̈ܝ ܩܘܕܫܟ ܆ ܗܼܘ ܢܝ̇ܒܠܢܝ ܥܕܡܐ ܘܝ ܠܘܬܟ. ܘܢܟ ܡܪܝ ܢܡܨ̈ܥܢܝ ܒܝܢܬܗܘܢ ܆ ܐܦ ܐ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܒܥܠܡ ܐ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕ܀. ܘܢܐ̇ܠܦܢܝ ܣܼܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܬܝܐ ܠܟ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܐ ܫܡܢܼܥܐ. ܒܪ̈ܝ̣ܢܝ ܡܪ̈ܝ ܒܪܖ̈ܝܼܬܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܡܕܡܼܝܬ̇ ܠܫܘܦܪܟ ܆ ܘܢܬܢܼܫܐ ܘܢܛܼܥܐ ܠܟܝ̇ܿܢܢ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܀
3 O Christ who with your pure blood have paid the debt that our foolish will had contracted, open the eyes of our intelligences so that we may know toward where we are advancing. May your light which illuminates like the sun the choirs of your Holiness lead me up to You. May your Spirit, Lord, place me in their midst, from here below and in the world of light, and may He teach me their language so that I may proclaim with them your inaudible praise. Create in me, Lord, a new creature resembling your beauty, that we may forget and ignore our previous nature.
ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܫܦܝܥܘܬ݀ ܪܲܚܡܬܟ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܠܐ. ܦܬܝܼܚ ܗܘ « ܡܪܝ ܬܪ̈ܥܟ ܘܠܝܬ ܕܥ̇ܐܠ ܓ݂ܠܐ ܫܘܒܚܟ ܘܠܝܬ ܕܡܐܨܕ. ܕܢܝܼܚ ܘ ܢܘܗܪܟ ܒܒ̈ܒܬܐ ܝ ܘܠܐ ܨܿܒܝܢܢ ܠܡܥܝܙܐ ܙ ܦܫܝܛܐ ܝܡܝܢܟ ܠܡܬܠ ܘܠܝܬ ܕܢ̇ܣܒ. ܡܓܿܙ̈ܓ ܐܢܬ ܒܫܘܘ̈ܕܠܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܬܦܝܼܣܝܢܢ. ܡܕܚܿܠ ܐܢܬ ܒܣܘܪ̈ܕܐ ܡܙܝ̈ܓܝ ܒܪ̈ܚܼܡܐ ܘܠܐ ܥܿܪ̈ܩܝܢܢ ܠܘܬܟ. ܐܠܗܢ « ܛܒܐ ܚܘܼܣ ܥܠ ܫܦܠܢ. ܒܳܪ̈ܘܝܢ ܒܣܝܡܐ ܥܨܘܼܒ ܬܒ̇ܪ̈ܲܢ. ܐܒܘܢ ܗ ܡܠܐ ܚܿܘܣܢܐ ܙܘ ܆ ܐܘܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܐܦܝܼܣ ܐ ܆ ¢ ܘܥܨܝܢ ܘܩ̇ܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܐܘ ܠܘܬܟ ܀ ܕܚܢܢ ܕܢܦܝܼܣܟ ܠܐ ܨܿܒܝܢܢ. ܕܘܐܦܩܼ ܢܦܫܢ ܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܚܒܘܫܝܐ ܕܚܢܢ ܠܢ ܚܼܒܫܢܢ ܆ ܠܢܘܗܪܟ ܫܪܝܪ̈ܪܐ ܐܦ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܨܿܒܝܢܢ ܀, ܢܚܼܣܢܢ ܡܪ̈ܝ ܚܺܝܠܟ ܆ ܘܢܕܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܛܘܒ̈ܥܐ ܕܠܗ
4 Glory to the overflowing of your ineffable love! Your door, Lord, is open, and there is no one who enters. Your glory is manifest, and there is no one who contemplates (it). Your light illuminates the pupils, and we do not want to see. Your hand is stretched out to give, and there is no one who takes. You incite by forethought, and we do not consent; You frighten by perils mixed with mercy, and we do not flee toward You. O our (very) good God, have pity on our misery! O our sweet Creator, bind our fractures! O our Father full of clemency, persuade Yourself to force us to approach You, since we ourselves


[p. 324] {#po.39.p324}
ܥܳܠܐ ܡܪܝ ܡܢ ܩܕܡ ܚܙܬܢ ܆ ܟܠ ܣܼܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܚܙܬܗ̇ ܕܢܦܫܢ ܡܬܚ̇ܦܝܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܕܠܡܚܙܐ ܢܘܗܪܖܟ ܫܪܝܪ̈ܐ. ܘܒܗ ܢܩܘܼܡ ܥܪ̈ܛܠ ܒܐܡܝܢܘ ܕܠܐ ܦܣܿܩ. ܒܐܦ̈ܐܓܠ̈ܝܬܐ. ܘܢܟܿܬܪ ܒܓܬܗ ܘܒܒܘܣܡܐ ܕܫܘܦܪܗ ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ ܐܡܝܢܢ ܀
do not want to persuade You! Bring our soul out of the prison in which we have locked ourselves and (lead it) to your true light, even if we refuse it! May your power, Lord, prevail over us and pull us from the drowning toward which we lean. Remove, Lord, from before our sight all the veils with which the eyes of our soul are covered and which prevent them from seeing your true light: that in it we may stand stripped, always and without ceasing, and
ܘ ܕܫܬ ܐ ܥܠ ܡܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܀ ܗܼܒ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܖ ܠܐܚ̈ܐ ܐܘ ܐܒܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܹܐܡܘܢ ܒܫܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܣܟܠܐܝܬ ܒܟܠ ܡܡܠܠܐ. ܘܐܦܠܐ ܕܢܿܥܗܕܘܢ ܫܡܗ ܒܡܘܡ̈ܬܐ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ. ܐܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܣܿܦܩܝܢ ܠܚܫܚܬܐ. ܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܒܝܼܫܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܆ ܕܡܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܥܿܒܕܐ ܠܢ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܝܼܥ̈ܐ. ܠܝ̣ܛ ܗܘ ܡܶܢ ܕܚܹܐܡܐ ܒܫܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܒܟܕܒܘܬܐ ܀ ܕܠܘ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܗ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܣܢܐܗ ܚ̇ܬܝܬܐ ܇ ܐܝܟ ܕܗܼܘ ܚ̇ܘܝܢܝ ܘܥܨܢܝ ܕܐܫܘܕܥܟ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܕܒܫܼܙܘܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܒܼܩ ܒܣܕܝ ܠܓܪ̈ܡܝ ܡܢ ܬܢܚܳܬܝ ܇ ܡܛܠ ܕܗܟܢ ܬܿܚܡ. ܕܠܐ ܐܿܬܠ ܛܝܒܘܬܝ ܒܢܦܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܝܥܐ ܡܢ ܫܡܝ ܕܫܒܝܚܐ ܘܐܦ ܡܬܟܢܝ ܕܐܘܐ ܐܠܐ ܡܒܿܣܪܢܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܡܬܚܿܫܚܐ ܐ:܀
remain in the desire and the delectation of its beauty, for ages of ages. Amen. 6 SIXTH LETTER [On swearing] 1 Father, give the Brothers the order not to swear by the Name of God in a foolish manner at every word (they say), and even not to mention his Name in any case in oaths. It is enough to use yes and no. What a calamity this is, which makes us strangers to God and unknown to Him! Cursed be he who swears falsely by the Name of the Lord, for he is not part of his own but is truly his enemy, as He Himself showed me and pressed me to let you know. Truly, my flesh, in this vision, stuck to my bones from the force of groans, for thus did He decide: "I will not make the gift of my grace in the soul that does not fear my glorious Name and does not even revere it, but uses it without caring for it".
ܐܢܬ ܕܝܢ ܪ݁ܚܿܡ ܠܡܪܗ ܐܐ ܛܸܢ ܚܠܦ ܫܡܗ ܆ ܕܡܝܬܓܕܦ ܒܦܘܡ ܣܟ̈ܠܐ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ. ܟܠ ܕܡܬܚܿܫܚ ܒܗ ܩܕܡܝܟ ܆ ܟܼܐܝ ܒܗ ܘܐܒܼܗܬܝܗܝ ܐ ܠܐ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܙܥܘܪܝܐ ܒܥܝܢ̈ܝܟ ܐܘ̇ ܬܥܼܕܘܠ ܠܣܘܪ̈ܛܝܢ. ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܚ̇ܘܝܢ ܕܢܫܘܕܥ ܠܟ ܘܟܠ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܥ̇ܗܕ ܐܢܐ ܠܚܙܘܐ ܡܬܬܙܝܼܥ ܠܒܝ ܡܢ ܠܘܚܡܗ ܚܿܝ ܗܘ ܡܪܝܐ. ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܫ̇ܡܥ ܘܢܛܪ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܐܦ ܠܪ̈ܚܿܡܗ ܕܪܚܝܼܡܐ ܆ ܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܠܐ ܩ̇ܒܠܬ̇ ܡܫܡܥܬܗ ܠܗܕܐ ܐ ܫܡܘܥܬܐ ܡܢ ܦܘܡ ܡܪ̈ܚܐ ܀
2 As for you, O friend of your Lord, be zealous for his Name which the mouth of the foolish blasphemes foolishly. Whoever uses it before you, reprimand him and confound him. May it not be in your eyes a matter of no importance; and do not criticize these lines either, (for) it is the Lord who showed us (this) so that we might make it known to you; and each time I remember (this) vision, my heart is agitated and detaches itself, as true as the Lord is living! Happy is he who hears and puts into practice! And happy is the friend of the Beloved if his hearing does not accept to hear (swearing) from the mouth of the impudent!


[p. 326] {#po.39.p326}
® ܕܫܒܥ ܐ ܆ ܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܀ ܙ ܡܟܝܠ ܐܢܐ ܡܢ ܡ̈ܠܐ ܫ̈ܠܐ ܖ ܐܢܐ. ܚܕ ܚܠܦ ܟܠ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܝ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܚܠܦܘܗܝ ܟܠ ܠܐ ܐܫܼܟܚ ܕܢܡܿܠܐ. ܐܣܬܿܟܠ ܐܚܝ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܗܢܐ ܆ ܕܗܘܼܝܘ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܥܠ̈ܝܐ ܘܕܝܠܢ ܘܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܒܥܠ̈ܡܐ ܕܠܐܓܿܡܪ̈ܝܢ. ܡܫܢ̈ܩܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܒܪܚܡܬ̇ ܫܦܝܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܗܿܢܘ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܟܠ ܦܘܠܚܢ. ܛܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܠܕܪ̈ܘܝܢ ܒܚܘܒܟ ܐܠܗܝ ܆ ܕܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܟ ܆ ܐܕܪ̈ܟܬ̇ ܐܢܘܢ ܫ̇ܢܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܘܛܸܥܘ ܚܫܚܬ ܗܘܢ ܩܕܡܝܬܐ ܀ ܛܥܡ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܘܚܼܙܝ ܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܕܐܒܘܢ ܛܒܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܢܝܐܐ. ܠܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܣܝܢܗ̇ ܠܐ ܫܸܩܠܘ ܆ ܡܢ ܡ̈ܠܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܐ ܡܬܘܡ ܡܬܝܕܥܐ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܒܘܣܡܐ ܆ ܠܪ̈ܚ̇ܡܘܗܝ ܒܗ ܡ̇ܪ̈ܘܐ ܘܡܒܿܣܡ. ܗܼܘ ܒܗ ܚܕܐ ܆ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܒܗ ܡܥܿܕܐ. ܗܘܼܝܘ ܫܘܦܪܿܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܗ ܆ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܠܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܫܘܼ̣ܒܚܗ ܡܫܿ̇ܚܠܦ ܒܓܠܝܢܗ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܗܘܼܝܘ ܡܫܬܘܬܗܘܢ ܘܓܢܘܢܐ ܕܚܕܘܬ ܗܘܢ ܚܿܙܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܗܘܢ ܘܪܘܿܙܝܢ. ܕܚ ܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܒܗܘܢ ܘܡܿܬܡܗ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܀ ܕ ܛܘܒܝܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܕܗܼܝ ܠܗ̇ ܡܚܙܝܬܐ ܝܿܕܥܐ. ܕܒܗ̇ ܡܿܨܕܐ ܘܚܿܙܝܐ ܙܝܼܘܗ ܕܟܼܣܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܗܿܘ ܕܒܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܐܸܡܪ ܕܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܚܿܙܐ ܠܝ ܘܚܳܝܐ ܆ ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܗܢܐ ܡܬܚܙܐ ܕܙ ܘܠܥܠܡ ܚ̇ܐܝܢ ܚܙܝܘ̈ܗܝ. ܐܘ̄ ܠܚܘܼܒܟ ܐܠܗܢ ܆ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܛܸܥܡܘ ܪܒܘܬ ܚܠܝܘܬ ܗ ܇ ܗܘܘ ܣܝ̈ܐܐ ܠܟܠ ܒܘܣܡ ܀
7 SEVENTH LETTER [of the saint] 1 I am henceforth going to cease speaking. In place of everything I have had one single thing: Him in whose place the all could not satisfy. Understand, my Brother, this mystery which is the life of the beings from Above, and ours also like theirs, in the centuries that do not end. Let us be tormented by the love of the Beautiful: that is the outcome of all asceticism. Happy are those who are drunk with your love, O my God! for their drunkenness with You has delivered them to the attacks of madness, and they have forgotten what, previously, was necessary for them. 2 My Brother, taste and see the sweetness of our All-Good Father and how lovable He is. Those who have not experienced it, words will never make them know it. He who is for Himself delectation inebriates and delights by Himself those who love Him. He rejoices in Himself and He rejoices them by Himself; He is Himself the beauty of his Being and He transforms them into the likeness of his glory by his revelation in them; He is Himself their wedding banquet and the bridal chamber where they rejoice. They see Him in them and they are in gladness; He appears in them, from the inside of themselves, and He stupifies them by his beauty! 3 Happy is the soul that knows itself to be a mirror, that fixes its eyes there and sees the splendor of Him who is hidden from all. (For) He who, on the mountain, said: No man will see me and remain alive, is seen in that place, and those who see Him live eternally. O our God, what love is yours, that those who have tasted the immensity of his sweetness have become enemies of all enjoyment!
ܕܝܠܗ ܕܬܡܢܐ ܐ :ܘܥܠ ܕ ܗܢܘܢ ܕܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܟܢܘ̈ܫܝܐ ܀ ܘܓܘ̈ܕܐ ܘܡܩܝܡܝܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܢܐ ܘܦܩܘ̈ܕܐ ܘܡܕܠܩܒܝܢ ܠܚܕܕܐ ¢
8 HIS EIGHTH LETTER [concerning those who gather to form clans, give themselves superiors and leaders, and oppose one another]
ܐܙܕܗܪ ܐܚܝ ܀ ܡܢ ܡܘܒ̈ܕܝ ܢܦܫܗܘܢ ܽܿ ܘܢܦܫ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܚܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܝܨܦܝ ܀
1 Beware, my Brother, of those who lose their souls and those of their brothers, by concerning themselves with charges and establishing superiors. From the work of God they have fallen into the labor of painful stubbornness.


[p. 328] {#po.39.p328}
ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܡܩܝܡ̈ܢ ܪ̈ܝܫܢܐ. ܢܦܠܘ ܓܝܪ̈ ܡܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܠܬܥܫܐ ܕܡܩܝܡܘܬ ܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ ܬܥܝܫܐ. ܘܨܒܝܢ ܕܢܥܒܕܘܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܓܒܐ. ܡܫܥ̈ܒܕܝ ܠܣܛܢܐ ܕܠܘܚܐ ܙܙ ܕܡܫܝܢܐ. ܘܐܢ ܣܓܝ ܡܠܙܝܢ ܠܟ ܆ ܦܢܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܡܘܟܟܐ ܘܬܘܟܠܝܐ ܕܥܠ ܡܪܟ ܠܡܬܪܨܘ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܟܝܒܬܐ ܢܙ ܦܩܬ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܆ ܠܘ ܝ ܕܐܬܪܨ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܩܠܝܬܝ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܘܟܢܘܫܝܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܟܢܫܐ ܕܚܘܫ̈ܒܝ ܘܚܫ̈ܝ. ܙ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ :ܐ ܙܕܩ ܠܝ ܕܐܩܘܡ ܪܝܫܐ ܡܦܪܢܣ ܙ :ܙ ܥܡܘܪ̈ܝ :ܐ ܒܝܬܝ ܐܙ ܫܦܝܪ. ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܝܨܦ ܐܢܐ. ܕܒܗܘܢ ܦܩܕ ܢܙ ܪܥܝܐ ܐ ܟܠ ܠܡܙܕܗܪܘ ܀ ܘܡܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܡܗܘܐ :ܐܧ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ. ܘܐܢ ܡܬܚܡܬܝܢ ܒܗܕܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܛܒ ܪܥܐ ܒܗ̇ ܀
with which they want to make themselves many companions and partisans: (these are) people subject to Satan, the disturber of the peaceful. If they bother you too much, answer them by humbling yourself and trusting in your Lord: "It is to direct my guilty soul that I left the world, and not to direct others. My convent is my cell; the gathering in which I participate is that which consists of collecting my thoughts and (containing) my passions. That is what I should be made superior of; such are the inhabitants of my house of whom I must take great care. When it is so, I will occupy myself with other people: those whom the Shepherd of all will have commanded me to watch over, while remaining a stranger to others." If they are irritated by these (words), God, He, will be very satisfied with them.
ܚܣܡܐ ܠܐ ܢܚܙܐ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܠܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܐ ܥܕܠ ܒܚܣܡܗ. ܕܐܡܝܢ ܡܪ̈ܛܢ ܕܓܝܙ ܡܢܗ ܕ ܫܐ ܐܠܗܐ. ܕܡܪܝܡ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܫܝܘܠ ܡܬܬܚܬܐ. ܕܡܡܟܟ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܡܪܗ ܐܧ ܡܬܕܡܐ. ܪܚܡ ܨܒܬܐ ܐ ܦܥܠܐ ܗܘ ܕܕܝܘܐ ܐܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܕܥܠ̈ܡܝܐ ܐ ܒܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܆ ܒܥܠܕܒܒܐ ܗܘ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܕܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܙܕ ܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܘ ܕܡ̈ܠܐܟܐ ܀
2 The envious will not see the light, for, in his envy, he criticizes the luminous. He who murmurs incessantly draws upon himself the wrath of God. He who exalts himself is brought down to Sheol. He who humbles himself makes himself like his Lord. He who aspires to be charged with something is the agent of the demon of fornication. He who loves to mingle with seculars is the enemy of God. He who loves the saints is the companion of the angels.
ܚܘܨ ܢܦܫܟ ܪܦܝܐ ܕܐܟ ܘܬܝ ܗܐ ܩܠܝܠ :ܕ ܘܐ̈ܬܐ :ܕ ܪܡܫܐ ܆ ܕܢܕܡܟ ܘܢܬܬܢܝܚ ܡܢ ܥܡܠܢ ܕ ܇ ܘܢܩܘܡ ܒܨܦܪܐ ܕܚ̈ܕܘܬܐ ܀ ܛ ܕܬܫܥ ܐ ܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܐ.ܕ
3 Strengthen your soul, (you) who like me is a slacker: a little more time, and here comes the evening when we will sleep and rest from our fatigue; then we will rise in the morning of joy... 9 NINTH LETTER [of the saint
ܠܝܬ ܠܢ ܕܦܪ̈ܨܘܦ ܚܕ̈ܕܐ ܢܚܙܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܨܥܝܘܬܗ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܐ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܕܢܚܙܐ ܟܣܝܘܬ ܢܦܫ̈ܬܢ ܘܢܚܕܐ ܬܘܒ ܀ ܒܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܒܗܝܢ ܆ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܨܥܝܘܬܗ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܘܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܠܡܬܕܡܪܘ ܒܚܘܒܟ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܚܠܦ ܕܟܕ ܙܠܝܩܝ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܢܦܫܝ:܀ ܠܐ ܡܬܘܡ ܚܙܝܬ ܆ ܠܗ̇܀ ܥܡ ܗܘ ܕܒܢܦܫܟ ܪܦܬ ܕܠܐ ܫܠܝܐ ܡ ܟܦܩ ܐܢܬ ܇ ܘܣܘܘܚܗ̇ ܝ ܚܠܐܛ ܐܢܬ ܒܣܘܘܚܗ ܕܗܘ ܕܒܠܒܟ ܡܝܬ ܬ ܙܝܥ ܇ ܡܫܢܐ ܟܠ ܙܘܥ̈ܝܢ. ܕܠܐ ܒܗܘܘܢ ܥܡܗ ܀ ܥܡܘܪ̈ܐ. ܐܠܐ ܢܦܪܥܟ ܡܪܢ ܚܠܦ ܚܘܒܟ ܕܠܘܬܝ ܕܓܠܝܢܝܗ ܕܒܢܦܫܟ ܕܗܘܝܘ ܚܘܒܐ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܀
1 We cannot see each other's faces without the intermediary of created light. Likewise, we cannot see the hidden (depths) of our souls, nor rejoice in the mysteries they contain, without the intermediary of the creative light. How then would I not be astonished at your unfailing love, since, although you have never seen the rays of Life in my soul, you embrace it (and press it) against that (love) which is constantly stirred in your soul, and you mingle its impulse with the impulse of what moves in your heart and drives away all (other) movements so that they do not cohabit with it. Be that as it may, may Our Lord reward you for your love for me by His revelation in your soul, He who is the true Love!


[p. 330] {#po.39.p330}
ܝ ܕܥܣܪܐ ܐ ܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܩܕܝܫܐ ܐ ܕ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܚܘܗܝ ܕ
10 TENTH LETTER OF THE SAINT [It is one of those that were sent to his brother]
ܝܥܠܦܬ ܥܠ ܐܝܟܢܝܘܬܟܘܢ ܆ ܘܫܒܚܬ ܠܡܪܢ ܥܠ ܚܘܠܡܢܟܘܢ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ. ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܒܗܝܬܐ ܆ܓܠܝܙ ܐܢܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܝܬܪ̈ܘ. ܘܫܟܝܚܐ ܀ ܠܝ ܗܕܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܕܐܟܠ ܘܕܡܟ. ܫܬܐ ܘܡܕܫ ܀ ܕܫܕܐ ܨܦܬܗ܀ ܡܣܟܐ ܠܡ̈ܘܬܐ. ܒܪܡ ܐܡܪ ܙ ܐܢܫ ܆ ܕܐܝܬ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܟܕ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܡܗܠܟ ܇ ܐܘ ܥܠ ܣܦܪ̈ܝܗ̇ ܀ ܩܐܡ ܐܘ ܠܒܪ̈ ܡܢܗ̇ :ܙ ܇ ܐܝܬ ܐܡܬܝ ܫܪܝ ܦܗܐ. ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܙ :ܐ ܡܫܬܬܩ :ܙ ܒܡܥܒܕܢܘܬ ܚܝܠܐ :ܙ ܐܚܝܕ ܟܠ ܘܛܥܐ ܢܙ ܗܘ ܠܗ ܘܠܟܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܗ. ܘܟܕ ܙܐ ܪܐ ܬܡܝܗ ܘܬܗܪ :ܙܐ ܒܫܘܦܪܪܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܐܬܢܓܕ. ܡܢ ܕܡܣܬܟܠ ܢܣܬܟܠ. ܘܟܠ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܟܠ ܢܬܠ ܠܗ ܡܪܢ ܕܢܕܪܟ. ܒܪܡ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܨܘܪ ܒܨܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܨܐܪ ܙ ܟܠ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܐܝܬ ܒܝ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܒܚܘ̈ܩܐ ܕܝܘ̈ܬܢܝܐ ܙ ܐܫܬܥܝܘܗܝ :ܙ ܠܫܘܦܪܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܘ ܘܪ ܠܝ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܬܫܡ̈ܫܬܢ ܚܣܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܡܪܐ ܟܠ ܢܫܬܟܚ̈ܢ. ܘܝܐ ܕܝܢ ܥܦܝܦܝܐ ܠܢܦܫܢ ܢܬܠ ܆ ܐܡܬܝ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܡܢܢ ܠܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܢܡܢܥܢ ܇ ܐܐ ܕܡܪܗ ܐ ܕܒܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܢܫܡܫ. ܐܠܐ ܢܫܠܐ ܘܢܬܡܗ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܢܫܟܚ ܐܐ ܠܗܘ ܕܫܠܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܨܐ ܕܢܡܠܠܝܘܗܝ ܀ ܨܠܐ ܥܠܝ ܡܛܠ ܡܪܢ. ܕܐܬܪܗ ܠܓܥܘ ܡܢܟ ܬܓܕ ܠܢ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܢܥܘܠ ܆ ܟܕ ܕܝ ܚܢܢ ܒܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܢܒܥܝܘܗܝ ܇ ܕܠܘܬܢ ܢܦܘܩ ܢܬܚܙܐ. ܡܘܚܕ ܢܘܪܗ ܒܢܦܫܢ ܘܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܗ ܒܗ̇ ܡܢܘܐ ܆ ܐܢ ܢܫܟܚܝܗ̇ ܕܡܨܠܠܐ ܡܢ ܪܛܝܒܘܬ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܗܘܠ̈ܢܝܐ ܀ ܝܐ ܕܚܕܥܣܪܐ ܐ ܐ
1 I have learned how you are and I have praised Our Lord for your good health in God. As for me, I am ashamed to be devoid of all virtue. One finds in me only this: I eat and I sleep, I drink and I let myself go. He who rejects (thus) all concern for himself must expect death. Yet someone said that sometimes, when he walks on the path, or stands on the edge of it or (even) outside of it, it happens that he begins to lose himself: he is suddenly reduced to silence by the operation of the All-Powerful Force, and he forgets himself as well as everything that is his, stunned and amazed by the beauty that has attracted him. He who understands, let him understand! And whoever does not understand, may Our Lord grant him to grasp it! As for me, I cannot figure by figures Him who has given figure to all, and I do not have the capacity to depict by lines of ink the creative Beauty. Woe to me, when our services will be found deficient by the Lord of all! Yes, we will proclaim our soul doubly miserable when all things among us will have reached their completion, that we will not have served the Master of the house! But let us be silent and be in astonishment, in order to find Him of whom only silence can speak. 2 Pray for me, for (the love of) Our Lord, so that His place within you may attract us, so that we may enter (there) to Him, when we will beg Him with desire to come out to us to appear to us || will light His fire in our soul and will make seen in it His operation, 51} finds it purged of the humidity of material passions. 11 ELEVENTH LETTER
ܥܠܬܐ ܠܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܕ ܗܘܬ ܠܢ ܐܓܪ̈ܬܟ ܗܝ ܡܠܝܬ ܥܩ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܘܛܘܒܐ ܀ ܝܗܒ ܐܢܐ ܪ ܠܕܘܝܘܬܢ ܕܫܘܬ ܠܡܫܬܐ ܟܣܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܝ ܦܪ̈ܘܩܢ. ܟܕ ܡܦܣ ܘ
1 Your letter full of affliction has been for us a reason to give thanks, and I proclaim our misery blessed that it has been


[p. 332] {#po.39.p332}
ܐܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܡܫܬܝܐ ܡܪܿܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܆ ܢܒ̈ܥܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܫܒܝ̈ܚܢ ܕܒܗܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܿܙܝܢ ܟܫܝܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܫܒܝ̈ܚܐ ܥܒ̈ܕܢ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܕܡܫܿܒܚܢܗܘܢ ܀
rendered worthy to drink the cup of the sufferings of our Savior; for I know that from the bitter drink drunk because of Him spring those glorious things in which the diligent rejoice and which make them glorious in the glory of their Glorifier.
ܐܬܠܝܛܐ ܕܚܐܪ ܠܟܠܝܠܐ. ܠܐ ܢܬܩ̇ܛܥ ܡܢ ܥܘܫܢܐ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܀ ܟܠܝܠܐ ܕܡܥ̇ܠܠ ܒܗ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܠܪ̈ܚܶܡܗ ܒܫܘܠܡ ܬܟܬܘܫܗ ܆ ܚܙܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ. ܛܘܒܝܟ ܐܸܢ ܠܗܢܐ ܢܝܫܐ ܬܸܚܘܪ ܡܬܟܬܫܢܐ ܆ ܘܠܒܣܬܪ̈ܟ ܠܐ ܬܼܦܢܐ ܀ ܘܐܢ ܬܼܒܠܥ ܒܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܣܢܐܝ̈ܟ ܐܼܢܳܐ ܆ ܗܐ ܡܠܟܟ ܕܘܩܐ ܩܪܒ ܘܗܼܘ ܒܗ ܡܿܐܣܐ ܫܘܡܡܬܟ ܘܡܕܗܢ ܠܟ ܒܡܫܚܐ ܡܒܿܣܡܐ ܡ̣ܫܚܐ ܗܘ ܕܚܕܘܬܐ ܆ ܘܡܥܝܼܕ ܗܕܡ̈ܝܟ ܠܐܝ̈ܐ ܕܐܬܛܲܪܦܘ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܕܚܼܫܟ ܫܡܫܐ ܒܿܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܒܒ̈ܬܟ ܡܢ ܬܘܩܦܐ ܕܥܩ̈ܬܐ. ܡܢ ܫܠܝ ܒܚܼܠܦܐ ܢ̇ܿܣܒ ܐܢܬ ܠܐܬܖܐ ܕܫܡܫܗ ܒ̇ܪܘܝܐ ܗܘ. ܘܡܿܦܨܚ ܐܦܝ̈ܟ ܒܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܛܸܠܠ. ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܕܐܨܛܒܥܘ ܒܕܡܐ ܕܚܒܪ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܚܘܼܒܗ ܕܚܬܢܐ ܦܼܐܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܀ ܗܢܘܢ ܨܿܡܚܝܢ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܐܝܬܝܝܐ ܐܦ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ. ܕܠܗܘܢ ܝܿܗܒ ܪ̈ܗܒܘܢܐ ܀
2 The athlete who looks toward the crown will not be discouraged by the harshness of the combats. And the crown with which Christ adorns him who loves Him, at the end of his struggle, is the vision of the Holy Trinity. Happy are you if you look toward this standard, 6 fighter, and if you do not turn back! If you are struck by arrows by your enemies, here is your King who observes your combat and who Himself heals your wounds; He anoints you with delectable oil, with the oil of joy, and relaxes your tired limbs which have exhausted themselves for Him. Because the external sun of your pupils has darkened due to the violence of adversities, you receive immediately in exchange the place whose sun is the Creator, and (this sun) makes your face radiate in the light without shadow. As for the limbs that have been stained with the blood of their wounds for the love of the Bridegroom more beautiful than all, they shine from here below with the essential glory of which He gives them the earnest.
ܠܐ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܥܰܪ̈ܘܩܐ ܡܢ ܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܇ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܡܘܬ ܓ݁ܳܙܪܺܝܢ ܡܢ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܡܠܟܝܐ. ܢܸܩܪܵܐ ܠ ܪܲܒ ܚܺܝܠܢ ܠܐܝܿܠܢ. ܘܗܼܘ ܝܗܒ ܠܢ ܙܟܘܬܐ. ܢܚ̇ܘܐ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܡܚܝܠܘܬܢ. ܘܗܼܘ ܗܿܘܐ ܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܒܗܕܡ̈ܝܢ. ܢܗܘܐ ܨܗ̈ܝܐ ܟܠܫܥ ܠܚܙܬܗ ܆ ܘܡܥܫܢ̇ܘܐ ܠܢ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܦܪܨܘܦܗ ܀
3 No, my Brother, let us not desert the battle, like those who condemn themselves to death by virtue of the royal law. (But) let us call to our aid the Leader of our army, and He will give us, He, the victory. Let us expose our weakness before Him, and He will Himself be the strength (which will spread) in our limbs. Let us be at all times thirsty for His vision, and He will show us the beauty of His face.
ܟܠ ܕܓ݂ܠܙܘ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܆ ܢܸܣܒܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܗ ܠܒܘܼܣܡܗܘܢ ܟܠ ܕܐܫܼܬܝܘ ܐܼܒܣܢܬܝ ܘܓ̈ܕܕܐ ܘܡܕܪ̈ܐ ܒܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܕܥܡ ܩܢܘܡܗܘܢ ܆ ܕܢܫܿܚܠܦܘܢ ܨܒܝܢܗܘܢ ܠܨܒܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܗܘܢ. ܗܢܘܢ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܣܘܡ̈ܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܝܬ ܐܒܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܼܡܪ̈ ܗܽܘ ܚܿܙܝܐ. ܡܫܝܚܐ ܗܘ ܦܬܘܪ̈ܗܘܢ. ܕܡܢܗ ܕܝܢ ܠܐܒܐ ܡܣܿܬܝܒܪ̈ܝܢ. ܥܠ ܕܡܕܪ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܣܝܒܪ̈ܬܗܘܢ ܒܦܘܡܗܘܢ ܒܕܡ̈ܥܐ ܘܬܢܚ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܐܟܠܝܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܕܠܒܐ. ܥܠ ܕܫܼܛܘ ܪܲܓܬܐ ܙܗܘܡܬܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬ ܪܚܡܬܗ ܆ ܒܗܘܢ ܐܦ ܕܢ̇ܚ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܙܬܗ ܕܙܪ̈ܓܝܓܬ̇ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܪܕ̈ܓܬܐ ܥܽܘܠܬܐ ܥܕ ܩܠܝܠ ܚ̇ܡܝܐ ܀
4 All those who, for His love, have reduced themselves to destitution, have received what is His for their delight. All those who have drunk wormwood and bitterness in the struggle against themselves with a view to changing their will into that of their Creator, become guests delighting in what is in the house of the Father, as that (illustrious) seer said: "Christ is their table, at which they feed on the Father." Because their food has been spoiled in their mouth by tears and groans, they eat God in the gladness of (their) heart. And because in the drunkenness of His love they have despised the fetid desire, the very beauty of His vision more desirable than all appears in them, and the evil desire is quickly extinguished.


[p. 334] {#po.39.p334}
ܐܿܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܝ ܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܫܪܝܪ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܬܦܿܠܓ. ܕܟܕ ܡܢ ܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܝ̇ܩܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܐܼܢܳܐ ܆ ܒܚܘܡܣܢܗ ܓ̇ܥܐ ܘܐܿܡܪ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܠܗܐ ܟܕ ܐ̇ܿܡܪ. ܐܠܗܝ ܛܒܐ ܆ ܗܼܒ ܠܝ ܡܘܬܐ ܕܦܓܪܐ. ܕܠܐ ܐܡܘܬ ܡܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܒܟ. ܠܐ ܡܖܪ̈ܝ ܐܼܢܳܐ ܕܚ̇ܫܚ ܐܢܐ ܠܩܪ̈ܒܐ. ܕܐܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܦܠܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܒܗ ܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܠܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܠܬܟܬܘܫܐ܆ ܣܼܒ ܡܢܝ ܚܫܐ ܘܩܪ̈ܒܐ. ܘܡܬ̇ܚܝܡ ܗܘܐ ܕܐܼܢܳܐ ܆ ܕܟܕ ܨܒܼܐ ܒܝ ܕܓܿܒܘܠܝ ܘܣܥܼܪ̈ܢܝ. ܡܢ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܒܙܗܝ̣ܪܘܬܐ ܪܒܬܐ ܐܿ̇ܚܕ ܐܼܢܳܐ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܢ ܦܓܥܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܫܐ ܆ ܘܡܢ ܥܘܗܕܢܗܝܢ. ܟܠ ܐܡܬ ܕܦܿܓܥ ܐܢܐ ܒܗܝܢ ܐܘْ ܒܚܘܫܒܝ ܘ ܥܘܗܕܢܗܝܢ ܀ ܣܿܠܩ ܆ ܗܟܢܐ ܡܬܗܼܦܟ ܓܘܝ ܘܡܬܚ̇ܿܒܬܐ ܬܐܪܬܝ ܇ ܕܒܥܡܠܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܕܐ̇ܣܒ ܣܝܒܪ̈ܬܐ ܒܝܘܡܐ ܗܘ ܡܢ ܥܘܗܕܢ ܗ̇ܝ ܣܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ. ܘܒܩܪ̈ܝܒܘܬܗܝܢ ܠܐ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܐܢܐ ܠܡܐܟܠ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ ܆ ܡܬܬܙܝܼܥ ܓܘܫܡܝ ܘܡܬܗܟܟ ܡܢ ܛܟܣܗ ܡܢ ܥܘܫܢܗ̇ ܕܣܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ. ܘܐܡܪ ܗܘܐ. ܕܐܦ ܡܢ ܦܓܥܐ ܕܕܟܪ̈ܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܥܕܟܝܠ ܛܠܝܢ ܆ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܼܚ ܙܗܡܘܬܗܘܢ ܨܲܿܒܪ ܡܘܚܝ. ܗܟܢܐ ܐܣܼܢܝܗ̇ ܐܠܗܐ
5 A trustworthy Brother told me - let no one doubt this! - that when he burned with the foul desire of fornication, he cried out to God with perseverance, saying to Him: "My Very-Good God, give me the death of the body so that I do not die to the Life that is in Thee! I am worth nothing, my Lord, for the combat. Therefore, as one (man) struck down who has in him no strength to fight and struggle, remove from me the passion and the combat." And he affirmed this to me: "Since my Creator was pleased to visit me, I avoid with great care meeting women and remembering them; for every time I meet them or their memory comes to my mind, the inside of myself is so upset and my conscience so deteriorated that I can only with great difficulty take food that day, because of the memory of that stench; and in their presence it is totally impossible for me to eat, for my body is agitated and upset by the violence of the stench." To which he added: "It is also when I meet boys still young that my brain deliriums due to their foul odor." God thus made repulsive to his eyes what captivates the slackers.
ܒܥܝܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܗܕܐ ܫܿܒܝܬ݁ ܠܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܀ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܦܪ̈ܫܘ ܛܥܡܐ ܕܡܐܟܠ̈ܬܐ ܡܢ ܥܘܫܢܐ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܆ ܐܦ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܕܗܼܢܬ̇ ܢܦܫܗܘܢ ܒܚܠܝܘܬ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܘܐܨܛܒܥܘ ܒܗ ܓܪ̈ܡܝܗܘܢ. ܘܐܦ ܗܘܼܝܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܣܘܩܐ ܡܒ̇ܣܤܝܡܢܐ ܕܚܿܝܠܢܐ ܬܠܝܬܝܐ. ܟܠ ܕܛܸܥܡܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܆ ܠܐ ܡܬܼܩܛܥܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܣܘܓܐܐ ܕܢܣܝܘ̈ܢܐ. ܘܟܠ ܕܨܿܒܝܢ ܕܢܛܥܼܡܘܢ. ܠܐ ܢܬ ܩܛܥܘܢ ܆ ܕܗܐ ܣܒܪܐ ܠܚܘܝ̇ܠܗܘܢ. ܘܥܕ ܩܠܝܠ ܕܢܿܚ ܫܡܫܐ ܕܚܕܘ̈ܬܐ ܠܓܥܘ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܆ ܘܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܟܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒܗܝܢ ܐܬܛ̇ܪ̈ܦܘ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܒܘܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܠܦܘܬ ܥܘܫܢܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܟܡܝܪ̈ܢ ܆ ܗܟܢ ܣܓ̈ܝܐܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܕܼ̈ܓܝܓܢ. ܡܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܢܬܩ̇ܛܥ ܒܐܘܠܨܢܝܢ ܐܚܝ̈ܢ ܆ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܡܫܼܬܒܩܝܢܢ ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܚܿܝܠܢ ܘ ܡܗ̇ܝܡܢ ܗܘ ܛܲܒܐ. ܕܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ ܐܡܝܢ ܀
6 Those very ones who have (no longer) distinguished the taste of food due to the violence of the combats, the anointing of the sweetness of the Creator has dilated their soul; their limbs have been impregnated with Him, and He Himself is for them the embalming breath of the Trinitarian Force. None of those who have tasted these things is discouraged by the multiplicity of temptations; let none of those who want to taste (them) lose (therefore) courage, for they have there a hope that will comfort them; and before long the Sun of joy will rise in them, and they will no longer remember that by which they will have been overwhelmed at the time of the trial: as much as the sad things will have been hard, so much will the desirable things abound. Thus, therefore, Brothers, let us not be discouraged in our afflictions, for we are not abandoned (to trials) above our strength. Faithful is the Very-Good. To Him be the glory for ever and ever. Amen.


[p. 336] {#po.39.p336}
ܐܓܪܬܐ ܕܬܪܬܥܣܪܬܐ ܥܠ ܨܠܘܬܐ
12 TWELFTH LETTER [On prayer]
ܩܳܐܡ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܬܚܬ ܡܢ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܐܡܝܢ ܡܨܿܠܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܒܝܕ ܙܘܥ̈ܐ. ܐܠܐ ܐܿܡܪ ܐܢܬ: ܠܐ ܬܔܕܦ ܐܚܐ ܆ ܝܬܝܪ̈ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܥܡ̈ܠܝܢ ܠܗܕܐ ܡ̇ܘܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܐܒܗ̈ܝܢ. ܘܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܡܩ̈ܠܣܢܝܗ̇ ܐܝܿܬܝ ܆ ܘܕܒܗ̇ ܢܓܡܖܘܢ ܫܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܝ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܝ ܫܳܐܠ ܐܢܐ. ܘܕܪ̈ܒܐ ܗܝ ܡܘܕܐ ܐܢܐ. ܕܘܕܪ̈ܡܝܐ ܗܝ ܆ ܘܪ̈ܡܬ̇ ܗܝ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܥܡ̈ܠܝܢ. ܡܕܝܢ ܫܚܝܼܩܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܡܢܝܼܚܬ̇ ܠܠܐܝ̈ܐ ܆ ܕܡܐ ܕܐܬܬܢܝܼܚܘ ܠܐ ܬܘܒ
1 The monk who continues to pray to God by means of movements still stands below the degree of perfection. But you will say to me: "Do not blaspheme, Brother! Our Fathers exalt this (prayer) more than all works." I too am one of those who praise it, and I ask my God that the rest of my life may be completed in it. I recognize that it is great and that it is sublime, more sublime than all works, since, laborious (like them), it relaxes those who have tired themselves and (causes) that when they have been relaxed they no longer tire!
ܠܐܿܝܢ ܀ ܐܣܬ̇ܟܠ ܐܚܝ ܐܡܼܪ ܓܝܪ ܠܫܡܥܘܢ ܪܝܫܐ ܕܫ̈ܠܝܚܝܐ ܠܟ ܐܬܠ ܩ̈ܠܝܕܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ ܕܬܼܐܚܘܕ ܘܬܸܦܬܚ ܠܟܠ ܕܨܒܐ ܐܢܬ. ܘܠܘ ܠܗ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܝܼܗܒ ܗܢܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܰܚܡ̇ܝ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ. ܨܠܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܢܩ̇ܫܐ ܕܒܬܪܥܗ ܕܝܿܗܘܒܐ. ܘܐܝܢܐ ܕܥܼܠ ܠܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܘܐܫܿܬܠܛ ܥܠ ܓܙ̈ܝܗ̇ ܆ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܢܿܩܫ ܥܠ ܬܪܥܐ. ܐܠܐ ܡܢܐ. ܡܬܒܿܣܡ ܒܛܘ̈ܒܐ. ܬܲܡܗ ܘܡܬܕܡܪ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܕܛܳܒܐ ܆ ܕܓܘܚܟܐ ܗܝ ܠܡܐܡܪ ܕܡܨܿܠܝܘ ܡܨܿܠܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܗܢܝܐ ܟܠܗ ܪܘܐ ܒܫܘܦܪ̈ܗ ܕܚܬܢܐ ܦܼܐܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܀
2 Understand (well), my Brother. (Our Lord) said to Simon, the head of the Apostles: I will give you the keys of the Kingdom of Heaven, so that you may close and open to whom you wish. And it is not only to him that He gave this power, but to all lovers of the Truth. (Now) prayer consists of knocking at the door of the Giver; but he who has entered into the Kingdom and who has received power over its treasures, how would he knock at the door? But then (what does he do)? 1} enjoys the goods (which are inside), he is in astonishment and admiration before the beauty of the Very-Good. And it would be laughable to say that he prays properly speaking, he who is to this point intoxicated by the beauty of the Bridegroom more beautiful than all!
ܨܠܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܿܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܕܗܘܢܝܐ ܗܿܝ ܕܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܩ̇ܿܛܥܝܢ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܡܢ ܕܢܼܚܗ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܗܘܢܐ. ܐܿܡܪ ܓܝܪ ܕܒܝܕ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܢܘܗܪܐ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܡܬܦܣܩܐ. ܡܕܝܢ ܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܬܗܖܪܐ ܗܘ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܼܡܪ̈ܢܢ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܡܝܢܘܬ ܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ. ܐܝܢܐ ܕܥܼܠ ܠܐܬܪܐ ܕܖ̈ܐܙܐ ܆ ܒܬܗܪܐ
3 "Prayer, it has been said, is the purity of the intellect, a purity from which the movements of prayer are cut only by the rising of the holy light of the Trinity upon the intellect." It is therefore said that by the wonder caused by the light, prayer is interrupted. Perfection is therefore indeed, as we have said, the wonder caused by God, and not that the movements of prayer last. He who has entered into the place of mysteries remains in the wonder they provoke,


[p. 338] {#po.39.p338}
ܕܒܗܘܢ ܡܩ̈ܘܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܦܬܚܬܿ ܬܪܥ ܓܙܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܠܡܬܠ ܠܒܥ̈ܝܐ ܡܘܠܝ ܣܘܢܩܢ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܡܓܢ ܠܡ ܢܣܼܒܬܘܢ. ܡ̇ܓܢ ܗܒܘ ܐܦ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܐ ܠܟܠ ܕܨ̈ܒܝܬܘܢ. ܘܐܝܟܢ ܢܿܡܪܚ ܐܢܫ ܠܡܐܡܪ. ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܫܿܬܠܛܘ ܥܠ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܐܦ ܕܢܼܬܠܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܠܟܠ ܕܨܿܒܝܢ ܆ ܕܒܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܢܸܩܫܘܢ ܐܓܪ ܚܕܘܪ̈ܐ ܫܐܠܺܝܢ ܙܕܩ̈ܬܐ ܠܡܘܠܝ ܣܘܢܩܢܗܘܢ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ. ܐܠܐ ܡ̈ܦܠܓܝ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܡܢܚ̈ܡܝ ܡܝ̈ܬܐ. ܛܥ̈ܝܼܢܝ ܣܒܪܐ. ܝܗ̈ܒܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܠܣܡ̈ܝܐ. ܐܢܬܘܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܸܡܪ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ. ܠܟ ܐܿܬܠ ܩܠܺ̈ܝܕܐ. ܠܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܒܥܳܝܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܕܒܕܝܠܟ ܩܼܢܐ ܐܢܬ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ܆ ܠܡܐܣܖ ܘܠܡܫܪ̈ܐ. ܒܥܠܡܐ ܗܢܐ ܘܒܥܠܡ ܥܠܡܝܢ. ܘܐܝܟܢ ܡܬܓܙܲܓܚ ܗܢܐ ܥܠ ܬܪܥܐ ܕܢܫܹܐܠ ܐܝܟ ܥܰܒܪܐܘܪܚܐ ܆ ܘܩ̈ܠܝܕܐ ܕܒܝܬܓܿܙܐ ܒܐܝܕ̈ܘܗܝ ܣܝܼܡܝܢ. ܠܡܣܒ ܐܦ ܠܡܬܠ ܆ ܠܡܼܐܚܐ ܐܦ ܠܡ̇ܐܚܝܘ ܀
and that is the true prayer which opens the door of the treasures of God to give to the solicitors what satisfies their needs. You have received freely, give freely you too to all those you please. Of those who have received the power to dispose of a fortune (for themselves) and to dispense it to whom they want as they would their own property, how would one dare to say that they knock at the door, like beggars asking for alms to provide for their need? Certainly not! On the contrary, they distribute life, resurrect the dead, bring hope, give light to the blind! You are, it is said, the light of the world. And: I will give you the keys. You are therefore no longer a solicitor, for you possess, as if it depended on you, the power to bind and to loose in this age and in the ages of ages. How would he who is in this condition prostrate himself at the door to beg like a vagabond, when he has in his hands the keys of the treasure to take and to give, to live and to vivify?
ܐܠܐ ܐܿܡܪ ܐܢܬ ܕܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܣܠܩ ܫܡܥܘܢ ܠܐܓܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܨ̈ܠܐ. ܘܦܘܠܘܣ ܗܽܘ ܪܒܐ ܆ ܐܿܡܪ ܕܐܡܝܢ ܡܨܲ̈ܠܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܰܝ ܆ ܐܟܡܐ ܕܠܢ ܡܬܝ̈ܕܥܢ ܆ ܥܡܢ ܡܬܚܿܫܚܝܢ. ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܢ ܦܩܚ̈ܢ. ܘܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܪ̈ܡܬܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܠܢ ܝ̇ܗܒܝܢ. ܠܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܠܓܡܪ. ܐܘ̇ ܠܐ ܫܸܡܥܬ ܕܟܕ ܣܼܠܩ ܠܐܓܪ̈ܐ ܢܦܠ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܬܼܡܗܐ. ܘܐܝܟܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܬܡܝܗ ܘܬܗܝܪ ܆ ܡܨܿܠܝܘ ܡܨܲܠܐ. ܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܚܠܦܝܢ ܐܡܪ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܡܨܿܠܝܐ ܡܕܝܢ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܐ ܗܝ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ܆ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܐܼܡܪ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܢܼܚ ܒܠܒܘ̈ܬܢ ܘܪܘܚܗ ܒ̇ܨܐ ܥܘܡܩ̈ܘܗܝ ܦܪ ܘܓܿܠܐ ܠܢ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ. ܘܨܒ̣ܝܢ ܠܡܥܒܕ ܗܕܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܐܦܼܣܬ̇ ܠܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܗ ܕܝܫܘܥ. ܕܡܕܥܐ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܢ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܐܒܐ ܀ ܥܠܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܡܟܝܠ ܠܐܬܪܐ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ ܕܩܼܢܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܒܥܠܡܐ ܕܚܙܘ̈ܢܐ. ܚ̇ܝܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܪܘܚܐ ܒܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ. ܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܠܳܐܝܢ ܒܨܠܘܬܐ. ܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܒ̇ܟܝܢ ܥܠ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ. ܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܩܳܪܺܝܢ ܗ ܡܢ ܪܘܚܩܐ ܚܳܘܐ ܠܢ ܫܘܦܪܟ. ܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܫ̈ܐܠܝܢ ܚ̇ܕܘܪܪܐܝܬ ܦܠܓ ܠܢ ܥܘܬܪܟ. ܝܿܗܒܝܢ
4 But you will say to me: Why then did Simon go up to pray on the terrace and why does the great Paul say: Do not cease to pray? These (Apostles), my Brothers, use for us (to speak of) things which, in the measure that they are known to us, are those which suit us, and by them they communicate to us an analogy of those which are transcendent: for our use and not at all for theirs. Or would you not have heard that when (Simon) went up to the terrace, stupor fell upon him? And how would he who is stunned and amazed pray properly speaking? It is the Spirit Himself who prays for us, says Saint Paul; it is therefore (here) a question of the operation of the Spirit and not of that of the movements of prayer, as he (also) said: God has appeared in our hearts and His Spirit searches His depths and reveals to us His mysteries. And, would we want to make (of ourselves) that (prayer), the Spirit of Jesus would not permit it to us, for we have the mind of Christ to see the mysteries of the house of the Father. 5 They have therefore entered into the place of admiration; they hold the power in the world of visions; the Spirit has united them to the admirable beauty; they no longer toil in prayer; they no longer weep at the door. They no longer cry from afar: "Show us Thy beauty!"; they do not ask


[p. 340] {#po.39.p340}
ܕܢܸܣܒܘ. ܡܦ̈ܠܓܝܢ ܕܥܼܬܪ̈ܘ. ܡܢܝܼܚܝܢ ܕܐܬܬܢ̣ܝܚܘ ܒܠܡܝܢܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܀ ܚ̇ܕܝܢ ܘܡܥܢܿܕܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܘܝܘ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܕܫܦܝܪܐ. ܢܗܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܠܡ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܟܪ̈ܣܗ ܕܐܝܢܐ ܕܡܗ̇ܝܡܢ ܒܝ ܘܠܡܢܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܪ̈ܢ. ܐܠܦܝܢܝ. ܡܿܚܝܢ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܡܿܫܩܝܢ ܠܨܗܝ̈ܐ ܀
anymore in the manner of beggars: "Distribute to us Thy wealth!"; they give because they have received; they distribute because they have become rich; they relax because they have been relaxed in the port of Life; they rejoice and they make rejoice because they have been intoxicated by the love of the Beautiful. Truly, rivers of living water flow from the breast of him who believes in me. But why do they flow, Lord? Teach it to me! - They vivify others
ܘܐܝܟܢ ܗܽܘ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܚܕ ܥܡ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܡܨܿܠܝܘ ܡܨܲܠܐ. ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܡܦ̈ܣ ܒܗ ܒܡܖܿܐ ܒܝܬܐ. ܘܠܡܿܢ ܡܨ̈ܠܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܘ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܐܒܘܟܘܢ ܕܒܫܡܝܐ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܡܢܐ ܡܬܒܼܥܐ ܠܟܘܢ ܥܕܠܐ ܬܫܐܠܘܢܝܗܝ ܘܠܡܢܐ ܐܸܡܪ ܕܨܲܠܘ ܘܠܐ ܬܐܼܡܢ ܠܟܘܢ« ܟܡܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܒܕܐ ܡܨܿܠܝܘ ܡܨ̈ܠܐ. ܡܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܐܬܝܼܠܕ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܥܠܡܗ̇ ܕܨܠܘܬܐ ܀ ܒܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܇ ܘܐܫ̇ܬܠܛ ܥܠ ܥܘܬܪ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܝܿܪ̈ܬܐ. ܘܠܘ ܡܒܥܐ ܒ̇ܿܥܐ ܀
and water the thirsty. 6 How would he who has become one with Christ pray properly speaking, like someone who does not know the Master of the house? And whom would he pray to, since he is a son of God? Indeed, your Father who is in heaven knows what is necessary for you before you ask Him. But then, why did (Saint Paul) say: Pray without ceasing? It is that, as long as he is a servant, he prays properly speaking; but when, in the world of prayer, he is born of the Spirit, then he is a son of God; the power over wealth has been given to him as to an heir, and it is no longer a question of asking.
ܡܕܝܢ ܪܒܬܐ ܗܝ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܥܡܠ̈ܝܢ. ܘܦܿܬܚܬ̇ܿ ܬܪܥܐ ܠܐܬܪܐ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ. ܡܥܝܼܚܬ̇ ܠܐܘܬܐ ܘܡܫܿܬܩܬ̇ ܟܠ ܙܘܥ̈ܝܢ. ܘܟܒܪ ܬܐܼܡܪ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ ܗܝ ܡܠܬܟ ܇ ܘܠܐ ܝܿ̇ܕܥ ܐܢܐ ܚ̇ܝܠܗ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ ܐܺܝܬܐ ܠܣܗܕܘܬܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܕܫܿܘܐ ܠܡܬܗܲܝܡܢܘ ܆ ܕܐܿܡܪ ܗܘܐ. ܕܐܡܬܝ ܕܨ̈ܒܝܐ ܒܝ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܝ ܘܢܬܦܐ ܠܗܘܢܝ ܠܬܗܖܪܐ ܕܒܚܙܬܗ ܆ ܡܟ̇ܬܪ ܝܘܡܐ ܟܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܇ ܒܐܬܪܐ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ. ܘܡܐ ܕܢ̇ܦܩ ܡܢ ܬܡܢ. ܡܨ̈ܠܐ ܘܡܬܟ̇ܫܦ ܆ ܕܢܕܢܚ ܠܗ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܟܼܣܐ ܠܓܘ ܡܢܗ ܇ ܒܥܠܡܐ ܡ̣ܠܐ ܬܗܪܐ. ܡܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܠܗܠ ܆ ܠܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܗܘ ܕܡ̈ܠܐ ܇ ܕܬܫܼܟܚ ܬܪܕܐ ܒܗ ܕܕܲܓܠܬܐ ܩܢܝܝܬܐ ܒܫܒ̈ܝܠܐ ܕܝܘ̈ܬܢܝܐ. ܗܖ̈ܟܐ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܣܝܼܡ ܫܬܩܐ. ܠܡܕܥܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܿܦܣ ܠܗ ܠܡܥܒܪ ܘܠܡܚܙܐ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܢ̇ܘܚܐ ܕܟܠ ܪ̈ܐܙܝܢ. ܠܗܢܐ ܫܰܠܝܛ ܠܗ ܠܡܥܠ ܘܠܡܬܗܖ̈ ܒܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܘܡܪܐ. ܕܠܗܠ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܘܟܼܣܐ ܒܓܘ ܟܠܐ ܀
7 It is therefore true that prayer is greater than all works: it opens the door (which gives access) to the place of admiration, it relaxes from fatigue and imposes silence on all movements. But perhaps you are going to say to me: "You speak (to me) of admiration, while I do not know the strength of (this) admiration...". I am going to give you as testimony the words of a trustworthy Brother who said: "When the grace of my God is pleased with me and draws my spirit toward the wonder that His vision provokes, it remains an entire day without movements in the place of admiration. And when it comes out of it, it prays and begs that the light of the hidden Being may appear to it, itself hidden within it, in the world full of wonder." From there, it is no longer the place of words in which the current of the pen could advance by ways of ink: here a limit has been set, and it is silence. To the intellect alone it is permitted to cross it and to see this place of rest (which is that) of all mysteries. For it, it is licit to enter there and to marvel at the admirable beauty which is beyond all and hidden inside all.
ܡܕܝܢ ܟܠ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܒܬܗܪܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܚܠܦܐ ܒܙܒܢ ܙܒܢ ܆ ܠܐ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܡܿܢܥܬ̇ ܠܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܼܡܪ̈ܢܢ ܡܢ ܠܥܠ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܡܟܬܪܐ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܕܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܒܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܆ ܐܢ ܬܸܡܗܐ ܕܒܚܕܘܬܐ ܀ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܟܠ ܟܠܗ ܠܐ ܛܥܼܡܬ̇. ܨܠܘܬܐ ܐܡܝܢܢܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܬܼܡܗܐ ܀ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ. ܗ̇ܢܘ ܘܣܟܗ̇ ܕܡܠܬܢ ܡܟܝܠ ܠܘܬ ܀ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܿܡܪ ܐܢܬ
8 Any prayer, therefore, which does not transform itself at certain moments into wonder at the mysteries has not yet reached perfection, as we have said above. And the prayer of movements itself will not last continuously if it tastes nothing of the stupor that the joy of God causes. Continuous prayer is stupor before God. This


[p. 342] {#po.39.p342}
ܠܐ ܬܐܼܡܪ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܡܦܿܣ ܐܢܬ ܘ ܠܐ ܬܡܿܠܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܢ̇ܣܝܬ. ܐܦ ܐܿܢܐ ܡ̇ܪܟܢ ܐܢܐ ܪ̈ܝܫܝ ܡܢ ܒܗܬܬܝ ܘܫܿ̇ܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܆ ܘܒܪ̈ܚܼܡܐ ܡܬܓܿܘܣ ܐܢܐ. ܥܕܪ̈ܝܢܝ ܒܨܠܘܬܐ ܐܚܝ ܀ ܝܓ ܕܬܠܬܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܐܓܪܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܫܠܝܐ ܘܥܪ̈ܘܩܝܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܢܝ̈ܢܐ ܀
summarizes what we have said. Following which you will retort: "Do not say things that you do not know, do not speak of what you have not experienced!". In fact, I bow my head in shame, I am silent and take refuge in mercy. Help me through prayer. 13 THIRTEENTH LETTER [On solitude and the flight from social gatherings]
ܫܡܥܝܢܝ ܐܚܝ ܘܐܿܡܪ ܠܟ ܫܖܵܪܵܐ ܕܝܿܠܦܬ ܡܢ ܐܠܗܝ ܟܕ ܥܡ ܥܠܡ̈ܝܐ ܀ ܥܼܢܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܚܘܼܒܐ ܆ ܥܠܡܝܐ ܚܫܝܼܒ ܐܢܬ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܘܟܕ ܚܠܝܼܛ ܐܢܬ ܥܡ ܝܚܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܪ̈ܚܡܺܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܆ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܐܢܬ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ. ܘܟܕ ܒܦܣܩܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܐܠܗܐ ܗ̇ܙܲ̈ܓ ܐܢܬ ܒܐܡܝܢܘ ܆ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܐܢܬ ܘܕܡܐ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܣܟܐ ܕܟܠ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܢ ܀
1 Listen to me, my Brother, tell you a truth that I have learned from my God: when you associate with love with seculars, you are considered by God as a secular; when you join in charity with the monks who are friends of God, you are a true monk; and when in separation from all you meditate continuously on God, you are divine and resembling God. That is the end (of the efforts) of all (our) races.
ܩ̇ܕܫ ܬܫܘܝܬܟ ܒܪ̈ܘܚܦܗ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܥܠܝܟ. ܢ̣ܦܘܚ ܪ̈ܝ̣ܼܚܐ ܕܗܕܡܝܟ ܒܕܡܼܘܬ ܗܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܡܢ ܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܥܠܝܗ̇ܿ ܐܓܣ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܒܡܓܢܢܘܬܗ ܕܩܕܝܫ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܗܢܘ ܐܚܝ ܢܿܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܢܿܛܪ ܠܪ̈ܚܿܡܘܗܝ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܐܦ ܒܫܼܢܬܗ ܝ̇ܿܕܥ ܠܚܝ̈ܠܐ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܫ ܕܠܩܘܒܠ̈ܝܐ. ܘܝ ܠܗ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܐܣ̈ܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܗ ܙܗܘܡ̈ܢ ܆ ܕܥܿܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܠܘ ܒܝܬܝܐ. ܘܝ ܠܗ ܠܕܝܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܩܥܐ ܕܥܠ ܓܘܫܡܗ ܠܐ ܒܣܝܡ̈ܢ ܆ ܕܠܒܘܫܐ ܕܠܒܝܼܫ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܗܘ. ܛܘܒܝܟ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܆ ܡܝܥ̇ܫܩ ܐܣ̈ܐ ܕܩܠܝܬܗ ܆ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܝܚܐ ܗܢܝܐܐ ܕܡܢܗܝܢ ܦܐܚ. ܢܦܝܼܠ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܣܐܩ. ܒܪ̈ܝܟ ܥܠ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܘܗܝ ܘܢ̇ܬܦ. ܩܶܐܡ ܥܠ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܘܝ̇ܩܕ ܒܫܘܠܗܒܐ.
2 Sanctify your bed by the flight of the Spirit above you: the odor of your limbs will exhale like (that) of the aromatics of the place on which you rest by the descent of the Most Holy than all. It is He, my Brother, the Guardian of (your) purity, He who guards those who love Him. Happy the monk who, even in his sleep, knows the holy Powers and discerns those which are adverse! (But) woe to the monk whose cell walls are foul-smelling, for he dwells there without being at home! Woe to the religious if the pieces of cloth he has on his body do not exhale perfume, for the garment he wears is (a) foreign (garment). Happy are you, O monk who kisses the walls of your cell because of the sweet odor that emanates from them; who, fallen on your face, breathes it, and, kneeling, draws it (into your chest); who, standing, burns in the flames; who, embracing and kissing the cross, shines with the beauty of the


[p. 344] {#po.39.p344}
ܡܥܿܦܩ ܘܡܝ̇ܫܩ ܠܨܠܝܒܐ ܆ ܘܡܙܕܗܐ ܒܫܘ̣ܦܪܗ ܕܥܠܝܐ. ܕܠܒܗ ܕܝܿܨܐ ܘܩ̇ܥܐ ܒܚܕܘܬܐ ܀
Most High! (O monk) whose heart leaps joyfully and who cries out with gladness!
ܢܸܚܕܐ ܠܡ ܙ ܠܒܗܘܢ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒ̇ܥܝܢ ܠܡܪܝܐ. ܠܹܐܝܘ ܕܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܥܘܦܩܗ ܕܪ̈ܚܝܼܡܐ. ܝܼܩ̈ܕܝ ܒܒ̈ܬܐ ܒܕܡܥ̈ܐ ܫܓܝܪ̈ܬ̇ܐ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ. ܫܼܠܺܝ ܐܕܢ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܨܘܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܩܠܺܝܢ ܒܬܡܗܐ ܕܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܕܫ̇ܦܝܪ̈ܐ. ܡܟܝܠ ܦܓܪܐ ܫܓܝܪ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܕܡܿܝܬ̇ ܠܐܠܗܐ܆ ܘܡܢܘ ܡܼܨܐ ܕܢܡܠܠ. ܛܘܒܝܟܐܘ̄ ܐ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܆ ܕܛܘܒܟ ܚܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܠܘ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ. ܘܡܼܢܘ ܕ ܢܫܬܿܥܝܘܗܝ ܆ ܠܐ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܀
3 Let the heart of those who seek the Lord therefore rejoice! The arms have tired of embracing the Beloved, the pupils have been burned by ardent tears of love, the ears have ceased to hear any voice in the stupor caused by the beauty of the Beautiful. Henceforth, the body is set ablaze by the fire and the Spirit, and the soul is like God: who could speak of that? Blessed are you, O monk, for your beatitude is one, and not multiple. And this one, who will tell it? That is an impossible thing.
ܝܕ ܕܐܪ̈ܒܥܬܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܠܕܐܡܝܢ ܥ̇ܡܕ ܥܡܿܕܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܢܝܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܟܼܣܐ ܀
14 FOURTEENTH LETTER 1 To him who is constantly immersed with a mysterious immersion outside of all, in Him who is hidden inside all.
ܡܬܓܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܕܠܝܠܐ ܒܝܕ ܟܫܝܪ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܡ̈ܨܕܢܐ ܕܠܥܘ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܆ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܕܗܢܘܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܥܶܒܕܝܢ. ܡܚܙܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܒܗ̇ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܚܿܙܝܢܐ. ܒܕ ܡܢܗ ܡܬܢܓܕܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܨܡܚ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܡ̈ܠܠܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܬܡܝܗܘܬ ܫܘܦܪܗ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܗܘܢ ܡܬܬ̇ܘܫܛܝܢ ܕܐܟ ܣܗܕܘܬܗ ܕܡܠܝܬܐ ܐܠܝܗܐ. ܛܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܠܕܟ̈ܝܐ ܕܒܠܒܗܘܢ ܢܚܼܙܘܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ ّ: ܀
2 He reveals Himself indeed, as a result of their diligence, to the small number of those who fix their eyes inside themselves: those who make of themselves a mirror in which the Invisible is seen. For it is He who attracts them through the intermediary of the ineffable resplendences which, coming from His stunning beauty, are communicated to them in themselves, as the Word of God testifies: Blessed are the pure, for they will see God in their heart.
ܕ ܐܘ̈̄ ܕܐܫܬܘܕܝ ܠܢ ܛܘܒܐ ܕܪܡ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܛܘܒ̈ܝܢ. ܐܫ̱ܼܘܐ ܠܢ ܠܛܘܒܐ ܕܡܠܼܟܬ݀ ܠܢ. ܗܒ ܠܢ ܣܒܪܐ ܕܣܿܒܪܬ ܠܢ ܕܠܢܦܫܢ ܡܫ̈ܢܩ ܀ ܒܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܒܥܬܗ ܘܡܛܠ ܕܠܐ ܕܟ̈ܝܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܫ̇ܘܝܢܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܕܘ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܣܛܪܟ ܆ ܢܚܽܘܪܘܢ ܣܘܓܐܐ ܕܟܘܬܡ̈ܬܢ. ܦܕܡܟ ܕܐܫܬܦܥ ܠܚܘܣܝܢ ܆ ܢܚܿܠܠܢ ܘܢܥܼܒܕܢ ܕܡ̈ܝܝܐ ܕܕܟܝܘܬܟ. ܕܒ݂ܣܒܬ ܠܟ ܡܪܝ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܢ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܠܟ ܆ ܗܼܒ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܝܟ ܕܠܐ ܩܢܝܼܢܢ. ܘܕܐܦܼܩܢ̱ܟ ܠܕܝܠܢ ܒܖ̈ܒܘܬ ܐ ܥܽܘܠܢ ܆ ܐܥܠܲܝܢ ܠܕܝܠܟ ܒܪ̈ܚܼܡܝܟ. ܘܒܥܠܡ
3 O You who have promised us a beatitude higher than all beatitudes, make us worthy of your promise. Give us the hope of which You have brought us the good news and which torments with desire our souls that seek it. And because we are neither pure nor worthy, may the water that flowed for us from your side whiten the multitude of our stains; may your blood which was shed for our forgiveness wash us and make us images of your purity. Because, from what is ours, You have assumed, Lord, what was not yours, give us, from what is yours, what we do not possess; and because we have made You go out


[p. 346] {#po.39.p346}
ܠ ܐܡܝܢ ܢܘܗܪܟ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܐ ܐܫܼܪܐ ܠܢ. ܫܘܒܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܒܟܠ ܢܝ
to what is ours by the enormity of our crimes, make us enter into what is yours by your mercy, and in the world of light, in your glory, make us dwell. To You be glory, from all, in all the ages of ages. Amen.
ܝܗ
15 FIFTEENTH LETTER
ܕܚܡܫܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܫܐܠ ܐܢܐ ܫܠܡܗ̇ ܕܢܟܦܘܬܟ. ܒܚܘܼܒܗ ܕܥܠܝܐ ܀ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܐܡܬܝ ܥܒܼܕܐ ܬܫܬܡܗ. ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܚܐܪ̈ܐ ܕ ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܡܪܐ ܥܠ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܛܡ̈ܐܐ ܕܚܕܪ̈ܝܟ. ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܚܼܪܘܒ ܘܬ̇ܘܒܕ ܠܥܘܪ̈ܠܐ ܕܒܡܕܝܢܬܟ. ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܓ݂ܙܘܪ ܓܙܘܖܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܢ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܥܳܡܘܪ̈ܐ ܥܿܡܪܺܝܢ ܒܝܬܟ ܒܡܬܓܙܪ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܕܒܪܪܘܚܐ. ܐܡܬܝ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܫܿܠܝܛܐ ܘܡܠܟܐ ܒܡܕܝܢܬ̇ ܥܠܡ ܆ ܘܢܦܘܫ̈ܢ ܡܢܟ ܠܟܐ ܚ̈ܡܫ ܘܥܣܖ̈ ܕܐܫܿܬܡ̈ܺܗܝ. ܐܡܬܝ ܢ̣ܣܓܕܘܢ ܠܟ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܡܠܟܐ ܆ ܘܫܒܚܘܢܟ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܠܫܢܐ ܇ ܕܗܼܘܝܬ ܟܘܪ̈ܣܝܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܠܡܠܟܐ ܡܠܟ ܥܠܡ. ܐܡܬܝ ܬܚܼܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܟ ܫܡ̈ܝܐ ܚܺܕܬܐ ܆ ܟܕ ܩ̇ܥܝܢ ܒܟ ܩܘ̇ܕܫ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܒܛܘܟ̇ܣ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܚܼܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܟ ܡܚܙܝܬ ܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܕ݁ܗܿܘ ܚܿܙܐ ܟܠ ܆ ܘܬܚܼܙܐ ܒܗ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܗ ܇ ܘܬܒܚܼܘܪ ܒܗ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܝܠܟ. ܐܡܬܝ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܠܒܟ ܩܼܐܒܘܬܗ ܕܡܪܿܝܐ ܡܠܟܐ ܕܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܆ ܘܢܫܬܡ̈ܥܢ ܠܟ ܡܢܗ ܒܢ̈ܬ ܩܰܠܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܒܘܕܩ ܖܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܇ ܘܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܒܝܬܟ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܢܒܝܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܩܥ̈ܝܝ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܗ. ܘܡܢ ܩܠ ܩܥܬܗܘܢ ܢܣܬ̇ܚܦܘܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܢܣܘܼܦܘܢ ܣܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܠܩܘܒܠܝܐ :ܐ ܀
1 I greet your Chastity in the love of the Most High. How long will you be called a servant? When will you be a free man? When will you be a lord who dominates the impure peoples that surround him? When will you destroy and annihilate the Gentiles who are in your city? When will you circumcise with the circumcision not made by human hands all the inhabitants of your house, with that circumcision which is done in spirit? When will you hold power and be king in the eternal city, and when will they be subject to you right here, the five and the ten cities of which mention was made? When will all the kings prostrate themselves before you, and when will all tongues praise you because you will have become the holy throne of the King, (the throne) of the eternal King? When will you see in yourself the new heavens proclaiming in you the "Sanctus" of the hidden Essence, in the order of their ranks? And when will you see in yourself the mirror of the light of Him who sees all, so that you may see in it what is His and that you may examine by it all that is yours? When will your heart be the ark of the Lord God of Israel, and when, coming from Him, will the words of God and the elucidation of His mysteries be heard by you, so that all the sons of your house become His prophets proclaiming His praise, and that at the sound of their clamor the foreign ramparts be overthrown and the troops of the adversaries annihilated?
ܐܡܬܝ ܢܬܚܳܦܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܗܘܢܟ ܒܥܪ̈ܦܠܐ ܕܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܘܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܙܕܗ̈ܝܢ ܒܨܡ̈ܚܐ ܫܒܝ̈ܚܐ ܕܕܢܿܚܝܢ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܓܘܗ̇ ܆ ܘܢܼܐܠܦ ܣܼܦܪܵܐ ܚܼܕܬܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܼܕܬܐ ܇ ܘܢܦ̇ܫܩ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܠܒܢ̈ܝ ܝܣܪܐܝܠ ܕܐܬܚܿ̇ܕܬܘ. ܐܡܬܝ ܢܼܖ̈ܕܐ ܡܿܒܘܥ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܟܪܣܟ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܬܼܫܐܠ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܨܗܝܐܝܬ. ܐܡܬܝ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܠܩ̈ܠܐ ܢܘܟܪܝܐ ܘܬܗܼܘܐ
2 When will the Moses (that is) your spirit be covered by the cloud of the glory of God and his face shine with the glorious flashes that shine upon him from inside that (cloud), so that he may learn the new Book of the new World and explain its mysteries to the sons of Israel who will have been renewed? When will the source of Life flow from your breast and you will no longer ask for water like a thirsty man? When will you be a stranger to voices and be a


[p. 348] {#po.39.p348}
ܡܠܬܐ ܚܳܝܬܐ ܡܥ̇ܝܕܬ̇ ܒܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܥܠܡ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܆ ܘܫܬܝܩܘܬܟ ܫܠܝܐܝܬ ܢܡܿܠܠ ܫܪܪܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܚܼܕܬܐ. ܐܡܬܝ ܢܒܠܗܝܘܗܝ ܐܘ ܪܘܚ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܠܫܢܟ ܦܐܩܐ ܡܡܶܠܠ ܕܕܥܒܝ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܘܢܽܘܥܐ ܒܟ ܠܫܢܐ ܚܼܕܬܐ ܇ ܘܢܡܠܠ ܒܗ ܚ̈ܕܬܬܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܇ ܘܠܟܠ ܣܦܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܠܦ̈ܢܐ ܬܥܼܒܕ ܝܠ̈ܘܦܐ. ܥܕܡܐ ܠܐܡܬܝ ܐܝܟ ܥܿܒܪ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܫܶܪܶܐ ܠܟ ܡܪܐ ܒܝܬܐ. ܐܡܬܝ ܟܝ ܬܗܼܘܐ ܓܝܙܒܪܗ ܆ ܘܒܐ̈ܝ̈ܕܝܟ ܠܟܢܘ̈ܬܟ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܢܬܦܠܓܘܢ ܀
living word united to Life in the world of light, so that your silence may speak without noise the truth of the new World? When will the Spirit of Life astonish your tongue, (this) stammering being who says coarse things, and will He cause a new tongue to grow in you, so that He may say through it the new things of the spiritual world and that you may make disciples of all the scribes and all the doctors? How long will the Master of the house turn you away from the door like a vagabond? When will you be his steward, and when will life be distributed by your hands to your companions?
ܡܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܫܺܐܠ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܿܡܪ ܠܟ ܕܝܠܟ ܕܥܕܟܝܠ ܐܳܢܐ ܒܕܝܠܝ ܠܐ ܡܦܿܣ ܐܢܐ. ܐܠܐ ܫܸܡܥܝܢܝ ܘܐܡܪ ܠܟ ܬܚܘܝܼܬܐ ܕܠܥܝܡܐ :ܠܝ ܘܠܟ ܆ ܘܐܦ ܙܕܩ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܪ̈ܝ ܥܠܡܐ. ܗܘܢܢ ܐܬܒܼܢܝ ܒܝܬ ܡܥܡܪܐ ܠܒܶܢܝܗ܆ ܐܢ ܚܢܢ ܒܚ̈ܫܐ ܠܐ ܢܛܘܫܝܗܘܝ ܐܘ ܕܢܓܥܨ ܡܢܗ. ܡܬܛܲܡܐܐ ܓܝܪ ܒܟܠܗܘܢ ܨܘܠܡܝ ܥܠܡܐ ܆ ܦܬܟܪ̈ܐ ܡܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܗ ܡܬܛܿܒܥܝܢ. ܐܝܬܐ ܠܟ ܠܕܘܼܡܝܗ ܒܝܬܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܘܐܣ̇ܬܟܠ. ܒܝܕܓܝܪ ܚ̈ܫܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܆ ܫܠ̈ܕܐ ܡܝ̈ܬܬܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܡܥܢ̈ܫܢ ܟܼܫܝܢ ܒܗ. ܒܪܒܘܬ ܟܪܣܐ ܆ ܡܸܠܝܢܝܗܝ ܣܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ ܡܢ̈ܦܨܬܿ ܟܠ ܡܐܟ̈ܠܢ. ܒܝܕ ܚܡܬܢ ܘܪ̈ܘܓܙܢ ܆ ܟ̇ܢܫܢܢ ܘܣܼܡܢܢ ܒܗ ܫܠ̈ܕܐ ܕܐܟ̈ܕܢܐ ܘܕܟܠ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܡܪܿܬܐ ܀ ܒܪܚܡܬ̇ ܟܣܦܐ ܆ ܣ̈ܢܒܢܝܗܝ ܒܫܠ̈ܕܐ ܕܒܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܕܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܕܦܪ̈ܚܬܐ ܘܕܟܠ ܕܒܗ ܡܙܕܒܢܝܢ. ܘܛܲܡܐܝܢܝܗܝ ܠܒܝܬ ܡܫܪܝܗ ܕܩ̇ܕܝܫ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܒܪܚܡܬ ܫܘ̣ܒܚܐ ܆ ܐܫܼܠܚܢܢ ܡܢܗ ܨܒܬܐ ܕܐܠܼܒܫܗ ܚ̇ܟܝܡܐ ܇ ܘܐܠܼܒܫܢܝܗܝ ܒܪ̈ܕܥܬܐ ܢܕܝܼܕܬ̇ ܠܡܪ̈ܢ. ܒܪܡܘܬܐ ܐܚܼܬܢܢ ܡܢܗ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܡܠܟܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܆ ܘܣܼܲܡܢܢ ܒܗ ܦܬܟܪ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܡܫܿܥܪܿܝܢ ܒܚܙܬܗ ܇ ܕܠܗ ܢܣܓ݂ܘܕ ܥܡܐ ܕܢܼܦܩ ܡܢ ܡܨܪ̈ܝܢ. ܘܒܗ ܥܿܒܕܝܢ ܥܺܐܕܐ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܆ ܘܡܿܫܪ̈ܩܝܢ ܘܡܪܩ̇ܕܝܢ ܬܡܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܕܝܘ̈ܐ. ܘܡܿܟܪ̇ܝܢ ܡܠ̈ܐܟܐ. ܘܪ̈ܓܝܼܙ ܡܪܗܘܢ ܒܗܢܐ ܓܘܢܚܐ ܥܫܝܢܐ ܀
3 Why do you ask me to tell you what is yours, when I do not yet know what is mine? Nevertheless, listen to me give you an example that suits you and me, as well as all the generations of the world. Our spirit was built as a dwelling intended for its Builder, on condition that we do not defile it with passions so that He takes an aversion to it. Now it is rendered impure by all the images of the world, (those) dead idols which are imprinted in it. I give you regarding it the comparison of the sensible house; understand it. Through the passions of lust we have piled up in it a heap of lifeless human corpses. Through gluttony we have filled it with a stench to make one vomit all food. Through our impetuosity and our anger we have gathered and stored in it corpses of vipers and of all (kinds) of poisonous reptiles. 4 Through the love of money we have defiled it with corpses of beasts of burden, of (various) animals, of birds, of everything that is bought with it, and we have polluted the dwelling of the Holiest of all. Through the love of praise we have stripped it of the finery with which the Wise One had clothed it and we have clothed it with a pack-saddle that disgusts the Lord. Through pride we have taken down from it the image of the eternal King and we have put in it a huge idol with a hideous appearance so that the people who came out of Egypt may worship it; the devils hold feasts there where all the sons of demons dance and hiss; they sadden the angels (there), and their Lord is irritated by this monstrous horror.
ܡܢܐ ܓܝܪ ܢܹܐܡܪ̈. ܢܗܘܐ ܕܚܕ ܡܢܢ ܢܚܼܙܐ ܠܒܝܬܗ ܕܡܼܠܐ ܘܛܡܝܸܼܡ ܒܗܕܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܣܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ ܘܢܦܝܼܣܝܘܗܝ ܪܚܶܡܗ ܕܚܿܒܝܒ ܠܗ ܛܒ ܆ ܕܢܬܼܠܝܘܗܝ ܠܗ ܕܢܥܼܘܠ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܒܗ ܥ̇ܡܘܪܐ ܆ ܟܝ ܫ̇ܡܥ
5 What to say to that? Suppose one of us sees his house filled and obstructed with all this rot and he prays to the friend who is particularly dear to him to do him the favor of entering it to become its inhabitant,


[p. 350] {#po.39.p350}
ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܆ ܠܐܓܝܪ̈. ܘܐܢ ܚܢܢ ܕܘܝ̈ܐ ܠܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܡܝܢ ܗܘܝܢ ܆ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܠܐ ܢܒܼܗܬ݀ ܕܟܕ ܡܠܝܼܢܢ ܡܢ ܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܆ ܢܦܝܼܣܝܘܗܝ ܠܛܒܐ ܕܢܸܥܒܕܢ ܕܝܢ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܠܕܟܝܘܬܗ. ܡܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܢܫܼܠܐ ܡܢ ܪܗܛ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢܕܟ̈ܝܘܗܝ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܬܼ ܩܢܗ. ܘܡܐ ܕܚܼܙܝܗܝ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܐܫܬܿܠܚ ܘܥܪܛܠ ܩ̇ܐܡ ܆ ܠܒܫܗ ܘܡܢ̇ܦܐ ܠܗ ܘܠܐ ܫܿܒܩ ܠܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܠܘܥܕܗ ܢܬܩ̇ܪ̈ܒܘܢ. ܒܝܬܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܺܝܬܝܢ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܐܬܒܢܝܼܢܢ ܠܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܛܲܒܵܐ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܒܝ̈ܫܐ ܀ ܐܢ ܐܿܟܬܘܒ ܠܟ ܪ̈ܡܬܐ ܥ̇ܕܠ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ. ܘܡܨܥ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܫܿܦܪ̈ܢ ܠܟ. ܘܡܐܟܬ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܒ݁ܰܣܡ̈ܢ ܠܟ. ܡܢܐ ܐܿܥܒܕ ܠܟ ܘܠܐ ܝܿ̇ܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܡܥܝܠ ܐܬܠ ܠܟ ܠܩܛܐ ܗܢܝܐܐ ܘܚܠܝܐ. ܢܥܿܠܐ ܡܠܬܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܥܠܡ ܙܒܢܐ ܆ ܠܘܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܒ̇ܪ̈ܐ ܥܠ̈ܡܐ. ܐܘ̄ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܐܡܬܝ ܒܐܘܟܡ̈ܬܐ ܢܦܫܟ ܡܒ̇ܝܐܐ ܐܢܬ. ܗܘܝ ܠܟ ܟܠܟ ܫܠܗܒܝܬܐ ܆ ܘܐ̇ܘܩܕ ܠܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܚܕܪ̈ܝܟ. ܕܬܼܚܙܐ ܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܟܼܣܐ ܠܓܘ ܡܢܟ. ܩܼܪܝ ܒܩܠܐ ܫܠܝܐ ܘܫܬܝܩܐ ܟܕ ܐܿܡܪܿ ܐܢܬ. ܐܘ̄ ܕܟܼܣܐ ܒܝ ܘܡܣܿܬܪ ܆ ܓ݂ܠܝ ܒܝ ܐܠܟ ܕܡܛܫܝ. ܚ̇ܘܐ ܠܝ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܟ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢܝ. ܐܘ ܕܒܼܢܢܝ ܗܝܟܠܐ ܠܥܡܘܪ̈ܝܟ ܆ ܐܠܓܢ ܥܪ̈ܦܠܐ ܕܫܘܼܒܚܟ ܒܓܘ ܗܝܟܠܟ ܕܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܢܩܼܥܘܢ ܒܗ ܡܫ̈ܡܫܢܝ ܩܘܼܕܫܟ ܩܘ̇ܕܫܐ ܪܬܚ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܒܙܘܥܐ ܚ̇ܪ̈ܝܦܐ ܡܙܝܓ ܒܬܗܪܐ ܘܕܘܡܪܐ ܕܒܟܢܝܼܟܘܬܐ ܘܪܘܡܪ̈ܡܐ ܡܬܬܙܝܼܥ ܒܙܘܥܐ ܚ̇ܝܐ ܠܚܼܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܟ ܀ ܗܘܝ ܐܚܝ ܢܘܢܐ ܣܚܿ̇ܝܐ ܛܵܐܣ ܒܓܠ̈ܠܐ ܕܫܦܥ̈ܝ ܪܒܘܬܐ. ܘܣܿܬܪ ܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܒܚ̈ܙܘܗܝ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܐ ܡܢ ܨܝ̈ܕܐ ܠܺܝܛܐ ܕܥܠ ܣܦܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܩ̇ܝܡܝܢ ܆ ܟܕ ܕܚܝܼܠܝܢ ܘܡܣܿܟܝܢ ܠܨܝܕܐ ܕܢܦܘܼܩ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܫܟܼܚܝܢ ܠܡܨܕ ܡܢܗ ܆ ܕܚܿܠܝܢ ܕܢܥܼܠܘܢ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܡܘܬܘܢ ܀ ܗܼܒ ܠܗ̇ ܡܠܘܐܐ ܠܢܘܪܗ ܕܝܫܘܥ ܆ ܕܬܚܼܘܕ ܒܗ̇ ܕܟܝܘܬ ܢܦܫܟ. ܠܐܓܝܪ̈ ܕܥܿܟܐ ܡܢ ܐܪ̈ܥ ܢܦܫܐ ܕܚܒܼܬ̇ ܒܗ̇ ܆ ܐܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܠܐ ܢܥܠܘܢ ܠܗ̇. ܒܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܕܗܕܐ ܢܘܪܐ ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܕܟܝܘܬ ܢܦܫܟ ܆ ܘܠܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܗ ܕܪ̈ܚܝܼܡܐ ܒܗ̇ ܟܕ ܕܢܝܼܚ ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܠܟ
will the latter listen to him? Surely not! If, therefore, the miserable people that we are would not consent to that, how would we not be ashamed, while we are filled with such things, to pray to the All-Good to make of us a dwelling for his purity? Therefore, let us not interrupt the momentum of our race until we have rendered this (dwelling) as pure for Him as when He built it. And when He sees that it has been cleared of everything and stands bare, He will clothe it and cover it, and He will not let strangers approach it. Thus, therefore, we are his house, we who have been built with a view to good works and not bad ones. 6 If I write to you about high things, you reproach me for it; middle things do not please you; lower things are tasteless to you: I do not know what to do for you! So I am going to present to you a sweet and gentle bait: let us raise our words (from the domain) of the things of the temporal world to (that of) the things of the Creator of the worlds. O man of God, how long will you console yourself (in fact) with black? Be (rather) entirely a (bright) flame, and set fire to everything around you to see the beauty that is hidden inside you! (And) launch this appeal, with a silent voice and without noise of words: "O You who are hidden and veiled in me, reveal in me your secret mystery, show me your beauty which is inside me. O You who built me to be a temple where You dwell, make the cloud of your glory rest inside your temple so that, under the effect of love, the servants of your Holiness may proclaim there the "Sanctus" bubbling with fire and spirit, in an ardent momentum mixed with wonder and admiration which, in gravity and exaltation, is animated by a living movement by the power of your Essence". 7 Be, my Brother, a fish that swims and darts in the waves of the effusions of Greatness. In their holy shimmerings hide yourself from the cursed fishermen who stand on their banks, frightened and waiting for the prey to come out; they cannot in fact fish there, for they fear to enter and die. 8 Give food to the fire of Jesus so that the purity of your soul may be inflamed at its touch. Assuredly, it will not go out on the earth of the soul in which it has been lit, if no foreign water enters it.


[p. 352] {#po.39.p352}
ܠܒܪ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܟ :܆ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܒܗ̇ ܒܠܥܕ ܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ. ܣܛܪ ܀ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܇ ܠܐ ܠܩܢܘܡܟ ܚܳܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܐܦܠܐ ܠܗ ܒܩܢܘܡܟ. ܒܗܕܐ ܀ ܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܫܿ̇ܢܩ ܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܘܐܡܼܝܬ ܡܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܀܆ ܕܬܚܼܙܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܟ ܢܘܚܡܐ ܀
In the light of this fire you will see the purity of your soul and, manifest in it, the beauty of the face of the Beloved. Indeed, the latter will not appear to you outside your soul, and it will not appear in it either without holy purity: without it you will see neither your being, nor Himself in your being. Make the desire for this a torment for you, and make yourself die to life
ܐܡܪܓܝܪ ܐܢ̈ܐ ܀ ܐܢܼܐ ܢܘܚܡܐ ܘܚܝ̈ܐ. ܘܡܿܢ ܕܐܡܼܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܆ ܐܫܼܟܚ ܠܗ ܒܗ ܢܘܚܡܐ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܩܝܡܬܐ ܡܩ̇ܕܡܬܐ ܕܐܼܡܪ ܦܘܠܘܣܓܿܠܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܐ. ܠܐܓܝܖ̈ ܪ̈ܚܝܩ ܡܢܟ ܗܘ ܕܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܐܐ ܐܢܬ ܘܡܬܛܲܪ̈ܦ ܐܢܬ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܬܟ. ܒܟ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܟܕ ܕܡܝܟ ܆ ܘܡܣܿܟܐ ܠܟ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܬܬܥܝܼܪ ܆ ܘܬܥܝܼܪܝܘܗܝ ܆ ܕܢܝܫ̈ܠܐ ܡܢܟ ܓܠ̈ܠܐ ܚܢܘ̈ܩܐ ܀
so that you may see in yourself the resurrection. 9 He said in fact: I am the Resurrection and the Life, and he who, for love of Him, makes himself die to the world, finds in himself the resurrection: it is the anticipated resurrection of which Paul, the revealer of mysteries, spoke. For He is not far from you, He for whom you tire yourself and exhaust yourself throughout the days: He is in you, sleeping and waiting for the moment when you will awaken and when you will awaken Him so that He may calm (and ward off) from you the waves ready to submerge you.
