ܩܿܕܫ ܢܦܫܟ ܒܥܘܗܕܢܗ. ܪܗܐ ܩܢܘܡܟ ܕܐ ܒܚܽܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗ. ܚܝܕ ܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܥܡܗ ܒܫܘܠܗܒܐ ܕܪܚܡܬܗ. ܒܗܕܐ ܆ ܢܦܫܗ ܡܢ̇ܘܐ ܠܟ ܘܡܥܢ̈ܕܐ ܠܟ ܒܚܙܬܗ. ܗܕܐ ܆ ܠܗ ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ ܘܪܘܚܗ ܒܢܦܫܟ ܡܫܪ̈ܝܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܡܼܘܬ ܚܙܬܐ ܥܿܒܕܐ ܠܟ ܆ ܘܠܟܠ ܓܒܺ̈ܝܢ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ ܚܳܙܐ ܐܢܬ. ܒܗܕܐ ܬܗܘܡܐ ܕܪ̈ܟ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܘܫܡ̈ܝܐ ܥܠ̈ܝܐ ܒܢܘܗܪܗ ܒ̇ܚܖܪ ܐܢܬ. ܗܕܐ ܪ̈ܐܙܝ ܪܘܚܐ ܡܡܿܠܠܐ ܒܟ ܆ ܘܢܘܗܪܐ ܠܐ ܒܼܪ̈ܝܐ ܙܠܡܗ̇ܠܟܘ ܒܗ ܓܿܠܝܐ ܘܡܢ̇ܘܝܐ ܠܟ. ܒܗܕܐ ܟܠ ܕܒܟ ܡܨܡܚ ܨܰܡܚ ܆ ܘܟܠ ܕܩܕܡܝܟ ܙܕ ܡܢܗܪ̈ܢܗܪ. ܗܕܐ ܡܢ ܥܒܼܕܐ ܡܠܟܐ ܥ̇ܒܕܐ ܠܟ ܆ ܘܕܝ̈ܫܬܐ ܕܕ̈ܓܠܝܟ ܐܐ ܣܺܝܡܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܣܩܘܪ̈ܝܟ. ܗܕܐ ܒܟܠܝܠܐ ܕܫܘܡܠܝ ܪܗܛܐ ܠܗܘܢܟ ܡܟ̇ܠܠܐ ܆ ܘܬܐܘܪܝܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܒܙܘܥ̈ܘܗܝ
10 Sanctify your soul by the memory of Him. Make your being shine by looking at Him. Unite yourself to Him in the burning of his love; it is by this that He will show Himself to you and will rejoice you by his sight; it is this (love) that will make Him dwell in your soul with his Father and his Spirit; it is it that will make you (yourself) like a vision, and on all sides you will see it extend; by it you will tread the abyss, and by its light you will scrutinize the heavens from on High; it is it that will utter in you the mysteries of the Spirit, and that will reveal and show you the uncreated light so that you may walk in it; by it everything that is in you will burst with splendor, and everything that is before you will shine with a vivid clarity; it is it that, from a servant, will make you a king and will put all your enemies under your feet to be trampled there; it is it that will crown your spirit with the crown that is given at the end of the race and will make appear in its movements the contemplation of the Holy Trinity. With this Harbor of all the worlds,
ܥܠܡܐ ܐ ܕܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܐܡܝܢ ܀ ܝܘ ܕܫܬܬܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܐ ܫܡܥ ܐܚܝ ܡܕܡ ܕܐܼܡܪܿ ܩܕܡܝ ܐܢܫ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ. ܕܟܕ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܪ̈ܚ̇ܡܐ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ ܆ ܪ̈ܚܝܼܡܐ ܦ̣ܓܥ ܒܝ ܘܐܗܼܦܟܢܝ ܀, ܗܲܠܳܟ ܩܕܡܝ ܩܠܝܠ
let us now put an end to our words. To Him be glory, unanimously. Amen. 16 SIXTEENTH LETTER 1 Listen, my Brother, to what a trustworthy man reported before me. "As I was walking," he declared, "in the company of friends, the Beloved


[p. 354] {#po.39.p354}
ܘܐܢܿܐ ܡܢ ܒܣܬܪܗ ܕܒܝܩ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ. ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܦܼܢܝ ܠܝ ܆ ܘܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܐܫܼܬܒܝ ܕ ܘܟܕ ܀ ܚܼܙܢܝ ܀ ܕܐܬܒܿܠܗܝܬ ܆ ܡܿܠܠ ܥܡܝ ܒܓܘܪ̈ܓܐ ّ ܘܐܦܼܨܚܢܝ. ܘܝܼܩܕ ܠܗ ܝ ܠܒܝ ܒܪܚܡܬܗ. ܘܡܢ ܫܘܠܗܒܐ ܕܒܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܐܐ ܫܪܺܝ ܕܢ̣ܦܪܪܚ ܡܢ ܕܘܟܬܗ. ܘܢܦܫܝ ܡܟܝܠ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܛܘܠܩܐ ܩ̇ܝܡܐ ܗܘܬܿ. ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܫܘܢܝܐ. ܘܐܬܥܼܛܝܘ ܡܢ ܠܒܝ ܪ̈ܚܿܡܐ ܆ ܠܐ ܪ̈̈ܚܝܼܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܣܢ̈ܐܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡ ܘܟܕ ܐܬܡܿܥܝܠܬ ܆ ܫܒܼܩܢܝ ܕܐ̇ܬܡܗ ܒܗ ܘܒܕܝܠܗ ܙܒܢܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܀
came to meet me and took me away." He walked a little in front of me while I followed Him closely. (Then) He turned his face toward me, and my conscience was captivated. And when He saw that I remained spellbound, He addressed words of encouragement to me and rejoiced me. Then my heart was inflamed with his love and, under the effect of the burning flames of desire, began to fly away from the place that is its own. My soul from then on found itself as if annihilated, without however leaving this life; and the friends were erased from my heart, as little loved as enemies of always. When therefore I had (thus) become without strength, He left me for a certain time in this
ܘܡܥܝܠ ܒܠܐ ܕ :ܙ ܗܘܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܐ :ܙ ܒܠܐ ܪܕܲܓܫܬܐ ܩܶܐܡ ܗܿܘܝܬ. ܠܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܫܼܡܥܐ. ܬܡܼܗܐ ܘܫܼܠܝܐ ܪܖܪܒܐ. ܕܠܐ ܙܘܥܐ ܬܡܢ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ. ܕܒܝ̇ܕܘܥܐ ܛܥܼܬ̇ ܗܝ ܠܗ̇ ܡܢ ܕܠܡܕܥ ܘܐ. ܘܟܕ ܠܙܘܥܐ ܐܪ̈ܦܼܝܢܝ ܆ ܫܒܼܩܢܝ ܠܐ ܡܬܬܙܝܥܢܐ. ܘܛܼܫܐ ܟܕ ܥܗܝܕ ܐܢܐ ܠܥܘܕ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܆ ܘܡܢ ܕܝܠܝ ܘܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐ ܫܟܝܼܚ ܠܝ ܘܐ ܀ ܡܫܟܚ̈ܢ ܟܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܡܐܠܦ ܡܢ ܘܐ ܩܢܝܝܐ ܘܕܝܘܬܐ ܆ ܠܐ. ܡܢܘ ܕܛܥܼܡܟ ܚܠܝܐ ܘܒ̇ܣܝܡܐ ܢܣܬ̇ܟܠ. ܡܢܘ ܕܐܬܡ̇ ܓ ܒܟ ܆ ܠܐ ܥܿ̇ܕܠ ܠܡܓܙܪ̈ܓܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܲܚܡܗ ܒܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܡܩ̇ܠܣ ܠܡܿܥܗܕܢܗ ܙܐ ܥܠ ܕܝܠܗ. ܘܡܘ̇ܪܒ ܠܡܫܿܟܢܝܐ. ܘܪܘ̇ܙ ܘܡܝܘܣܦ ܥܠ ܟܘܫܪܗ ܀
(state), astonished by Him and by what is His. 2 And from then on I stood without spirit, as if non-existent and without perception: neither vision nor audition, (but) astonishment and profound silence; for there is neither movement nor knowledge there, from the fact that in the knower (knowledge) has forgotten itself, outside of knowing. And when He gave me back the freedom to move, He left me (still) immobile and hid Himself, while I remained without any other memory than that of these things and without finding anything of what is mine". 3 Is it possible to learn this from pen and ink? No! (But) he who has tasted You, O Sweet One!, O Delicious One!, he will understand. And he who has been (thus) mingled with You does not blame him who encourages his friend with such things; on the contrary, he praises him who makes mention of what is His, he magnifies the Giver, he rejoices and redoubles his zeal.

### SEVENTEENTH LETTER
ܕܫܒܥܣܖ̈ܐ ܐܓܪܬܐ ܥܠ ܫܘܠܡܐ ܕܡܢ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܐܘܠܨܢܐ
[On the deliverance that puts an end to combats and tribulations]
ܕܐܡܪ ܗܿܘ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܒܥܘܬܟܘܢ. ܫܡܥ ܡܪܝܐ ܚܫܟܘܢ ܕܚܠܦܝ ܆ ܘܛܪܕ ܡܢܝ ܠܓܝܘܢ ܛܡܐܬܐ. ܘܐܢܗܪ ܢܦܫܝ ܒܕܝܠܗ ܘܚܿܕܝܢܝ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܢܬܼܡܗܘܢ ܒܒܗܬܬܗܘܢ ܆ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐܡܪܘ »ܝܫ ܐܗܐ ܐܗܐ ܚܙܬ̇ ܒܗ ܥܝܢܢ ܀, ܡܟܝܠ ܘܢܬܥܿܫܢ ܠܒܝ ܒܡܪܝܐ ܘܢܩ̇ܦܚ ܠܣܢ̈ܐܝܢ ܆ ܕܐܪܝܡ
1 Here is what he who is the object of your supplication said: "The Lord has heard the pain that you feel because of me, and He has cast out of me the impure legion. He has enlightened my soul by what is His and has rejoiced me. May they be henceforth astonished under the blow of their shame, those who said: Ha! Ha! our eye has seen it! May henceforth (also) my heart be strengthened in the Lord, and (He) will confound those


[p. 356] {#po.39.p356}
ܠܡܘܟܟܢ. ܘܫ̇ܦܠ ܠܒܥܠ̈ܕܒܒܝܢ ܕܡܿܓܢ ܥܠܝܢ ܐܬܪܘܪܒܘ. ܒܪ̈ܝܟ ܗܘ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܢ܆ ܕܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܥ̈ܫܢ ܘܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܚ̈ܕܝܢ ܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܢ ܡܦܪ̈ܢܣ ܀ ܘܐܡܪ ܙ :ܐ ܬܘܒ. ܒܡܘܢܥܗܘܢ ܕܣܘܪ̈ܛܝܢ ܕܠܘܬܟܘܢ ܡܢܥܢܝ ܡܪܢ ܠܝܪ̈ܬܘܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܇ ܘܗܼܢܘܢ ܦܼܫܘ ܒܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܘܠܥܠܡ. ܘܡܟܝܠ ܒܚܙܘܐ ܕܣܦܝܠܐ ܒܥܝܢ ܥܠܡ. ܒܡܪܗ ܕܒܝܬܐ ܢܶܨܕ ܒܥܘܒܐ ܟܘܠܢܝܐ. ܘܠܒܪ ܡܢܗ ܢܪܡܪܡ ܠܣܿܓܝ ܨܡ̈ܚܐ. ܘܥܿܛܝܐ ܕܟܠ ܒܪܐܙܐ ܢܫܼܟܚ. ܘܐܦܢ ܚܢܢ ܠܢ ܠܐ ܝܺܕܘܥܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܝ ܐܘ̄ ܡܥܝܢ ܣܝܿܢܐ ܣܣܪܝܐ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܒܝܼ̈ܫܢ ܆ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܗ ܙ ܘܡܣܿܬܪ̈ܝܢ. ܡܢܘ ܕܗܼܘ ܒܗ ܠܐ ܢܬܕܡܿܪ. ܟܕ ܙܐ ܡܢ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܬܼܡܗܐ ܢܚܼܘܬ ܆ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܦܼܓܥܐ ܒܚܺܝܠܝ ܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܥܶܒܕ݁. ܘܠܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܚܝܡܐ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܙ ܘܗܼܘ ܒܗܘܢ ܘܗܼܢܘܢ ܒܗ. ܘܥܡܗܘܢ ܒܡܪܝܐ ܥ̈ܠܡܐ ܐ ܐ ܀ ܕ ܡܢܐ ܗܘ ܐܬܪܐ ܡܫ̇ܐܠ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ̇ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܐ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܬܐ ܢܬܕܡܪ. ܐܘ̇ ܐܝܟܢ ܙܘܥܗ ܘܩܘ̇ܕܫܗ. ܫܼܠܝܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐ ܡܦܿܣ ܘܬܘܒ ܠܐ. ܡܿܢ ܕܣܿܦܩ ܢܣܼܦܩ. ܘܡܿܢ ܕܠܐ ܣܿܦܩ ܆ ܢܝܿܩܪ ܒܫܼܬ ܩܐ ܠܕܡܫܬܿ ܒܚ ܇ ܘܪܲܚܶܡ ܕܢܫ̇ܿܒܚ :ܐ ܕܨܒܐ ܕܢܫܬܿܒܚ, ܐܝܟܢ ܢܡܗ ܠܐܬܪܐ ܕܚܝܙܬܐ. ܕܘܡܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܗܘ ܕܒܗ ܘܐ ܟܠ ܚ̇ܿܙܐ. ܐܟܡܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܐ ܒܟܠ ܒܨܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܘܝܬܝܪ. ܡܕܝܢ ܒܫܼܬܩܐ ܠܡܠܬܢ ܢܝ̇ܩܪ ܆ ܘܒܬܼܡܗܐ ܠܪܐܙܢ ܢܥ̇ܦܩ. ܘܥܡ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܦܫܝ̈ܛܐ ܒܪܐܙܐ ܢܪ̈ܥܡ. ܒܪ̈ܝܟ ܗܘ ܐܝܩܪܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܡܢ ܐܬܪܗ ܀
who hate us; for He has raised our lowliness and brought down our enemies who, without reason, took advantage of us. Blessed be the Lord our God who dispenses for our good what afflicts and what rejoices!" 2 He said again: "By the arrival of the lines that we had addressed to you, Our Lord has made me arrive at the inheritance of Life! As for this one, it has remained with its own, and forever!" [Also, in the vision of sapphire and by the eternal eye, let us fix the Master of the house in the universal breast; and, outside of this one, let us exalt the Multi-resplendent: let us find in the mystery Him who erases everything, even if we become ignorant of ourselves. O source of stinking mud, fountain of all evils, in which (however) Life is found hidden: (who would not be astonished at you), who would not be astonished at himself, when he descends from the place of astonishment where he meets the Powers of light who look at him there as a beloved, where he is in them and they in him, and with them in the eternal Lord? 3 You ask what this place is, or else you desire that I speak to you about it? Come (rather) to admire with me. Or else (you ask) of what kind is the movement that animates it and of what nature is its "Sanctus": silence alone knows it, and no one else. He who understands, let him understand! As for him who does not understand, let him honor in silence Him who is glorified and who loves to glorify him who desires to be glorified. How (in fact) would we give a name to the place of the vision? It resembles Him who sees everything in Himself, and who also appears in everything, in the smallest things as in the greatest. Let us therefore by silence do honor to our word: in astonishment let us embrace our mystery and with simple beauties let us proclaim mysteriously:
ܨܠܐ ܥܠܝ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܐܿܗܘܐ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܠܣܢ̈ܐܝ. ܚ̇ܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܠܒܣܬܪܝ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܕܠܩܕܡܝ ܪܕܐ ܐܢܐ. ܘܠܚܫ̈ܝ ܕܒܝ ܢܘܚܡܐ
Blessed be, from his place, the majesty of the Lord! 4 Pray for me, I beg you, so that I may not be a joy for my enemies. I see that I am going more toward what is behind me


[p. 358] {#po.39.p358}
ܥܒܕ ܐܢܐ. ܗܕܐ ܕܒܗܬܬܗ ܕܩܢܘܡܝ ܡܿܥܪܙ ܐܢܐ ܆ ܕܘܠܗܕܐ ܡܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܝ ܫ̇ܘܐ ܐܢܐ. ܘܡܪܐ ܟܠ ܛܫܝܗ̇ :ܕ ܒܕ ܣ̈ܓܝܐܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܼܡܘܗܝ ܘܛܒ ܗܘ ܀ ܢܓ ܕܬܡܢܥܣܖ̈ܐ ܥܠ ܛܘܟܿܣܐ ܕܐܚ̈ܐ ܫܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܀ ܗ̇ܢܘ ܛܘܟ̇ܣܐ ܢܟܦܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܝܼܡ ܥܠ ܡܪܝܐ. ܕܠܐ ܢܚܼܘܪ̈ܢ ܥܝܢ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܐܢܫ ܠܟܐ ܘܠܟܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܠܩܕܡܘܗܝ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܚܿܐܪ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘ,
than toward what is in front, and that I am resurrecting the passions that are in me! I preach what makes me ashamed, and it is this (shame) that I deserve by my works; but the Lord of all has hidden it because his mercy is great and he is good. 18 EIGHTEENTH LETTER [Rule of the beginning Brothers] 1 Here is the chaste rule which is loved by the Lord. 2 Let not the eyes of man wander here and there, but let him look only in front of him
ܕ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܡܿܠܠ ܝܬܝܖ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܐ̈ܠܨܢ. ܘܒܠܒܘܫܐ ܒܣܝܐ ܠܡܘܠܝܐ ܕܣܘܢܩܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܢܬܚܿܫܚ :ܐ ܘܒܣܝܒܖܬܐ ܠܩܘܝܿܡܐ ܕܦܓܪܗ ܢܬܚܿܫܚ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܒܝܥܢܘܬܐ «. ܘܒܨܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܡܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܢܣܬܝܒܪ. ܘܠܐ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܢܿܣܠܐ ܆ ܘܢ̣ܡܠܐ ܟܪܣܗ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܨܒܐ ܨܒܝܢܝܗ ܧܐ, ܕܡܝܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܢܬܪ̈ܢ. ܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܐ ܗܝ
3 Let him not speak superfluous words, but only those which are necessary. 4 Let him use, to answer the need of the body, miserable clothing. 5 Let him use food for the purpose of sustaining his body, and not out of greed; let him feed on everything in small quantity, without rejecting a part of it and filling his belly with what his good pleasure chooses, for discretion is better than all virtues.
& ܘܚܡܪ̈ܐ ܣܛܪ̈ ܡܢ ܕܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܆ ܐܘ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܡܚܝܼܠܘܬܐ ܘܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܠܐ ܢܸܫܬܐ «, ܘܠܐ ܢܦܣܘܼܩ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܗܘ ܕܡܡܿܠܠ ܘܢܡܿܠܠ ܗܼܘ ܕܕܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܪܕܝܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܢܐܓܪ ܐܝܟ ܚܿ̇ܟܝܡܐ.
6 Let him not drink wine except in the case where he would be with others, or else because of weakness or illness. 7 Let him not interrupt the person who is speaking in order to speak himself, like an uneducated man; but let him wait, like a wise man.
ܘܟܠ ܕܘܟܐ ܕܡܫܬܟܚ ܆ ܗܼܘ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܫܡܫܢܐ ܕܐܚܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܠܐ ܢܓܠܐ ܚܕ ܡܢ ܗܕܡܘ̈ܗܝ ܩܕܡ ܐܢܫ. ܘܠܐ ܢܩܼܪܘܒ ܠܦܓܪܐ ܕܐܢܫ ܕܠܐ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܐܢܢܩܝ ܘܠܐ ܠܦܓܪܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܢܫܒܘܩ ܕܢܬܩܪܒ ܐܢܫ ܕܠܐ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܐܘܠܨܢܐ.
8 Wherever he is, let him make himself small and the servant of his brothers. 9 Let him not uncover any of his limbs before anyone and let him not approach anyone's body without a reason that makes it necessary; let him not let anyone approach his body either without a reason that obliges him to.


[p. 360] {#po.39.p360}
ܘܡܢ ܦܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܡܘܬܐ ܩ̇ܛܘܠܗ ܢܙܕܗܪ. ܘܢܿܩܢܐ ܛܘܟܿܣܐ ܢܟܦܐ ܠܡܕܡܟܗ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܪ̈ܚܩ ܡܢܝܗ ܚܿܝܠܐ ܢܛܘܪ̈ܗ. ܘܟܕ ܕܡܝܟ ܐܢ ܡܨܝܐ ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܚܼܙܝܘܗܝ. ܘܪ̈ܘܩܐ ܩܕܡ ܐܢܫ ܠܐ ܢܼܫܕܐ. ܘܐܢ ܢܼܐܬܐ ܠܗ ܫܥܘܠܐ ܥܠ ܦܬܘܪܐ܆ ܢܿܗܦܟ ܐ̈ܦܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܇ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܢܸܫܥܠ. ܘܢܟܦܐܝܬ ܢܼܠܥܣ ܘܢܼܫܬܐ ܆ ܕܐܝܟ ܕܙܿܕܩ ܠܒܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܘܠܐ ܢܽܘܫܛ ܐܝܕܗ ܩܕܡ ܚܒܶܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܡܪ̈ܚܐܝܬ. ܘܐܢ ܚܬܒ ܥܡܗ ܐܟܣܢܝܐ ܆ ܢܥܼܨܝܘܗܝ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܐܘْ ܬܠܬ ܙܒܢ̈ܝܢ ܕܢܼܠܥܣ. ܘܢܝܼܚܐܝܬ ܢܣܒ ܘܢܣܝܼܡ ܥܠ ܦܬܘܪܐ ܘܠܐ ܢܒ̇ܣܪ. ܘܢܗܘ̈ܝܢ ܡܛܟ̈ܣܢ ܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܘܡܐܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܥܠ ܦܬܘܪܐ. ܘܟܕ ܡܬܦ̇ܘܩ ܆ ܢܟ̇ܣܐ ܦܘܡܗ ܡܢ ܕܢܬܚܼܙܐ ܇ ܘܢܼܟܠܐ ܣܿܘܩܗ ܘܥܿܒܪ.
10 Let him guard against familiarity as against death, his murderess. 11 Let him adopt a chaste position for sleeping so as not to distance from him the Force that guards him; and when he sleeps, let no one, if possible, see him. 12 Let him not spit before anyone and if a cough comes to him at the table, let him turn his face away from it before coughing. 13 Let him eat and drink modestly, as befits the sons of God. 14 Let him not reach out his hand with impudence (to serve himself) before his companions; and if a stranger sits with him, let him urge him two or three times to eat; then, quietly, let him take (the food) and place (it) on the table, and let him not disdain it. 15 Let his feet and his clothes be well arranged at the table. 16 When he yawns, let him hide his mouth so that it is not seen; let him hold his breath, and it will pass.
ܘܟܕ ܥܳܐܠ ܠܩܠܝܬܐ ܕܐܦܢ ܕܪܒܗ ܐܘ̇ ܕܬܠܡܝܕܗ ܐܘْ ܕܪܚܿܡܗ ܆ ܒܙܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܢ̣ܐܚܘܕ ܢܦܫܗ ܇ ܡܢ ܕܢ̣ܚܙܐ ܘܢܬܒܿܩܐ ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܒܗܿ. ܘܐܦܢ ܢܬܥܼܨܐ ܡܢ ܡܪܗ̇ ܠܡܥܒܕ ܗܕܐ ܇ ܠܐ ܢܬܬܦܼܝܣ. ܡܿܢ ܕܡ̇ܡܪܲܚ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܆ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܗܘ ܠܐܣܟܡܐ ܕܕܝܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܠܡܫܝܚܐ ܝܗܘܒܗ. ܘܠܐ ܢܚܼܙܐ ܠܕܘܟܬܐ ܕܒܗ̇ ܣܝܼܡܝܢ ܡܐܢܝ ܩܠܝܬܗ ܕܪܚܿܡܗ. ܘܢܝܼܚܐܝܬ ܢܐܚܘܕ ܘܢܸܦܬܚ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܘܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܆ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܫܼܬܡܥ ܩܠܗ. ܘܠܐ ܢܣܼܬܪܗܒ ܒܗܠܟܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܕܐܠܨܐ.
17 When he enters a cell, be it that of his master or that of one of his disciples or else that of his friend, let him refrain with vigilance from seeing and observing what is there, and even if he is urged to do so by the occupant of this (cell), let him not consent to it. He who has the impudence to behave otherwise is a stranger to the habit of religious and to Christ who gave it to him. Let him not see either at what place are stored the utensils that his friend uses in the cell. 18 Let him close and open his door and that of others gently, without any noise being heard. 19 Let him not hasten when he walks, except in case of necessity.
ܘܗܼܘ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܡܫ̇ܥܒܕܐ ܒܟܠ ܥܒ̇ܕ ܘܡܫܬܡܥܢܐ. ܘܠܐܝܢܐ ܕܐܚܼܝܕ ܒܨܒܘ̈ܬܐ ܐܘ̇ ܒܟܼܣܦܐ ܐܘ̇ ܒܥܠ̈ܡܝܐ ܆ ܠܐ ܢܬܢ̇ܩܦ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܥܒܼܕܐ ܠܣܛܢܐ.
20 Let him be submissive and obedient in every action. 21 Let him not join himself to him who is attached to objects, to money, to people of the world, for fear that he may become a slave of Satan.


[p. 362] {#po.39.p362}
ܘܒܤܝܡܐܝܬ ܢܡ̇ܠܠ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܐܢܫ ܆ ܘܢܟܦܐܝܬ ܢܸܚܘܪ ܒܟܠ ܒܖ̈ܢܫ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܢܸܡܠܐ ܥܝܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܡܢ ܦܪܨܘܦܐ ܕܐܢܫ. ܘܟܕ ܐܿܙܠ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܆ ܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܩ̇ܕܡ ܠܗܘ ܕܩ̇ܫܝܫ ܡܢܗ. ܘܐܢ ܦܐܫ ܡܢܗ ܚܒܪܗ ܡܛܠ ܥܼܠܬܐ ܐܝܕܐ ܕܗܼܝ ܆ ܢܪܚܩ ܡܢܗ ܩܠܝܠ ܘܢܿܓܪܪ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܐܿܬܐ. ܡܢ ܕܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܥܿܒܕ ܆ ܣܟܠܐ ܗܘ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܚܙܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܡܘܣ. ܘܐܢ ܡܡ̇ܠܠ ܚܒܪܗ ܥܡ ܐܢܫ̈ܝܢ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦܓܥܝܢ ܒܗܘܢ ܆ ܢܐܿܓܪ ܗܼܘ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܣܪܗܒ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܡܡܠܠ.
22 Let him speak to every man with kindness and let him look at every person with modesty; let him not fill his eyes with the face of someone. 23 When he is on the way, let him not get ahead of him who is older than him, and if his companion leaves him for any reason, let him move away a little from him and wait for him until he returns. He who does not do this is a fool similar to a lawless pig. And if his companion speaks with people they have met, let him wait without pressing the one who is speaking to hurry.
ܘܐܝܢܐ ܕܚܠܝܼܡ ܆ ܗܼܘ ܢܹܐܡܪ ܠܗܽܘ ܕܡܥܝܼܠ ܩܕܡ ܙܒܢܐ ܕ ܬܐ ܕ ܢܼܠܥܣ.
24 Let him who is in good health say before the hour to him who is sick: "Come, let us eat!"
ܘܠܐ ܢܿܟܣ ܠܒܪܢܫ ܥܠ ܣܟܠܘܬܗ ܐܝܕܐ ܕܗܼܝ. ܐܠܐ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܢܼܥܒܕ ܚܝ̇ܒܐ.
25 Let him not incriminate any man, whatever his fault; but let him make himself guilty in all things.
ܘܟܠ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܒܨܝܪ̈ܐ ܢܗܼܘܐ ܓܿܒܐ ܠܡܣܥܪ ܒܡܘܟܟܐ. ܘܟܕ ܓܚܟ ܠܐ ܢܓܠܐ ܫܢܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܥܡ ܢܫܺܶܐ ܐܢ ܐ̈ܠܨܐ ܕܢܡܿܠܠ ܆ ܟܕ ܡܗܦܟ̈ܢ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܗܝܢ ܢܡܿܠܠ. ܘܡܢ ܒܢ̄ܬ ܩܝ̇ܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܦܚܐ ܕܣܛܢܐ ܢܿܪ̈ܚܩ ܡܢ ܦܓܥܗܝܢ ܘܥܢܝܢܗܝܢ ܘܚܙܬܗܝܢ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܿܝܒ ܠܒܗ ܒܣܝ̇ܢܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܐ ܛܡ̈ܐܐ. ܘܐܦܢ ܐܚܘ̈ܬܗ ܢܗܘ̈ܝܢ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܢܼܛܪ ܢܦܫܗ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ.
26 Let him be willing to do all humble work with the concern of lowering himself. 27 When he laughs, let him not uncover his teeth. 28 If he is obliged to speak with women, let it be with his face turned away so as not to see them. As for the Daughters of the Covenant, let him avoid meeting them, frequenting them and seeing them, (by turning away from that) as from a trap of Satan, for fear of defiling his heart with the mud of impure passions; and even if they are his sisters according to nature, let him guard himself from them in all things as if they were strangers.
ܘܡܢ ܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܒܢ̈ܝ ܓܢܣܗ ܆ ܢܗܘܐ ܙܗܝܼܪ ܇ ܕܠܐ ܢܸܩܪ̈ܫ ܠܒܗ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ.
29 Let him beware of associating with members of his family, so that his heart does not lose the fervor of the love of God.
ܘܡܢ ܚܒܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܕܥܡ ܛܠܝ̈ܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܪܚܡܘܬܐ ܕܥܡ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܢܬܪܚܩ.
30 Let him distance himself from the company of young boys and from familiarity with them, as he would from friendship with the Evil One.
ܘܚܕ ܢܗܘܐ ܒܪ ܪܐܙܗ ܘܒܪ ܥܢܝܢܗ ܘܫܘܬܦܗ ܆ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܕܚܿܠ ܡܢ ܡܪܝܐ ܘܫܠܝܐ ܥܡ ܢܦܫܗ ܇ ܘܡܣܟܝܢ ܒܥܘ̣ܡܪܗ ܘܥܬܝܪ̈ ܒܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܘܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܢܫ ܬܘܒ ܢܛܪ ܪ̈ܐܙܘܗܝ ܘܕܘܒܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܆ ܘܡܢ ܕܢܓ݂ܠܐ ܥܡ̈ܠܘܗܝ ܘܩܪ̈ܒܘܗܝ.
31 Let there be only one (man) who is his confidant, his familiar, his intimate—someone who fears the Lord, who is at peace with himself, who is poor in his dwelling and rich in the mysteries of God; with every other man, let him keep his secrets and his ways of life to himself, (taking care) not to reveal his labors and his struggles.


[p. 364] {#po.39.p364}
ܘܠܐ ܢܫܕܐ ܡܢܗ ܓܿܠܗ ܒܠܥܕ ܡܢ ܐܢܢܩܝ ܆ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܗ̇ܘܐ ܕܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܡܢ ܐܢܫ. ܘܢܟܦܐܝܬ ܢܗܘܐ ܢܿܦܩ ܠܚܫܚܬܗ ܆ ܐܟܡܢ ܕܒܿܗܬ ܡܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܢܿܛܘܪ̈ܗ.
32 Let him not take off his mantle without being obliged to do so where he can be seen; and let him go out for his needs modestly, like someone who is ashamed before his Guardian Angel.
ܕܒܕܚܠܬ̇ܿ ܐܠܗܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܡܬܚܿܫܚ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܆ ܘܒܥܨܝܢܐ ܕܡܿܘܬܐ ܐܦ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܨܿܒܐ ܠܒܐ.
33 Let it be in the fear of God that he uses everything, in a mortal constraint and against the very will of the heart.
ܘܥܡ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܠܡܠܥܣ ܆ ܦ̈ܩܚ ܠܗ ܣܿܡ ܡܺܘܬܐ ܠܡܐܟܠ ܆ ܐܦܢ ܐܡܗ ܐܘْ ܚܬܗ ܬܗܼܘܐ.
34 Rather than eating with a woman, even if she be his mother or his sister, it is better for him to swallow a deadly poison.
ܘܡܢ ܕܢܟ̇ܣܐ ܥܡ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܓܿܠܐ ܘܢܕܡܟ ܆ ܕܐܦܢ ܐܚܘܗܝ ܟܝܢܝܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܆ ܛܒ ܠܗ ܕܥܡܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܥܡ ܬܢܝܢܐ. ܘܐܢ ܢܸܐܡܪ ܠܗ ܗܿܘ ܕܩܿܫܝܫ ܡܢܗ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܐܘ̇ ܒܣܘܥܪܝܐ ܆ ܬܐ ܢܙܡܪ̈ ܆ ܢܫܼܬܡܥ. ܘܐܢܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܢܫܼܬܘܩ ܆ ܘܠܒܗ ܢܫܿܒܚ ܠܐܠܗܐ.
35 Rather than wrapping himself with another in the (same) blanket and sleeping (thus), it would be preferable for him to dwell with a dragon. 36 If someone older than him says to him on the road or during work: "Come, let us chant the psalms!", let him agree; otherwise, let his tongue be silent and his heart glorify God.
ܘܥܠ ܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܢܬܚܪܐ. ܘܠܐ ܢܸܣܪܘܒ ܘܠܐ ܢܓܠ ܘܠܐ ܢܹܐܡܐ ܒܫܡܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ.
37 Let him have no altercation about anything; let him not argue, let him not lie, let him not swear by the Name of the Lord.
ܘܢܬܬܫܝܼܛ ܘܠܐ ܢܫܼܘܛ. ܘܢܬܥܼܠܒ ܘܠܐ ܢܥܠܘܼܒ. ܦܩܚ ܕܢܐܒ̈ܕܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܦܓܪܐ ܥܡ ܦܓܖܪܿܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܕܬܚܸܣܪ ܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܢܦܫܐ.
38 Let him be despised, but let him not despise; let him be wronged, but let him wrong no one. (For) it is better that the things of the body perish with the body, rather than a single one of the things of the soul be damaged.
ܘܕܝܼܢܝܐ ܥܡ ܐܢܫ ܠܐ ܢܹܐܡܪ. ܐܠܐ ܢܣ̇ܝܒܪ ܐܦ ܟܕ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܙܟܿܝܐ ܆ ܕܢܬܬܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܝ̈ܒܐ.
39 Let him enter into litigation with no one, but let him endure, even when he is innocent, being condemned as if he were guilty.
ܘܠܐ ܢܸܪ̈ܚܡ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܚܕܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܥܠܡܐ. ܘܢܫܼܬܡܥ ܠܪ̈ܝܫܢܐ ܘܡܢ ܚܘܠܛܢܗܘܢ ܢܪ̈ܚܩ ܆ ܦܚܐ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ ܕܨܿܐܕ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܠܐܒܕܢܐ. ܐܘ̄ ܝܥܢܐ ܪ݁ܚܶܡ ܟܪ̈ܣܗ ܆ ܦܿܩܚ ܠܟ ܕܬܣܝܼܡ ܒܟܪ̈ܣܟ ܓܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܛܘܓ̈ܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܝܼܫܢܐ.
40 Let him not love himself in any thing of the world. 41 Let him obey his superiors, but let him avoid mingling with them: this is a trap that catches the lax to their ruin. O gluttonous friend of your belly, it would be better for you to put burning coals into it rather than the fried foods of the superiors!
ܘܢܫܦܥ ܪ̈ܚܼܡܘܗܝ ܥܠ ܟܠܢܫ. ܟܕ ܪ̈ܚܝܼܩ ܘܦܣܝܼܩ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܢܫ. ܘܡܢ ܣܓܝܐܘܬ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܢܙܕܗܪ ܆ ܡܛܠ ܕܗܼܘ ܡܕܥܟ ܡܢ ܠܒܐ ܙܘ̈ܥܐ ܢܘܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ.
42 Let him pour out his mercy upon every man while being distant and separated from every human being. 43 Let him guard himself against an abundance of words, for they extinguish in his heart the movements of fire that emanate from God.


[p. 366] {#po.39.p366}
ܘܡܢ ܕܪ݁ܫܳܐ ܕܥܡ ܒܝ̈ܬܝܐ ܘܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܐܪܝܐ ܣܿܪ̈ܪܘܚܐ ܢܸܥܪܘܩ.
44 Let him flee from discussion, with acquaintances and with strangers alike, as (he would flee) a ferocious lion.
ܘܒܫܒܒܘܬܐ ܕܚܡ̈ܬܢܐ ܘܢܨܝ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܢܸܥܒܪ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܡܠܐ ܠܒܗ ܚܼܡܬܐ ܇ ܘܢ̇ܡܠܟ ܒܢܦܫܗ ܚܫܘܼܟܐ ܕܛܘܥܝܝ. ܘܥܡ ܫܒܗܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܐ ܢܸܥܡܖ̈ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܬܬܪ̈ܝܼܡ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬ݀ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܡܢ ܢܦܫܗ ܇ ܘܬܗܼܘܐ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܠܟܠ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܒܝܼܫ̈ܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܙܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܆ ܐܢ ܬܼܛܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܘ̄ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ. ܘܬܬܼܥܢܐ ܒܟܠܙܒܢ ܒܗܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܒܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܒܩܠܝܠ ܙܒܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܟ ܕܬܚܼܙܐ ܗܝ ܒܗ̇ ܢܘܗܪܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܆ ܘܠܥܠܡ ܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܡܝܐ. ܕܠܗ ܫܘ̣ܒܚܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܕܪ̈ܚܳܡܘܗܝ ܆ ܐܡܝܢ.
45 Let him not pass into the vicinity of the irascible and the quarrelsome, for fear that his heart might fill with anger and that the darkness of error might reign in his soul. 46 Let him not dwell with the vain, for fear that the operation of the Holy Spirit might withdraw from his soul, and that it might become a dwelling for all evil passions. 47 O man, if you observe all these warnings and if you devote yourself at every moment to meditation on God, your soul, in truth, will be able in a short time to see within itself the light of Christ, and it will never again be blind. To Him be the glory from all those who love Him. Amen.
ܝܛ ܕܬܫܥܣܪ̈ܐ ܐ ܥܠ ܡܣܪ̈ܩܘܬܐ ܘܐܘܪܚܐ ܩ̇̈ܛܝܢܬܐ ܆ܘܕܠܐ ܐܨܦ ܕܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܓܝܪ ܕܬܬܒܚܢ ܚܺܐܪ̈ܘܬܢ ܒܣܿܝܒܪ̈ܢܘܬܐ ܢܝ̈ܚܐ ܠܢܦܫܢ ܒܿܥܝܢܢ. ܘܡܪܐ ܟܠ ܠܨܒܝܢܢ ܥܳܨܝܢܢ ܕܢܫܬܥܒܕ ܆ ܘܠܘ ܕܚܢܢ ܢܫܬܡܥ ܠܨܒܝܢܗ. ܚܰܘܝܢ ܒܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܗ ܥܡܝܼܠܝܬܐ ܘܐ̈ܠܝܨܬܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܢܼܪܕܐ ܒܬܪܗ ܆ ܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܣܝܼܡܐ ܠܢ ܕܠܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܢܡܛܐ :ܙ ܘܚܢܢ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܒܣܬܪܢ ܢܬܦ̈ܢ ܠܢ ܆ ܒܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܚ̇ܝܪܺܝܢܢ ܀ ܗܼܘ ܥܡ ܕܝܠܗ ܣ̇ܝܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܝܘܡܐ ܠܐ ܩܼܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܆ ܘܚܢܢ ܡܙܘܢܝܐ ܠܫܢ̈ܝܐ ܠܡܣܡ ܒ̇ܥܝܢܢ. ܙܗܖ̈ܢ ܕܡܚܪ̈ ܠܐ ܬܹܐܨܦܘܢ܆ ܘܦܠܝܓܘܬܢ ܠܡܬܚܐ ܚܫܚܬܐ ܒ̈ܥܝܐ ܠܢ ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܢܣܡܘܟ ܪܝܫܗ ܐܸܡܪ :ܙ ܘܚܢܢ ܠܫܘܡܠܝ ܦܩܪ̈ܘܬܗ̇ ܕܙ̈ܓܬܢ ܆ ܣܼܬܪܵܐ ܡܣܿܩܠܐ ܒ̇ܢܝܢܢ ܦܐܬܙܙ܂ ܘܝ ܠܢ ܠܡ̈ܠܝܠܐ ܕܨܒܬܐ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܡܪ̈ܓܫܝ ܒܨܒܬܐ
19 NINETEENTH LETTER [On detachment and the narrow path, and on the duty not to be concerned with bodily things] 1 Certainly, before our freedom has been tested by patience, we seek rest for our souls, and force the Lord of all to submit to our will, instead of submitting ourselves to His! He has (however) shown us by his laborious and painful journey the way to walk in his footsteps, if indeed we are determined to arrive where He is. (But) we look with desire at the things that pull us backward. 2 He himself and his own did not possess food for a day, and we, we seek to store up provisions for years. He warned us not to be anxious about the morrow, and our doubt seeks for us sustenance for a long time. He said that He had nowhere to lay his head, and we, to satisfy the fury of our desire, arrange for ourselves a decorated shelter. Woe to us, rational beings,


[p. 368] {#po.39.p368}
ܒ̇ܥܝܢܢ ܙ ܘܠܐ ܒ̇ܗܬܝܢܢ ܡܢ ܦܘܠܘܣ ܕܚܼܦܝܝ ܘܥܪܛܠ ܡܗܠܟ ܆ ܘܚܢܢ ܕܐ ܒܣܐܘܢܐ ܡܫܰܚܪ̈ܐ ܚ̇ܕܝܢܢ ܇ ܘܥܠ ܪ̈ܟܝܟܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܢ ܡܢܥܝܼܚܝܢܢ. ܦܘܠܘܣ ܡܿܣܢܐ ܦܓܪܗ ܕܢܫܼܦܪ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܕܓܒܝܗܝ ܆ ܘܚܢܢ ܕܘܝ̈ܐ ܡ̇ܿܫܦܪܝܢܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܢܣܼܬܢܐ ܡܢ ܫ̇ܦܝܪܐ ܀ ܒܪ ܕܐ ܙܒܕܝ ܬܠܡܝܕܐ ܪ̈ܚܼܝܡܐ ܕܝ̇ܕܥ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢ ܒܪ̈ܝܫܝܬ ܘܐܦ ܚܼܙܝܗܝ ܘܓܠܫܗ ܘܡܣ̇ܒܪ ܆ ܕܟܡܝܪ ܘܡ̇ܫܢܝ ܚܼܙܘܗ ܘܣܼܢܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܩܥܐ ܒܝܬܐ ܘܚܰܕܪ ܩܨ̈ܝܐ ܠܫܪܪܘܬܐ ܘܐ ܇ ܕ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܝܼܥܐ ܙܕ ܠܠܐ ܝܺܕܘܥܶܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܡܥܢ̇ܘܐ. ܘܐܢ̈ܐ ܫܦܠܐ ܘܡܩ̈̇ܛܥܐ ܠܡܖܪ̈ܕܐ ܒܬܪܗ ܡܢ ܚܒܫ̇ܢܘܬܝ ܠܐ ܐܿܫܟܚܬ. ܘܙܕܝܩܐ ܕܡܢ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܫܘܐ ܐܕ ܕܢܬܦܪ̈ܢܣ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܥܢ̇ܘܐ ܐܢܐ. ܘܥ̇ܿܛܦ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܡܿܠܘܟܝ :ܙ ܘܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ. ܕܐܢܿܐ ܚܫ̈ܝ ܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ :ܕ ܠܐ ܐܬܡܿܠܝܘ ܆ ܝܿܕܥ ܗܼܘܝܬ ܕܡܠܝܗܘܢ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܐܢܝܢ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܀ ܡܕܝܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܿܡܪ ܕܦܘܠܘܣ ܘܟܠܗ ܓܘܕܐ ܕܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܥ̈ܩܬܐ ܘܛܘܪ̈ܦܐ ܫܿܠܡܘ ܚܝܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܢܦܬܐ ܠܟܠܢܫ ܚܫܝܼܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܙ܀ ܘܠܐ ܚܫܝ̈ܒܐ ܠܙܠܝܠܐ ܚܫܝܼܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܀ ܆ ܟܒܪ ܠܘ ܒܨܒܝܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܙ ܪܗܿܛܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐ̇ܚܘܪ ܒܡܪܗܘܢ ܀܆ ܘܐܿܠܦ ܫܖ̈ܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܚܝܕ̈ܝܢ ܀ ܀ ܫܡܥܘܓܝܖ̈ ܕܐܼܡܪܿ. ܕܐܿܢܝܐ ܐܢܼܐ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ. ܡܕܝܢ ܛܪ̈ܩܐܝܬ ܫܿܠܡܘ ܐܦ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܀ ܘܡܿܢܥܘ ܠܘܬܗ. ܐܣܿܬܟܠܬ ܕܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܪܝܪ̈ܢ ܢܿܒܥܐ ܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܙ ܚܿܕܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܛܘܪ̈ܦܝ. ܕܢܚ̇ܕܝܢܝ ܒܕܢܚܗ. ܟܕܓܝܪ̈ ܛܥܼܝܢ ܐܢܐ ܫܘܡ̈ܬܗ ܒܗܕܡ̈ܝ ܆ ܪܿܘ̇ܙܐܢܐ ܕܫܟ̣ܝܚܵ̈ܢ ܒܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܣܿܝܒܪܖ ܘܐ ܐ ܀
who ask for ornament for that which is insensible to ornament! We are not ashamed before Paul walking unshod and unclothed, but we rejoice in (our) black-polished shoes and rest our bodies on delicate things. Here is Paul who makes his body ugly to be beautiful in the eyes of Him who chose him, whereas we, miserable ones, we embellish our bodies to be ugly in the eyes of the Beautiful. 3 And here is the son of Zebedee, the beloved disciple who knows Him who is from the beginning, who has even seen and touched Him and who proclaims Him (here he is) having a sad appearance and the look of a fool, provoking repulsion because of (his) old, patched clothes, begging for pieces of bread for (his) meal, presenting himself as a stranger to those who do not know (him); while I, a weak and negligent being who, because of my laziness, have not been able to walk in his footsteps, present myself as a righteous man deserving to be provisioned by a great number of people, and I retort to the one who exhorts me: "As far as I am concerned, all my passions have not been satisfied; and know that they are the work of the will of the Lord." I should therefore say (also) of Paul and of the whole cohort of luminous beings who spent their entire lives in afflictions and tribulations, who were held by everyone as refuse, who were considered by vile people as unworthy of consideration: perhaps they were not engaged in a race that the will of God traced? But it is their Master (Himself) that I must look at to learn the truth that they followed. 4 They indeed heard Him say: "I am the Way." And that is why they too finished (their lives) with valor and arrived until Him. (And I) I understand that it is from bitter things that his sweetness springs; I rejoice in my tribulations so that He may rejoice me by his appearance; bearing indeed his stigmata in my members, I exult that there is in me what he endured.
ܒܚܫܐ ܥܣܩܐ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܐ ܕܬܼܛܥܢ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܆ ܥܠܡ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܚܠܦܘܗܝ ܢܣܒ ܐܢܬ. ܐܢ ܬܣܿܝܒܪ̈ ܩܿܠܺܝܠ ܟܦܢܐ ܚܠܦ ܘܘ ܚܘܼܒܗ ܆ ܐܦ ܙ̈ܓܬܗ ܬܫܢܟ ܠܡܥܝܙܐ ܦܪܨܘܦܗ. ܐܸܢ ܢܬܚܼܙܐ ܚܼܫܟܐ ܙ ܒܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܟ ܒܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܕܚܠܦܘܗܝ ܆ ܢܪܗܝܟ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܐ ܢܘܼܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܣܿܘܦܐ. ܐܢ ܬܫܼܠܚ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܟ ܢܿܠܒܫܟ ܢܘܗܪܗ ܘܢܚ̇ܦܐ ܡܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܟ. ܐܢ ܬܫܒܼܘܩ
5 For the painful suffering of a (single) day that you will have endured because of Him, you will receive in exchange the world of light. If you endure hunger a little for his love, it is the desire for Him that will torment you (with the need) to see his face. And if darkness spreads over your face because of the labors accomplished for Him, He will make you shine with his glory for a duration without end. If you strip yourself of what is yours, He will clothe you with his light and hide from your eyes what is yours. If


[p. 370] {#po.39.p370}
ܗܠܝܢ ܕܩܢܼܐ ܐܢܬ ܆ ܬܩܼܢܝܘܗܝ ܠܗ ܒܢܦܫܟ ܘܠܥܠܡ. ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܥܡ̈ܠܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܨܘܬ ܢܝܼܫܐ ܒ̇ܥܐ ܡܪ̈ܐ ܟܠ ܀ ܀ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܠܐ ܡܣܿܝܟܝܢ ܠܗ. ܘܒܗܘܢܐ ܕܢ̇ܛܪ ܕܟܝܘܬܐ ܥܿܡܪ ܘܡܬܚ̈ܘܐ ܆ ܟܪ̈ܘܒܐ ܘܣܪ̈ܦܐ ܒܟܢܝܟܘܬܐ ܚܳܝܪܺܝܢ ܘ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܐ ܕܪܒܘܬܗ܆ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܕܟܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܚܦܝܬܐ ܚܿܙܝܐ ܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܒܩܢܘܡܗܿ. ܡܐ ܕܐܬܕܟܝ ܗܘܢܝܐ ܡܢ ܚܫܟܐ ܕܛܘܥܝܝ ܆ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܫܡܝܐ ܠܡܠܐܟܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ. ܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܬܓܠܝ ܫܘܼ̣ܒܚܗ ܕܡܪܗܘܢ ܒܚܘܼܒܐ ܆ ܬܡܢ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܩ̈ܥܝܢ ܩܘܿܕܫ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܫܪܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ ܕܐܼܡܪ ܩܕܡܝ ܐܚܐ ܚܕ ܗܟܢܐ. ܕܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܝܿܨܦ ܐܢܐ ܕܢܦܫܝ ܒܥܘܕܪ̈ܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܡܥܢ̇ܝܠ ܠܝ ܆ ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܬܓܠܝܢܘܬܗ ܒܠܒܝ ܒܫܘܼܒܚܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠ. ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܕܢ̇ܿܚܝܢ ܥܠܝ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܡܠ̈ܐܟܐ ܩܕܝܫ̈ܐ ܒܪܐܙܐ ܕܠܐ ܦܘܫܩ ܆ ܘܡܥ̇̈ܘܝܢ ܠܝ ܪܚܡܬܗܘܢ ܕܠܘܬܝ ܕܥܿܒܪܐ ܠܬܘܢܝܐ. ܘܒܙܒܢܝܐ
you abandon what you possess, you will possess Him Himself in your soul forever. (But) more than painful works, it is the uprightness of intention that the Lord of all seeks. 6 The heaven and the earth do not contain God, and He dwells and shows Himself in the spirit that keeps purity! The Cherubim and the Seraphim look with restraint at the glory of his Greatness, and the pure soul sees this glory without a veil within itself! When the spirit has been purified of the darkness of error, it becomes a heaven for the angels of light, and where the glory of their Lord has been revealed by love, there too they proclaim their "Sanctus." It is in all truth that a Brother declared before me: "At the moments when I take care of my soul with the help of God who strengthens me, I see his revelation in my heart in an ineffable glory. And then the holy angels appear to me also by an inexplicable mystery, and they show me the love they have for me, which surpasses what one can say of it. But when I let myself go, they show themselves to me with a joyless face."
ܕܡܗܡܝܢܘܬܝ ܒܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܐ ܠܐ ܦܨܝ̣ܚܐ ܠܝ ܡܬܚܿܘܝܢ ܀ ܛܘܒܝܗܘܢ ܠܕܕܟܝܢ ܒܠܒܗܘܢ. ܕܒܠܒܗܘܢ ܢܚܼܙܘܢ ܠܐܠܗܐ. ܒܐܬܪܐ ܓܝܪ ܕܐܬܕܟܝ ܆ ܒܗ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܫܘ̣ܼܒܚܗ ܕܕܟܝܐ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܐܼܡܪ ܕܠܓܘ ܡܢܟܘܢ ܡܛ̈ܫܝܐ. ܟܠ ܕܚܿܠܦܘ ܥܠܡܗܘܢ ܒܪ̈ܓܬܗ ܘܐܪ̈ܚܼܩܘ ܡܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܚܒ̈ܝܠܐ ܆ ܐܫܼܟܚܘ ܠܗܘܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܠܒܿܪܘܝܐ ܕܥܠ̈ܡܐ. ܠܐܓܝܖ̈ܝܢ ܝܿܕܥܝܢ ܦܣܘ̈ܩܐ ܡܛܠ ܕܚܫܘܟܐ ܒܐܬܪܗ ܠܐ ܡܫܬܿܡܗ ܀
7 Blessed are those who are pure in their heart, for in their heart they will see God. In the place that has been purified is seen indeed the glory of the Pure; and that is the Kingdom of which He said: "It is hidden within you." All those who, out of desire for Him, exchange their world and distance themselves from their corrupt life find for a world the Creator of the worlds; and they know nothing that cuts them off (from Him), for in His place darkness is not named.
ܟܟ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܆ ܘܥܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬ ܟܪ̈ܣܐ ܥܿܒܕܐ ܕܚܙܝܪܝܐ ܀ ܨܝܿܕܐ ܕܨ̈ܐܕ ܨܝܕܐ ܆ ܠܸܩܛܐ ܒܿܕܪ ܥܠ ܦܚܗ ܆ ܘܗܟܢ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܨܿܐܕ ܠܗ ܆ ܘܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܒܟܪ̈ܣܗ ܨܐܕ ܠܗ ܡܿܪ̈ܘܕܐ. ܒܐܒܘܟ ܐܬܒ̇ܿܩܐ ܒܡܢܐ
20 TWENTIETH LETTER [On gluttony which makes one like the pigs] 1 The hunter who seeks to capture his prey spreads bait on his trap and he can thus seize it. The monk, it is by his belly


[p. 372] {#po.39.p372}
ܨܕܗ ܢ̇ܿܟܘܠܗ ܆ ܕܬܼܐܠܦ ܐܢܬ ܐܝܟܢ ܬܩܪܒ ܥܡܗ. ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܡ̇ܠܐ ܟܪ̈ܣܗ ܆ ܪ̈ܥܝܐ ܗܘ ܕܚܙܝܪ̈ܐ ܇ ܘܣܓܝ ܒܝܼܫܐ ܡܪܥܝܬܗ. ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܢܿܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܆ ܪ̈ܥܝܐ ܗܘ ܕܥܢܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܇ ܘܛܒ ܕܪܰܚܶܡ ܠܗ ܕܐ ܪܒ ܪ̈ܥܘܬܐ ܀ ܒܚ̈ܕܕܐ ܥܙܝܼܠܝܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܐܚ̈ܝ. ܐܢ ܠܗܢܐ ܚܫܐ ܬܫܬܥܒܕ ܆ ܠܒܪ ܙܘܓܗ ܡܢ ܩܛܹܝܪ ܥܒܼܕܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܐܢܬ. ܒܪܡ ܡܥܝܢܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܒܘܬ ܟܪ̈ܣܐ ܗܝ ܆ ܘܝ ܠܗ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܕܫܢܬܐ ܟܝܐ ܠܗ. ܕܗܿܢܐ ܝܕܝܼܥ ܗܘ ܕܡܫܥܒܕܐ ܗܘ ܕܕܒܼܐ ܣܪܘܚܬܐ ܪܒܘܬ ܟܪ̈ܣܐ ܆ ܠܐ ܬܼܐܡܪ ܕܨܿܡܬ ܝܘܡܐ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܘ ܬܘܒ ܣܼܥܬ̇ ܥܠܝ ܫܸܢܬܐ ܝܬܝܖ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܫܸܩܠܘ ܢܣܝܝܐ ܡܛܟܣܐ ܐܳܿܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܕܐ. ܕܡܫܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܠܐ ܡܥܝ̈ܕܐ ܆ ܛܒ ܡܦܿܣ ܐܢܐ. ܐܢܕܝܢ ܬܚܼܘܕ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܒܨܝܪܘܬ ܣܝܒܖ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܬܫܿܚܠܦ ܛܟܣܐ ܒܥܼ̈ܠܠܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܘܠܝܬܐ ܆ ܒܿܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܠܗ ܠܢܝܳܚ ܦܓܪ̈ܟ ܘܠܐ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܐܢܬ. ܘܟܕ ܢ̇ܦܝܠ ܐܢܬ ܘܪܝܫܟ ܡܟܿܣܝ ܆ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܫܼܬܟܚ ܐܢܬ. ܘܣܟ ܕܒܐܡܝܢܘ ܣܢܝܼܩܐ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܆ ܠܐ ܬܡܠܐ ܟܪܣܟ ܣܲܓܝ܆ ܕܠܐ ܬܫܰܢܩܟ ܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܠܐ ܬܡܥܝܠ ܦܓܪ̈ܟ ܣ̈ܓܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܐ̈ܠܝܟ ܣܵܩܘܪܵܟ. ܛܟܣܐ ܐܚܼܘܕ ܆ ܘܗܐ ܡܟܝܠ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܡܠܟܐ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܐܢܬ ܇ ܘܕܠܐ ܕܚܼܠܐ ܗܿܘܝܐ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܟ ܀
that the Rebel captures him. Consider your father (and see) by what his deceiver caught him in order to learn, you, how to fight him. The monk who fills his belly is a shepherd of pigs and his flock is of the worst kind; (but) the monk who observes a discipline is the shepherd of the sheep of Christ, and he is much loved by the Master of shepherds. 2 The passions, my Brothers, are intertwined one with the other, and if you submit to one, you will necessarily be the slave of its companion; however, the source of all is gluttony. Woe to the monk whom sleep strikes down! It is clear that he is enslaved to the harmful bear that is gluttony. Do not say: "I fasted one day, or two days, and then I was beset by a more overwhelming sleep!". It is those who have not had the experience (of a) well-regulated (fast) who say that. That the members, unaccustomed, weaken in a short time, I know very well. But if you adopt the rule that consists of eating little, and if you do not modify (this) rule without grave reasons, you will seek to give rest to your body and you will not succeed: slumped, head covered, you will find yourself awake. It is without reserve and in a continuous manner that you need such a rule. Do not fill your belly abundantly, for fear that lust might torment you; (but) do not weaken your body too much, for fear that your enemy might exhaust you. Adopt a rule, and you will henceforth walk on the royal road, and your journey will be without danger.
ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܟܘܪ̈ܗܢܐ ܕܐܫܬܐ ܩܫܝܬܐ ܘܡܥܘܠ ܐܢ ܬܩܪ̈ܒ ܠܗ ܣܝܒܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܡܙܝܥ̈ܢ ܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܒܚܙܬܗܝܢ ܓܥܨܐ ܬܐܪܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܡܩܒܠ ܠܗܝܢ ܗܟܢܐ ܘܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܟܝܢܝܬܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܇ ܐܢܗܘ ܕܬܬܡܥܝܠ ܡܢ ܙܥܘܪܘܬ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ. ܘܢܝܬܘܢ ܫ̈ܐܕܐ ܘܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܕܡܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܨܘܦܐ ܡܙܝܥ̈ܝ ܚܫܐ ܆ ܚܢܢ ܒܠܐ ܐܢܟܝܢܐ ܡܩܘܝܢܢ ܇ ܟܕ ܓܥܨܐ ܬܐܪ̈ܬܢ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܓܬ ܙܢܝܘܬܐ ܆ ܡܛܠ ܕܒܡܥܝܠܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܚܫܐ ܟܝܢܝܐ. ܒܝܕ ܣܝܒܪܬܐ ܫܝܛܬܐ ܘܒܨܝܪܘܬܐ ܡܬܡܚܠܝܢ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܘܒܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܣܝܦܝܢ ܘܡܝܬܝܢ. ܗܢܘ ܣܝܦܝܐ ܩܛܘܠܗܘܢ ܀
3 Just as if you present to someone who has a strong bout of fever and who is sick some food whose appearance arouses the appetite, he feels disgust for them and refuses them: so it is with the lustful desire of nature if it is debilitated by the small quantity of food. Let the demons bring the memory of forms and faces that arouse passion, we remain unharmed, feeling disgust for the desire of lust, because the natural passion finds itself in a state of weakness. It is by humble and not abundant food that the passions weaken; and it is by the memory of God that they are extinguished and die: that is the sword that kills them.


[p. 374] {#po.39.p374}
ܡܪܢ ܝܫܘܥ ܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܡܝܬ ܠܪ̈ܓܝܬ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܥܒ̈ܕܝܟ ܒܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܒܟ ܐܡܝܢܢ. ܐܪܡܐ ܡܪܝ ܒܘܢܐܟ ܩܕܝܫܬܐ ܒܐܪܥ ܢܦܫܢ. ܘܢܐܩܕܘܢ ܡܢܗ̇ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܟܘܒ̈ܐ ܚܢܘ̈ܩܐ ܡܥܘܟܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܙܪܥܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܐܙܕܪ̈ܥܘ ܒܐܫܟܪ̈ܢ ܆ ܢܥܒܕ ܒܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܒܬܠܬܝܗܝܢ ܡܘܫܚ̈ܬܐ ܆ ܕܒܗܝܢ ܩܐܡ ܟܠܗ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܡܠ̈ܝܠܐ ܕܪܘܚ ܘܕܦܓܪ. ܣܟܐ ܆ ܕܗܘ ܕܡܠܐ ܟܪ̈ܣܗ ܘܠܗܢܐ ܚܫܐ ܐܫܬܥܒܕ ܆ ܥܒܕܐ ܗܘ ܕܗܘ ܠܗ ܫܥܒܕ ܘܠܟܠ ܚܫ̈ܝܢ. ܘܟܕ ܗܢܐ ܢܙܕܟܐ ܆ ܠܟܠܝܗܘܢ ܒܦܫܝܩܘܬܐ ܙܟܝܢܢ. ܗܘܝܘ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܙܢܝܘܬܐ. ܟܣܦܐ ܡܛܠܬܗ. ܫܘܒܚܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܡܛܠܬܗ ܆ ܕܢܓܣܓܘܢ ܐܝܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܟܠܒܐ ܠܐ ܡܟܒܫܐ ܆ ܡܛܠܬܗ ܢܒܚ ܕܠܐ ܝܗܒܝܢ ܠܗ. ܦܗܝܐ ܕܒܩܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܒܝܬ ܚܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܆ ܕܢܡܠܐ ܟܪ̈ܣܢ ܘܣܟܐ ܆ ܕܟܠ ܩ̈ܛܠܐ ܘܟܠ ܒܝ̈ܫܢ ܆ ܡܛܠܬܗ̇ ܕܫܝܘܠ ܕܬܬܡܠܐ. ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܣܥܪܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܣܪ̈ܘܚܬܐ. ܬܫܬܢܩ ܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܡܫܢܩܐ ܠܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܟܕ ܣܓܝ ܡܠܝܢܢ ܠܗ̇ ܙܒܠܐ ܡܣܓܝܢܢ. ܗܠܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ. ܘܒܕܡܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܘܗܠܝܢ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܡܬܝܠܕܝܢ ܕܩܕܡ ܐܬܐܡܪܘ ܆ ܘܟܕ ܗܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܓܥܝ̈ܨܐ ܆ ܡܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܓܠܙܝܢ ܠܢ ܒܫܘܡܠܝܗܘܢ. ܡܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܢ ܣܟ̈ܠܐ ܡܘܒܕܝ ܚܝܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܐܠܐ ܢܬܚܫܚ ܐܝܟ ܚܟ̈ܝܡܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܫܥܬ ܝܘܡܐ ܀
4 Lord Jesus Christ, make the passionate desire die in your servants by the desire for You. Amen! Cast, Lord, your holy fire upon the earth of our soul, that by it may be burned all the thorns which, by suffocating it, atrophy the holy seed that has been sown in our field, and that it may produce in us fruits at the three levels according to which is constituted the totality of the nature of rational beings (at once) spiritual and bodily. 5 This suffers no exception: he who fills his belly and is submitted to this passion (of gluttony) is a slave who has submitted to all the others. And when that one is conquered, we will easily conquer them all. It is the source of lust; money (is sought) because of it; because of it (also) human glory, thanks to which gifts abound; anger, that untamed bitch, barks because of it, because one does not give it (to eat); wandering in the villages and among companions, it is (still) in order to fill our belly. In short, all murders and all evil actions are perpetrated because of this one, so that (this) Sheol may be filled: all this is the work of (this) harmful wolf. Let him be tortured, so that he does not torture us; for the more we fill him, the more we make filth abound: these are the stinking fruits that he produces to the sight. And similar to them are the others that are born from him and that have just been said, (these fruits) so abominable that they deprive us of eternal life when they reach maturity. Let us not therefore be fools who lose their life, but let us use as wise men the things that last only for a day.
ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܛܒ ܒܣܪܐ ܠܝ ܬܐܪܬܝ ܆ ܟܕ ܡܬܒܩܐ ܐܢܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܘܠܝܐܝܬ ܡܬܚܫܚܝܢ ܒܗܝܢ. ܟܕ ܥܡ ܕܡܫܡܠܝܢ ܒܗܝܢ ܓܬܗܘܢ ܆ ܡܬܬܣܝܡ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܚܘܒܠܗܝܢ ܆ ܘܒܗܬܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܘܡܗܦܟܝܢ ܐܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܠܐ ܟܝ ܥܗܕܝܢ ܕܫܩܠܘ ܢܣܝܢܐ ܕܣܪܝܘܬܗܘܢ ܗܕܐ ܘܡܬܟܚܕܝܢ ܆ ܚܙܝܬ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܕܠܗܕܐ ܟܠܗ ܦܘܠܚܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܫܪܟ ܆ ܡܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܟܝܡܐ ܕܒܪ ܚܝܝ̈ܟ ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܡܢܗ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ ܣܢܝܩ ܐܢܬ ܀
6 For me, my conscience reproaches me harshly when I consider those who do not use them as is fitting. Yet, barely have they satisfied their desire with them than the corruption of these things is placed before their eyes, that they are ashamed of them and that they turn their faces from them! Could it be that they forget (each time) the experience they have had of this stench which is theirs and the confusion it causes them? You see, my Brother, that it is to this that all the activity of the world leads; direct therefore your life as a wise man in (the use of) what comes from it and which you need.
ܡܫܝܚܐ ܪ̈ܝܫܐ ܕܚܝܝ̈ܢ ܥܠܐ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܦܘܠܚܢܐ ܕܗܟܢ ܫܘܠܡܗ ܚܙܝܢܢ. ܘܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܢܗܝܢ ܕܓܝܓܐ ܠܟ ܡܬܚܙܝܢ ܆ ܘܐܦ ܠܡܫ̈ܡܫܢܝ
7 O Christ, Principle of our life, raise us above an activity that we see having such an end. Let the things whose fruits


[p. 376] {#po.39.p376}
ܩܘܕܫܟ ܡܢܕܝܢ ܆ ܒܗܝܢ ܢܗܘܐ ܦܘܠܚܢܢ ܘܗܙ̈ܓܢ ܇ ܒܗܢܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܡܬܚܡܐ ܕܝܗܒܬ ܠܢ ܠܒܘܩܝܢ ܚܐܪ̈ܘܬܢ ܀ ܘܢܡܢܥ ܡܢܗ ܒܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܚܦܝ ܠܘܬܟ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܐܡܝܢ ܀
appear desirable to You and also rejoice the Servants of your Holiness be, they, the object of our activity and our meditation, in this limited time that You have given us so that our freedom may be tested. And that outside of this (time) we may arrive until You in a confidence without a veil, by Your mercy. Amen.
ܟܐ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܚܕܐ ܆ ܥܠ ܚܡܬܐ ܘܥܠ ܙܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܀ ܘܝ ܠܗ ܠܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܪ̈ܓܘܙܬܢܐ. ܕܠܒܗ ܥܘܡܕܐ ܗܘ ܕܐܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܆ ܕܟܠܝܘܡ ܫܬܐ ܡܢ ܡܪܬܗ. ܡܠܩܛ ܡ̈ܠܐ ܗܘ ܠܗ ܕܚܩ ܡܢ ܡܪܝܐ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܀ ܠܐ ܫܟܢܐ ܥܠܘܗܝ. ܣܬܘܪܐ ܆ ܠܐܡܬܝ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܗ ܒܝܬܐ ܇ ܕܗܘ ܠܗ ܡܩܝܡ ܕܠܐ ܬܛܠܝܠܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܚܡܬܢܐ ܥܡ ܡܢܘ ܢܫܬܝܢ ܆ ܕܐܡܝܢ ܡܕܠܚ ܠܒܗ ܆ ܘܡܫܢܐ ܡܢܗ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܘܬܡܢ ܝܠܢܝܐ ܥܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܡܪ̈ܒܝܐ ܀
21 TWENTY-FIRST LETTER [On irascibility and on vigilance] 1 Woe to the irascible monk whose heart is a dwelling of vipers, for each day he drinks of their venom! He who speaks like a parrot pushes himself away from the Lord, for grace does not rest upon him. Until when will the slanderer find himself a house to erect for himself without the roof of God? The irascible man, with whom will he be at peace, he who ceaselessly troubles his heart and makes the Spirit of God depart from it, so that the scorpion breeds and multiplies there?
ܥܪܘܩ ܡܢ ܬܪܝܢ ܠܫܢܐ. ܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܣܡܝܡ̈ܐ ܡܪܡܐ ܒܠܒܐ ܕܨܝܘ̈ܬܘܗܝ. ܐܪ̈ܚܩ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܡܐ ܆ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܥܒܕܝܢ ܥܡ ܐܠܗܐ. ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܗܘܝ ܠܟܠ ܥܬܝܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܕܦܘܠܚܢܗܘܢ ܟܠܗ ܕܦܬܟܪ̈ܐ ܗܘ. ܠܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܢܨܝ̈ܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܬܫܪܐ ܠܓܝܘܢ ܒܓܘ ܒܝܬܟ. ܐܕܙܗܪ ܡܢ ܐܟܬܢܐ ܆ ܕܫܐܕܐ ܗܘ ܗܢܐ ܕܡܓܫܡ ܡܢ ܡܢ ܕܡܩܠܣ ܠܟ ܘܓ ܣܟܪ ܐܢܝܟ ܘܥܪܘܩ. ܕܠܐ ܢܫܠܚ ܡܟ ܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܘܢܠܒܫܟ ܒܪ̈ܕܥܬܐ ܕܠܒܝܫ. ܒܪܚܡ ܪ̈ܝܫܢܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܥܡܪ ܐܠܗܐ ܆ ܐܦܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܬܥܡܪ ܥܡܗ. ܕܡܩܝܡ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܒܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܐܠܨܐ ܠܨܒܝܢ ܐܠܗܐ ܣܢܐܐ ܗܘ. ܡܪܚܐ ܠܚܘܝܝܐ ܡܬܕܡܐ. ܘܡܐܟܘܠܬܗ ܥܦܪ̈ܐ ܗܘ ܕܡܪܝܡ ܩܠܗ ܝܕܝܥ ܗܘ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܠܝܬ ܒܗ ܀
2 Flee from the double-tongued man: he sends poisoned arrows into the heart of those who listen to him. Distance yourself from the proud, for they make war against God. Be a stranger to all the rich, for their work is entirely idolatrous. Do not be the companion of the quarrelsome, for fear that the Legion might dwell inside your house. Guard yourself against the irascible man, for he is an incarnate demon. Close your ear to the remarks of the one who praises you in your presence and flee from him, for fear that he might strip you of God and clothe you with the pack-saddle that he carries. In him who loves superiority God does not dwell: you too, do not dwell with him. He who does his own will when he is not obliged to is the enemy of the will of God. The impudent one is comparable to the serpent, and dust is his food. He who raises his voice, it is clear that Christ is not in him.
ܛܪ ܗܠܝܢ ܙܘܗܪ̈ܝ ܪܚܡܟ. ܕܒܗܘܢ ܬܬܢܛܪ ܀
3 Keep these warnings of your friend so that they themselves may keep you.
ܟܒ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ.
22 TWENTY-SECOND LETTER


[p. 378] {#po.39.p378}
ܐ ܠܐ ܬܚܫ ܡܛܠܬܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܗܘ ܕܒܙܒܢ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܘܬܝ ܡܛܠ ܚܛܗܝ ܆ ܫܪܝ ܡܚܘܐ ܦܐܝܘܬ ܙܠܝܩܘܗܝ. ܘܠܙܢܐ ܕܥܩܬܐ ܫܚܠܦ ܡܪܝ ܒܛܝܒܘ. ܟܕ ܠܐ ܚܐܪ ܠܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܥܒ̈ܕܝ ܆ ܚܣ. ܘܠܐ ܣܥܪ ܠܘܬܝ ܗܟܢܐ ܘܐܦܠܐ ܢܣܥܘܪ. ܐܠܐ ܒܐܡܝܢܘ ܚܠܛ ܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܒܡܪ̈ܝ. ܡܛܠ ܕܠܝܬ ܠܢ ܕܢܣܒ ܡܢܗ ܘܢܪܘܙ ܆ ܒܠܥܕ ܕܢܫܬܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܘܢܚܫ. ܘܕܠܐ ܢܬܒܥ ܢܝܚܐ ܐܡܝܢܐ ܀
1 Do not suffer on my account: the light of my eyes, that light which for a moment had (no longer) been with me because of my sins, has begun again to show the beauty of its rays. The Lord, by grace, has transformed the nature of my affliction, without taking into account what my works deserve. Far from me such a thought! He has not acted thus toward me and He will not do so! But it is constantly that He mingles His sweetness with my bitterness. And this is because we must not receive from Him (His gifts) and rejoice (in them) without also drinking of this (bitter draught) and suffering, and without ceasing to seek a continual rest.
ܕܚܟܝܡܐ ܗܝ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܝܕܥ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܡܪܗ. ܒܪܡ ܝܚܝܕܝܐ ܝܚܝܕܐܝܬ ܢܥܦܩ ܠܕܝܠܗ. ܐܦܢ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܫܥܬܐ ܢܬܦܠܗܕ ܡܢ ܓܕܫ̈ܐ ܐܝܬ ܐܡܬܝ ܒܥܠܡܐ ܕܓܕܫ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܝܢ ܐܚܝ ܆ ܢܙܕܗܪ ܒܥܝܢ̈ܝܢ ܘܒܐܕܢ̈ܝܢ ܘܒܟܪ̈ܣܢ ܘܒܪ̈ܓܬܢ ܘܒܚܡܬܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܗܢܝܢ ܓܠܙܢ ܠܢ ܡܢ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ. ܘܝܬܝܪ ܢܒܠܘܡ ܠܠܫܢܢ ܟܠܒܐ ܦܩܪ̈ܐ ܆ ܗܢܐ ܡܗܦܟ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܫ̈ܠܕܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܀ ܕ ܫܐܘ̈ܠܐ ܘܦܘܢܝ ܦܬܓܡ̈ܐ ܒܝܬ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܠܐ ܬܒܥܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܣܒܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܘܬܪ̈ܢܐ. ܡܛܠ ܕܐܘ ܠܢ ܡܩܪ̈ܫܝܢ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܬܚܢ ܕܙ ܆ ܐܘ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܒܚܣܡܐ ܡܬܕܥܪ̈ܝܢ. ܐܠܐ ܢܐܚܘܕ ܠܫܢܢ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܆ ܘܠܒܢ ܢܬܬܙܝܥ ܒܐܘܡܢܘܬܢ ܪܚܝܡܬ̇ ܥܠ ܐܒܐ ܇ ܡܢ ܗܝ ܕܒܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܬܘܒ ܒܐܘܪܝܫܠܡ ܢܡܘܣܐܝܬ ܡܫܬܡܠܝܐ ܆ ܠܝܬ ܠܢ ܕܢܣܦܩ ܠܗܕܐ ܆ ܐܠܐ ܒܠܡܐܢܗ ܢܝܚܐ ܕܫܬܩܐ ܡܥܕܐ ܠܩܢܝܘ̈ܗܝ ܀ ܟܓ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܬܠܬ ܐ
2 The duty of the wise man is only to know the will of his Lord. But the solitary, let him embrace, as if they were but one, Him who is his own, even though it happens to him at times to be distracted by the fact of events. We are, my Brothers, in the world of events; let us guard ourselves from our eyes, our ears, our belly, our desire, our irascibility, for (all) that deprives us of the holy light. And above all, let us muzzle the furious bitch that is our tongue: it changes the living into stinking corpses. 3 Do not seek the exchange of questions and answers in a large assembly, even if these seem profitable; for either they will cool our fervor, or others will be bitten by envy. Let us rather hold our tongue in silence, and let it be our heart that is active in the office which is ours and which is dearer to the Father than that which is accomplished according to the law on the mountain or even in Jerusalem. And this is only possible for us in the peaceful harbor of silence which rejoices those who are its masters. 23 TWENTY-THIRD LETTER
ܡܢ ܒܬܪ ܡܐܬܝܬܝ ܕܡܢ ܠܘܬܟ. ܐܙܝܥ ܥܠܝ ܐܪ̈ܟܘܢܐ ܣܝ̈ܥܬܗ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܥܘܠܐ. ܥܡ ܡܫܬܡ̈ܥܢܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢܐ ܕܝܠܗ. ܘܐܠܘ ܕܚܢܢܗ ܕܨܒܐ
1 After my return from thee, the Prince (of demons) set in motion against me all his impious troops, with those who obey him


[p. 380] {#po.39.p380}
ܒܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܟܠ ܆ ܠܐ ܐܫܬܦܥ ܥܠ ܕܘܝܘܬܝ ܆ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܟܝܠ ܡܢ ܟܕܘ ܒܬܪܥ ܛܘܠ̈ܩܐ ܩܝܡܐ ܗܘܬ. ܘܟܕ ܬܩܦܘ ܡܥܫ̈ܘܠܐ ܡܢ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܠܐ ܡܛܟܣܐܝܬ ܆ ܨܡܚ ܥܠܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܨܠܘܬܟ. ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܟܡܝܪܐ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܗܘܝܬ ܒܗ. ܘܒܝܕ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܕܒܠܫܢܟ ܦܐܪܩܠܝܛܐ ܠܝ ܡܠܠ ܆ ܢܓܕܬ ܕܐܥܠܬܢܝ ܠܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܚܣܝܟ ܡܢ ܕܕ̈ܓܠܐ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܀
(as) his vicars; and if the clemency of Him who wills that all should live had not spread over my misery, my soul would have found itself from that moment at the gate of annihilation. (But) when the stumbling blocks (placed) pell-mell by these two (kinds of) spirits became insurmountable, the light of thy prayer shone upon me in the sad place where I was. Through the words that were on thy tongue the Paraclete spoke to me; thou didst draw me and introduce me into the place that cannot be trodden by feet
ܐܣܬܟܠ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܦܓܥܢ ܪ̈ܐܙܢܝܐ. ܘܗܐ ܡܢ ܫܥܬܐ ܗܝ ܆ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܥܠ ܠܐ ܫܘܝܘܬܝ ܬܡܢ ܕܢܢ̈ܚܢ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܡܐܡܪ̈ܐ ܐܢܝܢ ܘܕܠܐ ܬܘܢܝ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܐܦ ܒܙܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܐܚܝܕ ܠܗ̇ ܗܘܢܐ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܡܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܬܫܒܘܩ ܠܟܢܪ̈ܗ̇ ܘܠܘܬܗܝܢ
of strangers. 2 Understand how our mysterious meeting came about. Behold, from that moment on, the things whose light rises upon my unworthiness are unspeakable and impossible to evoke, to the point that the spirit takes great care to restrain the soul, for fear that under the effect of the joy and love of these things, it might abandon its zither to flee toward them!
ܬܥܪܘܩ ܀ ܟܕ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܐ
24 TWENTY-FOURTH LETTER
ܐܩܝܡ ܐܚܝ ܗܘܢܝܟ ܒܠܐ ܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܆ ܘܬܗܪ ܒܚܘܒܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܠܘܬܢ. ܕܟܕ ܟܠܗ̇ ܡܕܒܪܢܘܬܗ ܘܡܐܬܝܬܐ ܕܒܪܗ ܘܡܘܬܗ ܠܦܘܪ̈ܩܢ ܚܫ̈ܝܢ ܣܥܪ ܆ ܘܣܝܡ ܠܢ ܦܘܩ̈ܕܢܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܢܨܛܒܬ ܒܦܫܫܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܢܐܚܐ ܆ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܢ ܘܠܐܝܩܪ̈ܢ. ܘܠܗ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܢܝܐ. ܘܟܕ ܗܟܢܐ ܚܙܝܬ ܠܫܦܝܥܘܬ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܕܛܒܐ ܘܣܓܝܕ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܐܠܗܢ ܆ ܕܟܕ ܢܥܒܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܠܢ ܡܐܚܝܢ ܘܡܩܪ̈ܒܢ ܠܢ ܠܚܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܡܗ ܆ ܐܝܟ ܗܘ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܚܫܒ ܕܥܒܕܝܢܢ ܠܘܬܗ ܕܚܢܢ ܠܚܝ̈ܐ ܢܬܩܪܒ ܀ ܐܘ ܡܐ ܕܬܗܝܪ ܚܢܢܟ ܐܠܗܢ. ܘܡܢ ܕܐܙܓܫ ܒܗ ܆ ܡܫܬܠܗܒ ܘܝܩܕ ܠܒܗ ܒܟܠܥܕܘܢ ܇ ܠܡܦܪܚ ܡܢܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܣܓܝ ܬܘܟ̈ܐ. ܘܝ ܡܢܟ ܣܓܝܓܕܫ̈ܐ ܆ ܛܘܒܘܗܝ ܠܡܢ ܕܣܢܟ. ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܢܐ. ܣܢܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܕܪ̈ܚܝܡܐ. ܘܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܓܥܝܨܝܢ ܠܡܫܡܫ̈ܢܘܗܝ. ܐܘ ܐܒܝ ܘܡܪܒܝܢܝ. ܦܣܘܩ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬ̇ ܚܝ̈ܝ ܡܢܗ. ܟܕ ܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܚܠܝܛܬ̇ ܒܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐ ܕܡܘܢܥܐ ܕܠܘܬܟ ܠܗܕܐ ܬܥܒܕ. ܕܐܦ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܣܡܬܢܝ ܒܗ ܆ ܕܡܢܗ ܠܗܕܐ ܐܬܬܓܪ. ܪܚܡܬܟ ܡܪܝ ܬܥܒܕ ܠܝ ܫܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܥܨܝܢܐ ܆ ܘܠܐ ܚܒ̈ܠܐ ܘܟܐܒ̈ܐ ܢܥܨܘܢܢܝ ܠܡܦܩ ܡܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܒܨܒܝܢܐ ܡܛܠ
1 My Brother, keep thy spirit without movement and marvel at the love of God for us: He who, when He rendered effective all His Economy, as well as the coming of His Son and His death for the salvation of our life, and when He established commandments so that we might be thanks to them embellished by what is good and live, did that to help us and honor us. Was there in that something for Himself? Although thou hast seen the mercy of the All-Good and more Adorable than all, our God, thus spread out, || He will consider, when we have accomplished the things that vivify us and bring us closer to union with Him, that it will be doing Him a favor to approach Life! 2 Oh! how wonderful is Thy clemency, our God! He who has felt it is inflamed, and his heart burns at every moment to fly away from this world full of evils. Woe to thee! Woe to thee, (world) full of uncertain events! And happy is he who hates thee, for he is not hated! Thy enemies (in fact) are the friends of the Beloved and thy friends inspire aversion in His servants. O my Father, O my Nourisher, cut off from him the course of my life, the desire, mingled with confidence, to arrive unto Thee provoking that. For it is precisely to this end that Thou hast placed me there: so that I might gain that from my commerce with him. May thy love to my Lord cause me to be delivered from it willingly, and may it not be


[p. 382] {#po.39.p382}
ܕܠܗ̇ܘ ܕܫܼܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܢܗ ܡܢܗ܆ ܠܡܩ̇ܘܝܘ ܒܗ ܥܿܨܝܢ ܡܠ̈ܐܟܐ ܆ ܥܕ ܢܼܡܛܐ ܙܒܢܗ. ܘܠܕܐܬܐܼܣܪ ܒܗ ܆ ܡܢܗ ܒܬܫܢܝܩܐ ܘܒܐܘ̈ܠܨܢܝܐ ܕܒ̇ܪ̈ܝܢ. ܡܐ ܙܓܝܓ ܘܫܘܢܝ ܪ̈ܰܚܡܺܝܟ ܠܘܬܟ ܫ̇ܦܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܟܡܐ ܥܣܝܩܐ ܡܦܩܬܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܠܟܠ ܕܕܝܠܗ ܡܛܠ ܕܗܿܢܘܢ ܕܠܝܪ̈ܬܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܫܿܢܝܢ ܆ ܘܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܡܬܓܠܙܝܢ ܀ ܟܗ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܚܡ̈ܫ ܥܠ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܢܝܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܕܫܿܐܠܬܢܝ ܕܐܚܝ ܆ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܟܠܗ ܕܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܇ ܟܕ ܒܕܘܟ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܿܝܟ. ܐܟܡܐ ܕܝܗܼܒܬ̇ ܠܝ ܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܡܫܝܚܐ ܀ ܓܿܒܘܠܺܝ ܒܥܘܕܪ̈ܢ ܨܠܘܬܟ ܆ ܫ̣ܡܥ. ܟܕ ܒܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܙܥܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܐ̱ܚܳܘܝܟ ܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܪܐܙܢܝܘܬ ܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܐܚܝܕ ܟܠ ܐܟܡܐ ܕܣܿܦܩܐ ܡܥܝܼܠܘܬ ܡܕܥܝ ܠܐ ܫܘܐ ܠܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ ܀
the pangs of death and the illnesses that constrain me to leave it against my will. For he whose spirit is delivered from it, the angels constrain him to remain there until his time comes; and he who is attached to it, they take him away into torment and afflictions. How desirable is the departure of thy friends toward Thee, O Most Beautiful! But how painful is the exit from the world for all his own! For the former depart toward their inheritance, and the latter are deprived of what is theirs. 25 TWENTY-FIFTH LETTER [On the incomprehensibility of God]
ܐܬܒܿܩܐ ܕܐܚܝ ܟ݁ܰܕ ܚܳܐܖ ܐܢܬ ܣܿܟܘܠܬܢܐܝܬ ܆ ܕܐܝܟܢܐ ܡܠܼܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܟܝܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܗܢܐ ܟܠ ܡܢ ܢܘܪܐ ܇ ܘܟܣܝܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܒܠܐ ܡܥܒܕܢܘܬܐ ܕܡܬܚܙܝܐ. ܟܕ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ. ܘܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܪܐ ܡܬܚܿܘܝܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܿܝܟܐ. ܟܣܝܐ ܢܘܪܐ ܒܒܙܩܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ. ܘܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ. ܘܥܡܗ̇ ܐܦ ܚܝ̈ܠܐ ܡܚܝ̈ܕܝ ܒܟܝܢܗܿ. ܟܕ ܐܢ ܓܝܪ ܢܼܨܒܐ ܐܢܫ ܕܢ̇ܦܩܝܗ̇ ܡܢܗ ܘܐ ܆ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܐܝܬ ܡܬܚܘܝܐ. ܘܢ̇ܿܦܩܐ ܟܕ ܡܩ̇ܘܝܐ ܒܗ ܒܠܐ ܚܿܣܝܪ̈ܘ. ܘܐܢ ܬܬܼܠ ܠܗ̇ ܡܠܘܼܐܐ ܆ ܡܘܩܕܐ ܥܒ̈ܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܘܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܕܒܪ̈ܐ ܘܟܠ ܕܚܿܫܚ ܠܝܩܕܢܐ. ܘܡܬܚ̇ܘܐ ܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܕܟܠܗ ܟܝܢܗܿ ܆ ܒܟܣܝܘܬܗ̇ ܕܒܓܘܫܡܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܘܒܨܝܪ̈ܐ ܥܡ ܙܗܪܿܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܨܡܢܗ̇ ܘܚܿܡܝܡܘܬܗܿ. ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܣܿܝܟܐܝܬ. ܐܠܐ ܫܟܝܼܚܐ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܘܚܿ̇ܝܠܗ̇ ܒܚܝܠܬܢܘܬܐ ܕܣܟܗܿ܆ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܫܟܝܼܚ ܣܟܗܿ. ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܒܨܝܼܪ ܣܟܗܿ. ܟܠܗ̇ ܡܟ̇ܢܫܐܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܡܥܘܝܐ ܬܩܝܼܦܘܬ ܡܥܒܕܢܝܘܬܗܿ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܒܓܘܫ̈ܡܐ ܕܫܝܼ̈ܛܐ ܘܒܨܝܪ̈ܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܘܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܠܢ ܐܬܚܙܝܬ̇ ܕܟܠܗ̇ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܟܕ ܒܕܘܟ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟܐ ܀
2 Consider, my Brother, by looking with sagacity, how all the natures of the universe are filled with fire, and how this is hidden in them without visible operation, while it is everywhere and can everywhere render itself entirely visible without being limited. Fire is hidden in a small pebble where it is invisible; and with it are also found all the powers that are united to its nature. But if someone wants to bring it out, let it show itself a little and it comes out, while remaining without diminution in the (pebble); and if thou feedest it, it sets fire to numerous forests, mountains, steppes, and everything that is combustible: then appear the power of its whole nature and how it is hidden in a small and insignificant body with the flashes of its resplendence and its heat. Not in a limited way, but by being there entirely with its power, in the strength of its totality, without anything limiting this totality. This, in effect, is not diminished; but it shows, by gathering it, all the vigor of its operation, hidden here in vile and insignificant bodies, from which it has appeared to us that it is entirely in every place without being limited by (any) place.


[p. 384] {#po.39.p384}
ܚܲܙܝ ܐܚܝ ܕܡܢ ܒܙܩܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܝܿܠܦ ܐܢܬ ܥܠ ܗܽܘ ܟܝ̇ܢܐ ܫܒܝܼܚܐ. ܕܟܣܐ ܟܠܗ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ ܟܕ ܒܕܘܟ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟ. ܒܗܕܐ ܕܡܼܘܬܐ ܐܬܒܶܩܐ ܐܚܝ ܗܘܢܢܐܝܬ ܒܚ̇ܘܪܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܡܓ̇ܘܝ. ܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܫܩܠ ܟܠ ܕܡܢ ܩܕܡܘܗܝ. ܘܒܢܘܗܪܐ ܡܬܡܿܲܨܥ. ܥܠ ܟܝܢܐ ܫܒܝܚܐ ܘܛܘܒܬܢ ܒܟܠ ܕܒܼܪ̈ܐ ܕܟܠ ܘܒܗ ܥܿܡܪ ܠܐ ܡܣ̈ܝܟܐܝܬ ܟܕ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܟܠܗ ܒܟܠܕܘܟ. ܘܥܠ̈ܡܐ ܟܠܝܗܘܢ ܠܐ ܡܣܿܝܟܝܢ ܠܗ. ܫܼܡܥ ܡܢܐ ܐ̇ܡܪ̈ܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܥܠ ܗ̇ܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܐܡܪ̈ܢܐ. ܗܿܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܥܿܡܪ ܒܩܕܝܫ̈ܘܗܝ. ܘܒܟܠܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܟܠܗ ܒܟܠܗ ܥܡ ܨܡܚ̈ܐ ܕܡܢ ܥܘܼܒܗ ܚܝ̈ܠܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܡܬܡܠ̈ܠܢܐ. ܘܡܿܥܒܕ ܘܡܥܢ̇ܘܐ ܚܿܝܠܗ ܟܠܗ ܬܡܢ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܣܿܬܝܟ. ܐܘ̄ ܡܢܐ ܐܡܪ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܆ ܦܣܼܩܗ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܠܡܕܥܝ ܡܢ ܡܖܪ̈ܕܝܬܗ ܆ ܘܐܬܡܿܚܠܬ̇ ܝܡܝܢܝ ܀ ܘܢܼܦܠ ܩܢܝܝܐ ܥܠ ܕܘܟܬܗ ܇ ܘܬܟܼܣܢܝ ܡܢ ܛܘܝܿܒ ܨܒܝܢܝ. ܟܕ ܪܡܙ ܠܝ ܕܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܫܬܩܐ ܠܚܡ ܀
3 See, my Brother, how thanks to a small pebble thou art instructed about this glorious Nature which is hidden entirely in every place without any place limiting it. Consider, my Brother, this similitude in a spiritual way, with a gaze that surpasses everything and penetrates beyond everything: with the gaze that removes everything that is before it and places itself in the midst of the light. Concerning the glorious and supremely blessed Nature which created everything and dwells therein in an unlimited way, being entirely in every place without the worlds all together limiting it, listen to what the Word says of this unspeakable Nature, namely: "He dwells in His saints." And this in each of them, and totally, with the resplendences that emanate from His bosom and which are His ineffable powers; and there || He puts into action and shows all His power without it being limited therein. Oh! what does He say there! Wonder has interrupted the progress of my intelligence, my hand has weakened, and the pen has fallen on the spot: He has prevented me from doing what my will was preparing to do, by indicating to me with a sign that here it is silence that is appropriate.
ܐܪܟܢ ܠܝ ܛܒܐ ܛܝܒܘܬܟ ܆ ܕܐܡܪ ܥܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܟ ܐܟܡܐ ܕܡܿܦܣ ܐܢܬ ܇ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܐܟܡܐ ܕܝ̇ܗܒ ܐܢܬ ܫܘܘܟܢܐ ܠܚܼܙܘܐ ܡܕܥܝܐ. ܕܗܐ ܕܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܕܘܡܪܐ ܥܡ ܬܼܡܗܐ ܠܐ ܫܿܒܩ ܕܢܦܼܘܩ ܠܟܠܡ
4 Incline toward me Thy grace, O Most Good, so that I may speak of Thy Greatness in the measure that Thou permittest, (even though it be) not to the measure of the gift that Thou makest to the intellectual vision; for behold, the wonder and admiration that accompanies stupor do not permit it to go out into the world of shadow. Glory to Thee who, although
ܘܡܢ ܟܠ ܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟ ܐܢܬ ܥܿܡܪ ܐܢܬ ܒܛܝܒܘܬܟ ܒܡܠܝܼܠܐ ܪܲܚܡܿܟܪ ܀ ܘܒܗ ܡܥ̇ܘܐ ܐܢܬ ܫܘܒܰܚܟܪ ܟܠܗ. ܘܚܺܝܠܟ ܟܠܗ. ܘܝܕܥܬܟ ܟܠܗ̇ܿ. ܘܠܐ ܡܣܿܬܝܟ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܘܒܟܠ. ܠܐ ܒܟܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܫܘܒܚܟ: ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܟ. ܘܠܐ ܚܰܝܠܟ ܕܐܚܼܝܕ ܟܠ ܘܡܐܚܐ ܟܠ. ܟܕ ܦܐܝܘܬ ܫܘܼܒܚܟ ܒܬܡܼܗܐ ܡܩܝܼܡܐ ܥܠܡܢ ܘܩܘܕܫܟ ܆ ܘܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ ܡܫܬܩܐ ܙܘܥ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܇܀ ܘܠܗܘܢ ܥܡܗ̇ ܡܢܢ̇ܝܕܐ ܀
being totally in every place, containest everything and art contained by nothing! Thou dwellest by Thy grace in the rational being, Thy friend, and Thou showest in him all Thy glory, all Thy power, and all Thy knowledge; but neither Thy Nature, nor Thy glory, nor Thy knowledge, nor Thy power which dominates everything and vivifies everything, are limited by anything and in anything, when the beauty of Thy glory makes the worlds of Thy Holiness stand in stupor, (while) in drunkenness it reduces their movements to silence and unites them to itself.
ܐܟܡܐ ܕܝ̇ܗܒ ܠܟ ܪܡܙܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܠܡ̇ܕܪ̈ܟܘ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܗܝ ܆ ܠܪܛܡܐܐ ܟܠܗ ܘܠܐ ܐܦܼܣ ܬܘܒ ܠܝ ܠܡܥܒܪ ܆ ܟܕ ܬܟ݂ܣܢܝ ܡܢ ܕܠܡܿܡܪܚܘ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܨܒܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܬ. ܐܠܐ ܐܦ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܕܬܼܐܠܦ ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡܠ̈ܠܢ ܀
5 Just as it is now for Him to make thee a sign secretly so that thou mayest understand, He no longer permitted me, I who am entirely impure, to go further, preventing me from having the temerity to do what I wanted. However, even through these things, it is possible for thee to learn those that are unspeakable.
ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܫܬ
26 TWENTY-SIXTH LETTER


[p. 386] {#po.39.p386}
ܕܫܐܠܬ ܡܢܝ ܕܐܽܘܕܥܟ ܥܠ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܟ ܆ ܒܗܕܐ ܛܒ ܒ̇ܗܬ ܐܢܐ. ܐܦܢ ܐܡܝܼܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܒ̇ܿܗܬܝܢ. ܒܪܡ ܕܝܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܪ̈ܐ݁ܓ ܐܢܬ. ܐܝܬ ܠܗܝܢ ܕܢܬܓ̈ܠܝܢ ܆ ܘܠܘ ܠܙܒܢܐ ܡܬܝܼܚܐ. ܐܢ ܐܬܪܐ ܢܫܬܿܦܐ ܆ ܣܓܝ ܡܛܪ̈ܐ ܫܿܬܝܢ ܐܝܠ̈ܢܐ ܇ ܐܠܐ ܦܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܙܒܢܗ ܗ̇ܘܐ. ܘܐܟܪܐ ܒܐܡܢܸܼܢܘ ܡܣܿܘܚ ܠܫܘܡܠܝ ܙܪܥܗ. ܒܪܡ ܟܘܢܫ ܥܠܠܬܐ ܝܕܝܼܥ ܗܘ ܙܒܢܗ ܇ ܘܐܒܐ ܠܒܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܒܝܘܡܐ ܒ̇ܥܐ ܕܩܿܘܡܬܐ ܡܫ̈ܡܠܝܬܐ ܐܢ ܡܫ̣ܟܚܝܚܐ ܗܘܬܿ. ܐܠܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܒܿܠܒܠ ܛܟܣܐ ܟܝܢܝܐ ܒܣܘܘܚܐ ܀ ܡܕܝܢ ܩ̇ܕܡ ܠܟ ܩܝܛܐ ܘܬܐܟܘܼܠ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܫܡܝ̈ܢܐ. ܥܿܒܪ ܫܘܠܡܐ ܕ ܣܬܘܝܐ ܆ ܘܬܚܼܙܐ ܠܐܝܠܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܗܒܒ ܒܡܨܥܬܿ ܦܪܕܝܣܟ. ܕܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܬܐܟܘܼܠ ܆ ܠܥܠܡ ܠܐ ܡܐܬ ܐܢܬ ܡܛܠ ܕܙܒܢ̈ܬܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܬܐ ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܕ݁ܫ̇ܪܺܝ ܦܿܩܚ. ܘܚܿܒܛ ܠܗ ܒܪ̈ܕܐ. ܡܫ̈ܒܐ ܠܐ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܫܿܡܥ
1 That thou hast asked me to inform thee about what is thine, that is what truly makes me ashamed, even though it is said: "The ashamed have (no longer) shame." Be that as it may, the things that thou desirest will certainly be revealed (to thee), and before long. If a place is leveled, the trees absorb much rain, but the branches only come in their time. The cultivator is always in a hurry for what he has planted to reach maturity; however, the time to gather the harvest has been fixed. The father, too, would like his sons, if it were possible, to finish growing in a day; but the order of nature is not disturbed by (his) impatient desire. 2 Thus, therefore, let summer precede thee, and thou shalt eat ripe fruits; let winter end, and thou shalt see the Tree of life flower in the midst of thy paradise and when thou hast eaten of it, thou shalt never die. For I often see it begin to flower, then the cold strikes it, (while) I hear winds moving that are outside of nature.
ܐܢܐ ܕܡܬܬܙܝܼܥܝܢ ܀ ܐܢ ܢܒ̣ܥܝܘܗܝ ܠܡ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܡܬܓܠܐ ܠܢ. ܚܼܙܝ ܗ̇ܝ ܕܐܢ ܡܬܓܠܐ. ܡܕܝܢ ܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܡܢ̇ܦܝ. ܓܠܝ ܬܚܦܼܝܬܐ ܕܢܬܚܿܘܐ. ܐܪ̈ܝܹܝܡ ܘܝܼܠܐ ܕܢܬܚܼܙܐ. ܐܬܪܐ ܗܼܘ ܒ̇ܥܐ ܕܢܫܼܟܚ ܠܗ ܕܢܚ̇ܘܐ ܫܘܼܦܪܗ. ܣܓܝ ܡܧܣܿܘܚ ܕܢܬܚܼܙܐ. ܒܣܘܘܚܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܡܫ̇ܬܦܐ. ܒܣܘܟܝܐ ܩܝܛܐ ܕܚ̈ܕܘܬܐ ܐܬܐ ܠܢ ܘܣܟܐ ܆ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܒܙܒܢܗܝܢ ܐܬܝ̈ܢ. ܒܩܠܝܠ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܟ ܬܪ̈ܒܝܬ̇ ܟܫ̇ܢܐ ܆ ܐܦ ܒܢ̈ܝ ܐܠܗܐ ܕܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܘܠܢ ܢܫܘܬܦ ܒܝܖ̈ܬܘܬ ܚܰܒܝܒܗ ܐܡܝܢ ܕܐ ܀
3 Certainly, if we seek God, He will reveal Himself to us. But pay attention to this: if He reveals Himself, it is therefore that He is inside and that He is hidden. Remove what hides Him so that He may appear; take away the veil so that He may render Himself visible. He seeks, He, a place where He can show His beauty: lively is His eagerness to be seen. It is by (our) eagerness (of our own) that the place is leveled; it is with expectation that the summer of joy comes to us. In short, all things come in their time, little by little, following the progress of nature. And it is thus with the sons of God themselves. To Him be glory, and may He, || associate us with the inheritance of His Beloved. Amen.
ܟܙ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܫܒ̈ܥ
27 TWENTY-SEVENTH LETTER
ܐܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܐܒܐ ܒܡܫܝܚܗ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܠܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܪܘܚܐ ܚܳܙܐ ܐܢܐ. ܐܦܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܠܒܪ ܡܢܗ ܩ̇ܘܡܐ ܘܙܘܥܐ ܘܚܝ̈ܐ
1 I know the Father in His Christ, and the Son I see Him through the Spirit. There is for me outside of Him neither stability, nor movement, nor life, nor


[p. 388] {#po.39.p388}
ܘܪ̈ܓܫܬܐ. ܘܟܕ ܡܬܒܼܠܥ ܐܢܐ ܒܬܗܖ̈ܐ ܆ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܚܕ ܠܡܦܐܕܐ ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܘܒܕܡܘܬܗ ܡܨܛܡܚ ܐܢܐ. ܘܡܥܝܠ ܐ݈ܢܳܐ ܒܝ ܬ݁ܺܗܪ ܐܢܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܿܙ ܐܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐܝܬ. ܕܒܝ ܫܟܝܼܚ ܠܝ ܢܼܒܥܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ. ܗܢܘ ܣܟܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܠܐܓܫܼܝܡܐ ܀
perception. And when I am absorbed by wonder, I see Them (being) a unique lamp, and like this one I shine. Also, I marvel at myself and rejoice spiritually that in me is found the Source of Life, this Source which is the end of the incorporeal world.
ܦܘܫܩܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܢܼܥܒܕ ܚܿܟܝܡܐ ܠܗܢܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܕܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܗܿܘ ܕܡܥ̇ܟܡ ܠܕܝܠܗ ܒܕܝܠܗ. ܘܓܿܠܐ ܫܘܼܦܪܗ ܠܒܘܼܣܡ ܪ̈ܚܳܡܘܗܝ ܀ ܟܚ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܬܡ̈ܢ
2 The explanation of this, the wise man cannot provide. Glory to Him who makes His own wise by what is His, and reveals His beauty for the delight of those who love Him. 28 TWENTY-EIGHTH LETTER
ܡܢܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܕܐܿܟܬܘܒ ܠܟ ܆ ܐܘ̄ ܚܿܒܝܒ ܠܝ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܇ ܥܒܕܐ ܕܦܠܚܐ. ܗܢܐ ܕܠܝ ܠܐ ܟܬܝܼܒܐ. ܕܒܡܚܙܝܬ̇ ܡ̈ܠܐ ܠܕܟܝ̈ܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ. ܢܬܼܠ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܒܠܒܟ ܠܚܙܬܐ ܗܫܐ ܘܠܥܠܡܝܢ ܆ ܘܬܫܼܢܐ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܗ ܘܬܫܿܬܠܗܒ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܀ ܘܗܪܟܐ ܡܥܝܠ ܐܘ ܕܠܼܐܐ ܘܡܛܪܿܦ ܒܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܡܪܗ ܆ ܣܝܼܡ ܪ̈ܝܼܫܟ ܥܠ ܒܘܪ̈ܟܝ ܡܪܟ ܘܐܬܬܢܼܝܚ. ܐܓ݂ܣ ܥܠ ܚܕܝܗ ܘܣܘܼܩ ܪܘܚ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܕܢܬܚܿܠܛܘܢ ܚܝ̈ܳܐ ܒܓܒܝܼܠܬܟ. ܐܣܼܬܡܟ ܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܗܘܼܝܘ ܦܬܘܪܟ ܘܡܢܗ ܠܐܒܘܗܝ ܐܬܬ̇ܪ̈ܣܐ. ܕܟܐ ܡܚܙܝܬܟ ܘܕܠܐ ܦܘܠܓ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܚܕܢܝܐ ܆ ܬܠܝܬܐܝܬ ܠܟ ܒܗ ܡܬܚܿܘܐ ܕܣܹܝܡ ܗܕܐ ܥܠ ܠܒܟ. ܘܬܪܲܓܫ ܕܚ̇ܺܝ ܗܘ ܕܐܠܗܟ ܀
1 What shall I write to thee, O thou who art dearer to me than everything, O Servant, O Ascetic? What is mine cannot be written so that in the mirror of words || appears to the pure alone. May He give Himself in vision in thy heart, now and forever! May His love make thee mad and may His beauty inflame thee! 2 Thou who art tired and exhausted by the service of thy Lord, lay then now thy head on His knees and rest. Abandon thyself on His breast and breathe in the Spirit of Life, so that Life may mingle with thy being. Lean on Him, for He is thy table and at this one feed thyself on the Father. Purify thy mirror, and without separation the unique Light will show itself to thee in a trine manner. Take this to heart, and thou shalt perceive that thy God is living.
ܟܛ ܕܥܣܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܬܫ̈ܥ ܐ
29 TWENTY-NINTH LETTER
ܨܠܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܟ ܐܘ̄ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܨܿܒܐ ܐܢܬ ܕܢܼܣܒ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܬܦܢܟܐ ܆ ܫܿܬܩ ܒܟ ܠܟܠ ܡܥܒ̈ܕܢܘ ّ ܕܒܟܠ ܀ ܘܛܥܢ ܢܝܼܪܗ ܕܡܪܟ ܒܠܝܒܟ ܘܬܗܪܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܒܗܘܢܟ ܒܐܡܝܢܼܢܘ. ܥܕܡܐ
1 Thou art, O man, the image of God. Dost thou want the image to take the likeness of the Model? Silence in thee every operation, whatever


[p. 390] {#po.39.p390}
ܕܢܙܕܗܐ ܒܫܘܒܚܗ ܘܢܫܬܚܠܦ ܠܗ̇ ܘܠܕܡܼܘܬܐ ܆܀ ܘܬܗܼܘܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܐܠܗܐ ܩܼܢܐ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܥܿ̇ܒܘܕܗ ܇ ܒܚܕܝܘܬܐ ܡܿܣܒܗܬܿ̇ ܒܗ ܀ ܘܙ ܢܘܪܗ ܕܡܪܟ ܙ ܆ ܘܗܼܘ ܫܿܬܩ ܐܐ ܒܟ ܓܘܙܠܗ̇ ܕܢ̇ܠܦܟ ܢܟܝܢܐ ܕܐ
may be the object; carry continually in thy heart the yoke of thy Lord and in thy spirit the wonder before His Greatness, until this one shines with His glory and is transformed into the Likeness and until thou becomest in God a god having acquired the likeness of
ܕܦܘܠܗܕܐ. ܕܡܐ ܕܢܸܫܪ ܒܟ ܗܢܐ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܆ ܗܝܕܝܢ ܢܼܕܠܩ ܒܟ ܫܘܠܗܒܗܿ :ܙ ܘܢ̣ܚܡ ܠܒܟ. ܘܒܣܪܟ ܢܐܩܕ ܒܥܘܙܗ̇. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܫܘܼܢܝܐ ܥܼܒܕܬ̇. ܐܠܐ ܫܬܩܐ ܠܸܒܟܬ̇ ܠܬܘܪ̈ܨܐ ܀ ܠ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܐ ܩܰܪܺܝܬ݁ ܟܬܝܼܒܬܟ ܘܐܬܡܿܠܝܬ ܚܫܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܼܬܝܒܪ̈ܢܐ. ܘܗܼܘܐ ܕ
His Creator by the union that assimilates to Him. 2 As for the fire of thy Lord, He Himself has calmed its burning in thee in order to teach thee the damage that dispersion causes. But when the consciousness of this has been strengthened in thee, then His flames will burn in thee, thy heart will be incandescent, and thy flesh will be consumed by its ardor. For He has not left, but He stands quiet with a view to forming (thee). 30 THIRTIETH LETTER
ܠܗ ܦܓܖ̈ܝ ܟܠܗ ܥܡ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܡ̇ܙܓܝܢ ܒܚܫܐ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܀ ܠܐ ܚܫ̇ܬ ܚ̇ܫܘܫܐܝܬ ܒܚܝܠ ܐܠܗܝ ܘܡܗܝܡܢ ܐܢܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܪܣܣܐ ܀ ܕܡܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܕܝܠܝ ܝ ܐܫܩܐ ܠܐܫܟܪܗ ܕܡܪܝ ܆ ܘܢܬܚܼܙܐ ܒܗ̇ ܝ ܙܪ̈ܪܥ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕ̈ܓܝܓܐ ܘܗܕܝܪ̈ܐ. ܘܒܐܡܝܢܼܢܘ ܐܥܒܕ ܡܢܗ ܡ̈ܠܐܐ ܆ ܘܒܗܘܢ ܒܥܠ̈ܕܒܒܐ ܢܬܚ̇ܢܩܘܢ ܀ ܘܡܟܝܠ ܚ̇ܝ ܗܘ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ. ܕܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܢܦܘܼܩ ܠܟ ܝܘܡܐ ܫܠܡܐ ܣܦܝܼܩ
1 I have read thy letter and I have been filled with an unbearable suffering. With my soul, it is my whole body that has been penetrated by the suffering of a little time. 2 By the strength of my God, I have not suffered in a passive way but I have faith that thanks to the sprinkling coming from my heaven I will irrigate the field of my Lord, and that there will rise the seed of the desirable and splendid Life. And, always, I will make of it a surging of waves in which the enemies will be drowned.
ܡܢ ܐܠܗܗ. ܫܿܡܥ ܠܬܒܝܪ̈ܝ ܠܒܐ. ܠܝ ܡܟܝܠ ܬܐܢܚ̈ܬܐ ܢܣܓܝ̈ܢ ܆ ܘܠܟ ܒܘ̈ܝܐܐ ܙ :ܙ ܡܫ̈ܚܠܦܐ ܇ ܒܝܼܠܙܕܐ ܪ̈ܚܝܼܡܐ ܕܡܬܬܨܝܼܪ ܦܘܠܘܣܐܝܬ ܀ ܠܐ
3 Long live the Lord! From now on thou wilt no longer pass a whole day that is empty of His God who hears those who have a broken heart. And then, may the groans increase for me, and for thee all sorts of consolations in the beloved Child who is formed in a Pauline manner.


[p. 392] {#po.39.p392}
ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܚܕܐ ܐ ܐ ܕܒܟܠܙܒܢ ܐܲܓܢܲܓܟ ܒܡܠܬܝ ܫܛܘܖܪܬܐ ܐܘ̄ܐ ܥܼܒܕܐ ܦ̇ܠܚܐ ܠܐ
31 THIRTY-FIRST LETTER
ܡܐܢܐ ܠܝ ܕ ܪܚܡ ܠܡܪܟ ܀ ܒܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ. ܘܗܟܢܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢܦܫܟ ܟܠܗ̇ ܬܨܛܒܥ ܒܗܢܝܐܘܬܗ ܆ ܘܬܼܛܥܐ ܝܿܬܗ̇ ܇ ܘܬܼܕܥ ܗ݈ܝ ܠܗ̇ ܝܼܠܕܗ ܕܐܒܐ ܀ ܐܸܡܪ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܚܕ ܒܢ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܟ ' ܡܥܢ̇ܝܕܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܕܢܗܼܘܘܢ ܥܡܗ ّ ܚܕ ܒܐܒܘܗܝ ܟܕܓܝܪ ܠܪܚܡܬܟ ܕܒܪ̈ܚ̈ܲܡܝܟ ܘܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܒܟ ܥُܗܕ ܐܢܐ ܐܘ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܟܠ ܥܠܡ ܆ ܗܕܡ̈ܝ ܦܓܪ̈ܝ ܡܬܦ̈ܣܩܝܢ ܡܢ ܥܘܼܫܢܗ̇ܿ. ܘܡܪܿܕܝܬ̇ ܚܘܩ̈ܐ ܒ̇ܿܛܠܐ. ܐܝܢ ܒܟ ܀ ܡܪܝ ܠܕܝܠܟ ܕܝܠܟ ܡܡܿܠܠ ܐܢܐ. ܘܕܠܐ ܥܕܠܝ ܠܝ ܬܬܚܼܫܒ ܀ ܐܘ̄ ܥܼܒܕܐ ܕܚܿܠܦ ܥܒܕܘܬܗ ܒܒܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܠܡܪܐ ܥܠ̈ܡܐ & ܚܘܨ ܢܦܫܟ ܩܰܠܝܠ ܘܙ ܕܩܼܪܒ ܙ ܙܐ ܪܡܫܐ ܕܫܼܘܡܠܝ ܦܿܥܠܘܬܟ. ܐܨܕ ܐܢܬ ܒܟ ܩܝܠ ܘܙ ܕܢܼܕܢܚ ܒܓ ܨܡܚܐ ܡܢܢܿܕܝܢܐ. ܘܗܕܐ ܝܬܝܪ̈ ܐ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܬܦܼܠ ܥܠ ܐ ܡܚ ܠܝܬ ܙܒܢܐ ܠܚܙܬܐ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܟܠ ܐܝܟ ܗܢܐ. ܗܪܟܐ ܡܬܓܠܐ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܘܡ ܒܣܡ ܀ ܕܚܿܙܐ ܝܥܢܐܝܬ ܠܐ ܣܿܒܥ. ܘܕܒ̇ܿܥܐ ܠܡܖܪܗ ܠܒܪ ܡܢܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܗܢܐ. ܢܦܫܐ ܕܒܗ̇ :ܙ ܡܪܝܚܐ ܠܒܪ ܡܪ̈ܗ̇ ܆ ܢܒܗܐ :ܐ ܕܬܫܒܘܚܬܗܿ :ܐܙ ܒܬ ܗܪܐ :ܐ ܕܫܘܦܪܗ :ܙ ܡܫ̇ܬܬܩ ܒܠܚܘܕ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܐ ܕܐܫܼܬܝܬ̇ :ܙ :ܐ ܡܛܓܐ ܕܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܘܐܬܡ̇ܛܓܬ̇ܿ ܗܝ ܒܗ ܘܗܼܘ ܒܗܿ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܙܝܐ ܕܐܡܝܼܢܐܝܬ ܣ̈̇ܫܝܩܐ ܠܗ ܠܪܝܼܚܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܘܡܗ̇ ܒܣܝܡܐ. ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܩ̇ܥܝܐ ܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܗܬܬܐ. ܕܡܥܘ ܡܫܼܟܚ ܕܡܒܟ ܢܦܪܫܢܝ ܠܗܕܐ ܡܚ̈ܘܐ ܡܫܡܫ̈ܢܘܗܝ ܘܠܥܙܝܢ ܥܡܗ̇ ;ܐ ܒܓܘܙ̈ܓܐ ܀ ܐܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܬ ܕܫܼܠܝ ܠܟ. ܠܐ ܬܫܢܩܼܢܝ ܒܕܐܝܟ ܗܠܝܢ. ܘܐܦ
1 I will not tire, O Servant who givest thyself to asceticism, of stimulating thee at every moment by my inept words (and of saying to thee): "I pray thee, love thy Lord!". And it will be so until thy soul bathes entirely in His suavity, and until it forgets itself and knows itself to be a child of the Father. 2 (Thy Lord) said: That they may be one in us. Glory to Thee, O Unifier of those who love Thee so that they may be with Him one in His Father! When I remember the presence of thy love in those who love Thee and theirs in Thee, O Life of each of the worlds, the members of my body disjoin under the effect of its vehemence, and the course of the pen is interrupted. Truly, my Lord, it is through Thee that I say what is Thine to what is Thine: do not hold it against me! 3 O servant who hast exchanged thy servitude for the condition of son of the Lord of the worlds, strengthen thy soul for a moment, for the evening approaches when thy work will end. Fix for a time thy eyes within thyself so that the Resplendence source of joy may appear in thee; and this, especially when thou throwest thy face against the earth, no moment being like that one to see Him who sees everything: He reveals Himself then to those who love Him and He makes their delights. 4 Whoever looks without restraint is not satisfied, and whoever seeks his Lord outside of himself does not enjoy Him. (But) the soul that in itself escorts solemnly the Son of its Lord, the impulse of its praise is only reduced to silence by the wonder before His beauty: that is the soul that has drunk the mixture of His sweetness and has been mingled with Him, and He with it; that breathes continually the exquisite odor of its Beloved who cries out without being confused: Who will be able to separate me from Thee? To this soul He makes see His Servants, and these incite it to murmur with them (their "Sanctus").
ܐܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܟ ܫܿܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܀ ܐܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܟ ܫܿܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܀
5 But thou sayest to me: "Be silent and do not torment me with this kind of thing!". Also, in accordance with thy words, I am silent.


[p. 394] {#po.39.p394}
ܠܒ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܥܠ ܢܣܝܘ̈ܢܐ ܕܐܕܪ̈ܟܘܗܝ ܘܐܠܝܨܘܬܿ ܢܦܫܐ ܀
32 THIRTY-SECOND LETTER [On trials by which he was afflicted, and on the anguish that seizes the soul]
ܐ ܒܥܐ ܐܢܐ ܡܢܟ ܥܕܪ̈ܝܢܝ ܒܨܠܘܬܐ. ܥܿܬܕܘ ܠܝ ܫܺܐܕܐ ܢܣܝܘܢܐ ܆ ܕܫܼܡܥܗ ܡܣܲܪܲܕ. ܘܠܐ ܙܿܕܩ ܕܢܬܟܬܒ. ܕܠܐ ܢܬܬܙܝܼܥ ܠܒܐ ܕܫܡ̈ܘܥܐ ܆ ܘܗܿܢܘܢ ܫܦ̈ܠܐ ܢܬܚܿܝܠܘܢ. ܚܸܙܘܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܓܥ̈ܝܼܨܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܐܝܬ ܠܝ ܡܬܚܿܘܝܢ. ܘܠܥܝܢ̈ܝ ܕܝܠܝ ܡܣܟܼܢܐ ܨ̇ܒܝܢ ܕܢܣܿܝܛܘܢ ܘܢܚܿܪ̈ܟܘܢ ܒܢܘܪ̈ܢܘܬܗܘܢ. ܘܟܕ ܡܙܕܥܦ ܐܢܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܒܚܝܠ ܡܪܢ ܡܣܬܚܦܝܢ. ܘܬܘܒ ܒܓܠܝܙܘܬ ܗܘܢ ܗܿܦܟܝܢ ܙ ܘܡܚ̇ܘܝܢ ܚܪܡܘܬܗܘܢ ܠܘܼܩܒܠܝ. ܘܓ̇ܝܙܡܢܢ. ܘܟܕ ܢܚܼܫܐ ܕܨܠܝܼܒܗ ܕܚܝ̈ܝܢ ܥܿܒܕ ܐܢܐ ܠܘܩܒܠܗܘܢ ܆ ܠܚܫܘܟܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܗܿܦܟܝܢ ܙ ܘܢܘ̈ܪܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܥܿܟܐ. ܥܼܗܕ ܗܠܝܢ ܐܓܢܒܪܐ ܡܪ̈ܝ ܐܢܛܘܢܝܘܣ. ܒܪܡ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܗ̇ܢܝܢ ܙ ܘܢܘܪ̈ܢܝܢ ܕܥܡܗ ܡܛܠ ܟܘܼܫܪ̈ܗ. ܘܥܡܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܡܚܝܼܠܘܬܝ ܘܫܦܠܘܬܝ. ܘܡܛܠ ܚܛܗ̈ܝ ܕܠܐ ܣܟ ܀ ܚܙܝ ܕܠܡܐ ܐܢܫ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܢܚܼܙܐ ܐܘ ܢܫܼܡܥ ܣܛܪ ܡܢܟ ܘܡܢ ܚܒܝܼܒܟ. ܘܚܫܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܬ̇ܣܓܘܢ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܨܠܘ ܠܡܪܟܘܢ ܕܢܬܼܠ ܠܝ ܚܺܝܠܐ. ܥܠ ܡܪܖܝܐ ܢܸܫܕܐ ܨܦܬܢ ܘܒܗ ܢܚܿܡܣܢ. ܕܣܓܝ̈ܐܢ ܘܥܙܝ̈ܙܐܢ ܘܡܪܝܪ̈ܢ ܐܢܝܢ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܛܥ̣ܝܢ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܡܢܝ ܒܐܡܝܼܢܘ ܀
1 I beg you, help me with prayer! The demons have devised for me a trial the hearing of which would terrify anyone who heard of it, and which it is not fitting to write down, for fear that the hearts of the listeners might be shaken and that these vile beings might be made stronger by it. Their repulsive forms appear to me in a bodily manner, and they want, poor wretch that I am, to burn my eyes and consume them with their fire. And when, by the strength of Our Lord, I repel them with wrath, they are put to flight; but it is only to return afterwards with (as much) audacity, manifesting their wickedness toward me and threatening me with it. (However), when I make the sign of the cross of our Life before them, they return to the darkness that is their lot, and their fire disappears. Remember what (happened) to the valiant Mar Anthony, even though it was something more spiritual than this, and that the beings of fire who were with him were there because of his zeal, whereas (these) are with me because of my weaknesses and my baseness, and because of my innumerable sins. 2 See to it that no one other than you and the one who is dear to you sees or hears these things. And do not suffer overmuch because of them, but only pray to your Lord to give me strength. Let us cast our care upon the Lord and in Him let us hold fast! I am continually giving Him things to bear that are more numerous, more violent, and more bitter than these!
ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܬܠܬ ܐ ܐ ܒܗ ܒܢܝܼܫܐ ܕܢܣܝܘ̈ܢܐ ܡܢ ܡܹܐܙܠܬܟ ܕܡܢ ܨܝܕܝ. ܟܕ ܫܘܡ̈ܬܝ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܢ ܆ ܢܦܫܝ ܒܐܡܝܼܢܘ ܡܣܿܟܝܐ ܠܫܘܢܝܐ. ܘܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܘܡܛܠ ܕܚܛܗ̈ܝ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܝܢ ܆ ܣܼܓܝܘ ܐܦ ܢܣܝܘ̈ܢܝ ܘܥܼܒܪܘ ܠܪ̈ܝܫܝ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܟܕ ܕܡܝ̱ܟ ܐܢܐ
33 THIRTY-THIRD LETTER [Also on the occasion of temptations] 1 Since your departure from me, my wounds are so numerous that my soul is continually expecting (to have) to depart: I do not know how this comes to be. Because I have accumulated sins, my trials, too, have accumulated until they are above my head; in such a way


[p. 396] {#po.39.p396}
ܘܟܕ ܥܝܼܪ ܐܢܐ. ܗܟܢ ܡܟ̇ܢܫܝܢ ܘܡܿܝܬܝܢ ܥܠܝ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܙ ܬܢܝ̈ܢܐ ܘܥܩܪ̈ܒܐ܆ ܕܠܡܥܝ̇ܢܗܘܢ ܠܝܬ ܣܟܐ. ܣܛܪ ܡܢ ܫ̈ܢܕܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܘܓܠ̈ܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܥܢܿܝܢ ܕܒܩܢܘܡܗܘܢ ܡܫܬܿܢܕܝܢ ܒܝ. ܘܠܢܦܫܝ ܡܥܢ̇ܝܒܬܐ ܘܒܡܨܥܬܿ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܝܬ ܣܒܪ̈ܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܪܡܙܗ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܒܿܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܐ ܫܦܝܼܥܘܬܗ ܕܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܡ̇ܠܠܐ ܀
that, whether I am asleep or awake, the Rebels gather and direct against me dragons and scorpions in infinite number, in addition to the innumerable tortures, interior and exterior, that they inflict upon me themselves. And my condemned soul has no other hope, in the midst of these things, than the hidden sign that the Creator makes to it. (But) it is there that the outpouring of His ineffable grace is known.
ܐܦܝܼܣ ܐܚܝ ܠܡܪܢ ܆ ܕܢܬܼܠ ܠܝ ܚ̇ܝܠܐ ܠܡܣܿܝܒܪܘ ܟܐܒ̈ܐ ܕܛܿܝܒܘ ܠܝ ܚܛܗ̈ܝ. ܘܣܛܪ ܡܢܟ ܐܢܫ ܠܐ ܢܚܼܙܐ ܠܗܠܝܢ ܙ ܕܠܐ ܒܬܚܿܡܬ ܥܠܝ ܢ̇ܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܝ ܘܢܬܪ̈ܚܼܩ ܡܢܝ ܆ ܘܐܡܘܬ ܡܢ ܐܝܺܕܝ ܣ̈ܢܐܝ ܒܝܼܫܐܝܬ. ܠܐ ܬܼܚܫ ܣܓܝ ܡܛܠ ܕܝܡܝܢܗ ܕܡܪܝܐ ܣܿܡܟܐ ܘܥܿܒܕܐ ܚܿܝܠܐ. ܘܡܒ̇ܝܐܐ ܒܡܨܥܬܿ ܗܠܝܢ ܀
2 My Brother, beseech Our Lord to give me the strength to endure the sufferings that my sins have prepared for me. Let no one other than you see this, for fear that the Guardian of my life might become angry with me and depart from me, and that I might die cruelly at the hand of my enemies. Do not have too much sorrow, for the right hand of the Lord sustains, raises up strength, and consoles in the midst of (all) this.
ܠܕ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܝ
34 THIRTY-FOURTH LETTER
ܥܠ ܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܡܶܢ ܕܨ̈ܒܐ ܕܢ̣ܐܡܪ ܆ ܡܟܪܙ ܠܐ ܝܼܕܥܬܗ. ܒܕ ܠܐ ܐܙܪ̈ܓ݂ܫ ܒܠܐ ܡܬܡܨܝܢܘܬܐ ܠܓܡܪ̈ܐ. ܬܗ̄ܖܪ ܐܢܐ ܒܚܟܡܬܗ ܕ ܕ݁ܒ̇ܪ̈ܘܝܢ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܟܕ ܐܣܪ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܒܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܠܐ ܬܦܼܪܲܚ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܫܘ̈ܘܟܢܐ ܕܟܕ ܒܪ̈ܓܬܗ ܡܬܒܿܠܥܐ ܆ ܛܥܝܐ ܘܫܿܒܩܐ ܠܗ ܠܦܓܪܗ̇ ܒܚܿܝܘܬܐ ܠܚܘܕ ܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܼܒܗܐ ܘܪܕܲܓܫܬܐ ܛܘܪܐ ܣܓܝܐܐ. ܘܬܘܒ ܦ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܣܿܥܪ̈ܐ ܒܗ ܙܙ ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܝܠܗ̇ ܐܢܝܢ ܠܚܫܚܬܐ ܕ ܙ ܘܩܘܝܿܡ ܚܝ̈ܘܗܝ. ܘܟܕ ܚܳܙܝܬ ܐܢܐ ܫ̇ܦܠܐ ܘܡܚܝܼܠܐ ܬܚܘܒܘܬܝ ܕܗܟܢ ܪ̈ܚܝܼܩܢ ܐܢܝܢ ܡܠܐ ܘܨܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܠܡܢܚ̇ܘܝܘ ܫܖ̈ܪ̈ܐ ܐܟܡܐ ܕܪܚܝܼܩ ܗܿܘ ܕܩ̈ܐܡ ܥܠ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܙ ܘܚܳܐܪ ܒܫܡܝܐ ܡܢ ܓܫܬܗ ܘܐܚܝܕܘܬܗ܆ ܡܦܝܼܣ ܐܢܐ ܕܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܢܥܠܢܝ ܒܡܬܐ ܘܐ
1 Whoever wishes to speak of the love of God preaches his own ignorance, for he has not perceived that this is totally impossible! I marvel at the wisdom of our Creator, to see how, although He keeps the soul bound to the body so that it does not fly away at the moment of these gifts when it is absorbed by the desire for Him, it nevertheless forgets its body and limits itself to leaving it alive, without movement or perception for a long space of time, then returns to it and operates through it all that depends on it to (ensure for it) what it needs and to make it subsist. And when, miserable and weak, I see that words and images are as far from showing the reality as one who stands on the earth and looks at the sky is far from touching it, I pray that I may no longer be engaged in such a task!
ܕܗܠܝܢ ܐܧ ܠܐ ܢܪܡܐ ܀ ܒܪܡ ܛܘܒܐ ܝܿܗܒ ܐܢܐ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܢܣܝܢܐ ܐ ܕܪ̈ܐܙܐ ܐܠܗܝ̈ܐ ܢܣܒ ܒܩܢܘܡܗ. ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܐܢܝܢ ܚܠ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܒܪ̈ܢ. ܗܶܢܘ ܕܝܢ ܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ
2 Be that as it may, I proclaim blessed the one who has experienced the divine mysteries for himself. There are (then) two things whose


[p. 398] {#po.39.p398}
ܘܚܕܘܬܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ ܩ̇ܛܠܐ ܘܡܡܝܼܬܐ ܠܙܘܥ̈ܝ ܗܘܢܐ ܒܬܡܼܗܐ. ܘܚܕܘܬܐ ܡܥܼܝܪܐ ܘܡܥ̇ܚܡܐ ܠܙܘܥ̈ܘܗܝ ܢܘܪ̈ܢܐ. ܥܠ ܚܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܦ ܠܡ̇ܘܕܥܘ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ̇ ܐܦܠܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܘܐܦ ܣܘܩܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܕܒܗܠܝܢ ܘܥܡ ܗܠܝܢ ܙܚܠܝܼܛ ܘܡܙܝܓ. ܕܡܢܘ ܢ̣ܐܡܪ. ܕܗܢܝܐܘܬܗ ܘܡܫܿܝܢܘܬܗ ܘܒܘܣܡܐ ܕܕܡܢܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܡܿܠܠܝܢ ܕܐܦܠܐ ܙܕ ܒܠܫܢ ܡܠܐܟܐ. ܕ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܡܬܒ̇ܣܡܝܢ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܝܢ. ܐܦܢ ܕܗܘܢܐ ܗܘ ܠܚܘܕ ܡܩ̈ܒܠܢܗܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܓܫܬܐ. ܐܢ ܒܦܓܪ ܘܐܢ ܕܠܐ ܦܓܝܪ ܕ ܠܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܐܢܐ. ܐܡܪ ܙܕ ܝܿܕܘܥܐ.
sweetness cannot be borne: love and joy. Love kills and annihilates by stupor the movements of the spirit, and joy awakens and resurrects its movements of fire. The sweetness (of this), even the Spiritual ones cannot make known its greatness. Likewise, the holy breath that is mingled with them and by which they are penetrated, who will speak of it? Its suavity, the peace it brings, and the delight it causes are unspeakable, even in the language of the angels. The body and the soul (of man) delight together in all of this, although only the spirit is the receptacle of its perception: Whether it was in the body or out of the body, I do not know, says the Gnostic.
ܕܘܝ ܠܝ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܝܘܡ̈ܝ ܒܫܼܡܥܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܡܢ ܦܘ̈ܡܹܐ ܕܡܥ̈ܝܕܐ ܢܦܫܝ ܡܒ̇ܿܣܡ ܐܢܐ. ܟܕ ܡܢ ܛܥܡܬܗܝܢ ܓܠܝܙܐ ܘܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ. ܐܠܗܝ ܘܫܿܟܢ ܠܝ ܕܐܝܟ ܕܡܢ ܕܝܠܝ ܐܟܪܙ ܥܠ ܕܝܠܟ. ܘܠܐ ܐܗܘܐ ܙ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܳܢܐ ܫܡܥ̈ܐ. ܐܿܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܗܠܝܢ ܐܠܘ ܙ ܗܕܡ̈ܝ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܬܿܚܒܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܟܕ ܪܡܼܐ ܥܠ ܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ. ܪܡܐܓ ܗܘܐ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܬܚܼܙܘܩܝ ܢܦܫܐ ܡܢܗ. ܡܢ ܠܐ ܥܘܗܕܢܐ ܕܚܫܚܬܐ ܕܩܘܝ̇ܿܡ ܚܝ̈ܘܗܝ. ܐܠܐ ܢ̇ܬܦ ܠܗ̇ ܡܢ ܕܝܠܗ̇ ܠܘܬ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܗ. ܡܢ ܥܣܩܘܬܐ ܕܝܘܒܿܫ ܗܕܡ̈ܘܗܝ.
3 Woe to me who, every day, delights my soul by hearing these things from the mouth of those to whom they are familiar, while I am deprived and empty of the perception of their savor! My God, grant me to announce what is Yours as coming from what is mine, and not to report only what I hear (from them)! They say (therefore) that in the drunkenness caused by these things, if the members of the body did not wither while it is cast face to the ground, it would remain thus until the soul left it, because it no longer remembers what it needs to stay alive. But He draws it from its own domain toward His own domain because of the excessive dryness of its members.
ܠܗ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܚܡ̈ܫ: ܥܠ ܫܥܬ̇ ܡܘܬܐ. ܕܟܼܬܒܬ ܠܝ ܐܚܝ. ܕܕܠܡܐ ܢܓܕܫ ܠܟ ܚܙܘܼܩܝܐ ܕܫܘܼܠܡܐ. ܘܐܫܟܚ ܐܘܠܨܢܐ. ܐܢܗܼܘ ܕܬܗܼܘܐ ܗܕܐ. ܠܐ ܬܼܚܫ. ܕܐܦ ܗܢܘ ܪܣܘܟܝ ܘܣܘܘܿܚܝ ܕܠܘܬ ܡܪܝ. ܕܒܫܿܥܬܐ ܗ̇ܝ ܠܐ ܢܫܼܬܟܚ ܠܝ ܐܝܠܐ ܘܐܚܕ ܥܝ̈ܢܐ ܣܛܪ ܡܢܗ. ܕܟܕ ܪܡܼܐ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܐܦ̈ܝ. ܒܕ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܒܗ ܠܗܕܐ ܐܿܫܟܚ. ܘܙܒܢܝ̈ܢ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܢ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܐ ܗܝ ܠܝ ܗܕܐ ܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡ̈ܥܕܝܢ. ܘܡܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܥ̈ܝܩܢ.
35 THIRTY-FIFTH LETTER [On the hour of death] 1 You write to me, my Brother: "(The hour of) your final departure may perhaps come, and I will be in affliction." If that happens, do not suffer for it. For such is precisely the object of my expectation and my aspiration before my Lord, that at that hour there be no one other than Him to help me and close my eyes, and that, cast face to the ground, I may arrive there by desire for Him. And very often, it is closer to me than what rejoices or what saddens.


[p. 400] {#po.39.p400}
ܐܝܠܝܢ ܕܝܢ ܕܒܼܥܝܬ ܡܥܢܝ. ܠܡܥܬܕܐ ܠܟ ܒܿܗܬ ܐܢܐ. ܢܬܼܠ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܶܣܒܪ̈ܢܘܬܟ ܕܥܠܝ ܒܥܒܿܕܐ. ܐܠܐ ܥܕܟܝܠ ܠܐ ܦܪ̈ܝܫܢ ܕܝܠܺܝܬܝ ܡܢ ܕܚܙܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܣܼܝܪܪ ܠܐܪ̈ܡܠܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܐܘܪ̈ܝܗ. ܕܐܝܟ ܗܟܢ ܐܝܬܝ ܒܟܠ ܕܡܝܘܬܝ. ܘܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܿܚܬܝܢ ܡܢ ܟܼܦܬ ܫܡܝܐ. ܡܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܢܿܚܬܘܢܢܝ. ܘܐܝܕܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܐܝܬ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܕܒܨܝܼܪ̈ܐ ܡܢ ܗܕܐ ܕܢܼܬܠܘܢ ܠܝ. ܕܐܦ ܗܼܢܘܢ ܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܫܩܝܼܠܝܢ ܒܟܠ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܘܥܡܠܗܘܢ ܗܿܢܘ ܕܢܼܦܠܚܘܢ ܒܝܪ̈ܬܘܬܗܘܢ ܠܟܠ ܕܠܗܘܢ ܫܿܡܥ.
2 As for what you asked me, I am ashamed to write it to you. May the Lord grant (me) to be effectively what you think of me! But until now my distinctive traits do not differ from those of the pig that the widow had tied in front of her manger; that is all I resemble! Also, from where will they have to bring me down, those who lower (others) from the vault of heaven, and what resemblance will they have to attribute to me that is viler than that one, they who deserve this comparison by all their actions? And it is this same task that they will accomplish in (the place of) their inheritance against anyone who listens to them.
ܠܘ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܫ̈ܬ ܐ
36 THIRTY-SIXTH LETTER
ܠܕܚܿܕܬ ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܕܡܼܘܬܐ ܕܐܪܚܗ ܕܒܘܪܣܝܐ. ܐܢ ܥܡ ܫܡܫܟ ܠܠܝܐ ܦܿܓܥ. ܗܼܘܝ ܐܿܠܝܐ. ܘܐܣܓܐ ܬܐܢܚ̈ܬܐ. ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢܼܕܢܚ ܕܒܐܬܪܟ ܫܡܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܚܡ. ܗ̇ܘ ܡܪܟܒ ܙܡܝܪ̈ܬܐ. ܕܗܢ̈ܝܐܢ. ܡ̈ܠܝܢ ܚܕܘܬܐ. ܙܡܥܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܙܡ̇ܪܶܬ ܕܗܢܝܐ ܩܠܗܿ. ܘܪܚܘܼܡܝ ܫܡܥܗ̇ ܘܐܬܬܥܼܝܪ̈ ܡܢ ܫܼܢܬܗ ܘܨܬܗܿ. ܕܒܣܼܡܬ̇ ܠܗ ܡܢ ܟܠ. ܘܦܪܿܕܬܿ ܫܢܬܗ ܘܒܥܼܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܠܥܠܡ ܢܩ̇ܘܐ ܠܘܬܝ. ܘܐܡܪ ܠܝ. ܟܕ ܡܿܦܨܚ ܐܘܪܟ ܙܡܹܝܪ̈ܵܬܟ. ܘܿܫܚ ܩܝܼܢ̈ܬܟ. ܘܦܼܬܚ ܬܪ̈ܥܝܟ. ܘܢܥܘܼܠ ܠܬܘܘ̈ܢܝܟ. ܘܡܟܥܝܠ ܒܟܣܝܐ ܠܚܫ ܘܐܒܼܗܬ ܠܣܩܘܪ̈ܝܟ. ܕܟܕ ܒܙܡܝܪܬܟ ܡܬܩ̇ܦܚܝܢ. ܩ̇ܠܗ̇ ܠܐ ܫܿܡܥܝܢ. ܪܐܙܐ ܠܝ. ܪܐܙܐ ܠܝ ܘܪܐܙܐ ܠܝ ܥܡ ܪ̈ܲܚܡܿܝ. ܕܒܣܿܡܢ ܒܢ. ܕܡܠܟܢ ܒܓܘܢ.
1 He who has renewed himself, to him the likeness given by the tanner's host! 2 If despite (the existence of) your sun the night surprises you, lament and increase your groans until the Sun whose brilliance has no end rises in your place, and He will compose (there) harmonious songs full of joy. I (myself) have sung a melody in a very sweet mode: my Beloved heard it and He awoke from His sleep; He listened to it because it was more pleasing to Him than anything else. Sleep fled from Him and in the eternal vigil || will (henceforth) remain with me. He said to me joyfully: "Prolong your songs, scan your hymns! Open your doors, that we may enter into your dwellings! And from then on, whisper (only) in secret, and confound your enemies who, although slapped by your song, will not hear its voice. My mystery is mine! My mystery is mine! My mystery is mine as well as my friends'! For our delight is within us, since our King is inside us."
ܛܘܒܝܟܝ ܨܗܝܘܢ. ܕܨܗܝܼܬܝ ܘܪܵܘܝܼܬܝ ܒܚܡܖܐ ܕܡܥܥܿܕܐ ܟܠ. ܘܛܥܝܼܬܝ ܠܕܝܠܟܝ. ܘܫܢܝܼܬܝ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬ ܐܕ ܪܚܡܬܗ. ܐܣܿܬܡ̈ܢ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܟܝ ܒܢܘܗܪܗ.
Happy are you, O Zion, for you have thirsted for the Wine that rejoices everything and you


[p. 402] {#po.39.p402}
ܘܢܼܗܪ ܠܒܟܝ ܒܫܘܼܦܪܗ. ܕܒܗ ܐܬܡܛܲܫܝ ܝܼܠܘܕܐ ܡܘܠܕ ܟܠ ܠܥܠܡܗ ܘܕ ܫܿܦܝܪ̈ܶܐ ܀
have been made drunk by it! You have forgotten what is yours and you have been made mad by the drunkenness of His love; your eyes have been blinded by His light, and your heart has been illuminated by His beauty, for in this one was hidden the Child
ܡܐ ܪܲܓܝܓ ܐܢܬ ܘܪܲܚܝܼܡ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ ܝܼܠܘܕܐ ܆ ܘܪ̈ܓܬܟ ܫ̈ܒܝܐ ܢܦܫ̈ܬܐ. ܢܦܩܼܬ̇ ܢܦܫܝ ܒܬܪ̈ܟ. ܘܟܕ ܗܝ ܒܗ̇ ܡܨܕܐ ܠܓܘ ܡܢܗ̇ ܫܘܦܪܟ ܠܗ̇. ܫܘܼܦܪܟ ܕܢܚ܆ ܘܠܕܡܼܘܬܟ ܕܡܼܓܘܬ ܗ ܕܐܒܘܟ ܚܳܙܝܐ ܇ ܘܠܗ ܒܟ ܡܥ̇ܦܩܐ. ܡܐ ܫ̇ܦܝܪ ܐܢܬ ܘܪܲܚܝܼܡ ܐܢܬ ܕܡܐ ܠܐܒܘܗܝ. ܡܐ ܚܠܐ ܐܢܬ ܘܗܢܝ ܆ ܘܛܥܡܟ ܠܛܥܘܡ̈ܝܟ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܝܕܥ. ܣܿܩܬ ܪܝܚܟ ܒܣܝܼܡܐ ܆ ܘܠܒܝ ܐܫܬܿܢܝ ܒܝ »܀. ܘܡܢܘ ܕܢܦܿܫܩ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ. ܡܢܘ ܕܫܡܥܗ ܠܫܡܥܘܢ܆ ܘܐܦ ܐܬܡܙܓ ܒܕܝܠܗ. ܢܼܕܥ ܗܠܝܢ. ܡܢܘ ܕܗܿܠܟ ܒܥܩܒ̈ܬܗ ܢܼܐܬܐ ܒܬܪܝ ܆ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܸܣܛܐ ܡܢ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܢ. ܚܿܙܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܐܘ ܕܟܼܦܪܬ ܥܠ ܬܪܥܐ. ܕܠܗܿܘ ܕܠܙܥܘܪܐ ܛܸܥܢ ܥܠ ܚܕܝܗ ܒܥܘܒܼܟ ܛܥܲܝܢ ܐܢܬ. ܘܡܼܢܘ ܕܢܼܕܥ ܗܕܐ ܙ܀ ܒܫܼܬܩܐ ܢܬܓ̇ܘܣ ܙ, ܕܗ̇ܘ ܕܡܢ ܠܓܘ ܢ̇ܦܩ ܠܬܘܘ̈ܢܝܢ ܆ ܠܫܢܢ ܘ ܠܐ ܙܗܝܼܪ̈ܐ ܒܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܿܦܩ ܐ ܪܕܐ ܠܗ ܀ ܐܚܼܘܕܝ ܬܪ̈ܥܝܟܝ ܐܘܪܝܫܠܡ ܆ ܕܢܩ̇ܘܐ ܚܬܢܟܝ ܒܓܘܟܝ. ܘܫܘܼܥܝ ܘ ܟܘܝ̈ܟܝ ܕܢܐܿܓܪ ܪ̈ܝܼܚ ܒܣܡܢ̈ܘܗܝ ܙ ܟܕ « ܬܪ̈ܥܝܟܝ ܢܬܦܬܚܘܢ ܘܠܟܐ ܘܠܟܐ ܬܸܚܘܪ̈ܝܢ ܆ ܬܒܥ̣ܝܢܝܗܝ ܘܠܝܬܘܗܝ. ܀ ܘܒܕܡܥܝ̈ܟܝ ܘܦܟܝ̈ܟܝ ܬܼܸܨܒܥܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܬܫ̣ܟܚܝܢܝܗܝ. ܘܐܢ ܐܧ ܬܫܟܚܝܢܝܗܝ ܆ ܠܡܚܣܢ ܇ ܠܐ ܐܙ ܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐܝܬ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬܐ. ܡܕܝܢ ܘ ܛܪ̈ܝ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ »ܘ ܒܙܗܝܪܘ ܆ ܕܠܐ ܢܓܕܫܟܝ ܡܕܡ ܕܓܕܫܟܝ ܒܗ ܡܢ ܩܕܝܡ. ܬܢܝ̇ܢܘܬ ܐ ܕܐܢ ܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܀ ܬܥܼܒܕܝܢ ܆ ܐܢܬܝ ܠܗ ܬܿܫܠܡܝܢ ܒܐܝ̈ܕܝ ܡܪ̈ܚܐ. ܘܐܦ ܚܕܘܬܝ ܕܝܠܝ ܬܗܦܘܼܟ ܠܡܪ̈ܩܘܕܬܐ. ܪ̈ܚܡܿܝ ܥ̇ܦܩ ܠܕܝܠܟ ܘܐܬܟ̇ܫܦ ܙܐ ܕܢܗܼܘܐ ܐܦ ܕܝܠܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܝܠܟ ܀ ܡܝ
who gives birth to everything to His splendid world. 3 How desirable and lovable You are, O Child: the desire for You captivates souls. Mine has gone in your wake, and when it fixes its eyes within itself, your beauty appears to it inside it. It sees your image, O Image of your Father, and in You it embraces Him. How beautiful and lovable You are, like to your Father! How sweet and suave You are! Your savor is known only to those who savor You. I have breathed your delicious odor, and within me my heart has been transported. Whoever would want to explain this could not; (but) whoever has heard Simeon and, what is more, has been united to Him who was his, that one will know what it is about. And whoever has walked in his footsteps will follow me, and (may it please God) that we do not stray from our path! I see you, you who have renounced opening your door (I see) that He whom he carried for a moment against his chest, you, you carry Him in your bosom! Whoever knows this will take refuge in silence, for He who, from the inside, proceeds toward our dwellings, our stunned tongue makes Him go out running through the door. 4 Close your doors, Jerusalem, so that your Spouse remains within you! Keep your windows closed so that the odor of His perfumes persists. For when your doors open and you look here and there, you will seek Him in vain; you will bathe your cheeks with your tears and you will not find Him; or if you find Him, it will be with difficulty, and not with the confident freedom that love gives. Guard your doors then with vigilance, for fear that something of what once happened to you might happen again; for if you do not do that, you will deliver Him into the hands of the impudent and my joy will turn into lamentation. My friend, embrace what is yours, and pray that what is mine may also be like what is yours.
& ܛܘܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܡܿܢ ܕܡܕܒܚܗ ܒܗ ܐ ' ܘܩܕܘܫ ܐ ܩܘ̈ܕܫܘܗܝ ܠܓܘ ܡܢܗ܆ ܘܬܡܢ ܢܫܼܡܥ ܒܢ̈ܬ ܩܥ̈ܝܘܗܝ ܕܐܠܗܐ. ܕܒܙܘܥܐ ܢ ܝ ܡܙܝܼܥ ܘܡܣܿܪܕ ܕܠܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܟܠܗܘܢ &܀ ܘܬܡܢ ܢܚܼܙܐ ܥܪ̈ܦܠܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܗܽܘ ܣ̈ܓܝ ܨܡ̈ܚܐ ܇ ܘܒܗ̇ ّ: ܢܬܚ̇ܦܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܚܙܬܐ :ܘܡܢ ܟܠ ܚܙ̈ܝܐ "× ܗܢܘ ܐܬܪܐ
5 Happy is he whose altar is within him and whose Holy of Holies is inside himself! There, he will hear the voice of God who, with (a single) movement, shakes and terrifies all the Rebels. There, he will see the cloud of


[p. 404] {#po.39.p404}
ܩܕܝܫܐ :ܝ ܕܒܗ ܩܕܝܫ ܩܕܝ̈ܫܝܢ ܒܙܒܢ ܘܝ ܒܕܢܚܐ ܙ ܕܙܠܝܩܘ̈ܗܝ ܡܬܚܼܙܐ ܆ ܘܒܙܒܢ ܒܥܡܛܢܐ ܕܫܘܼܒܚܗ ܠܚܙܬܗ ܡܢܿܦܐ ܇ ܒܚܼܙܘܐ ܗܘ ܪܡ ܡܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܘܝܼܕܥܬܐ ܀
light with multiple flashes, and in it he will be hidden from all vision and all seers. It is the holy Place where the Supremely Holy sometimes makes Himself visible by the appearance of His rays, sometimes hides His vision in the darkness of His glory in this spectacle that surpasses all vision and all
& ܡܐ ܐܧ ܕܚܼܡ ܠܒܟ ܆ ܬܡܢ ܐܿܨܕ. ܡܐ ܙ ܕܐܫܬܪܿܝܘ ܗܕܡܝ̈ܟ ܒܪ̈ܬܚܐ ܆ ܬܡܢ ܦܠ ܥܠ ܐܦܝ̈ܟ. ܘܢܗܼܘܐ ܪ̈ܲܚܝܼܡ ܠܟ ܠܡܩܪ̈ܐ ܐܒܐ. ܐܒܐ. ܒܠܚܘܕ ܙ ܆ ܝܬܝܪ ܡܢ ܕܠܡܿܣܓܝܘ ܕܡܥ̈ܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܝܠܺܝܕܝ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܗܝܕܝܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܗܣܝܐܐ ܕܓܡܝܪ̈ܐ. ܟܕ ܚܠܝܼܛܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܐܦ »ܙ ܗܠܝܢ ܠܐ ܨܒܝ̇ܢܐܝܬ. ܘܟܕ ܚ̇ܳܠܐ ܠܟ ܙܘܡܿܪܵܐ ܬܿܡܗ ܘ ܐܢܬ ܠܟ ܐ ܘܬ ܗܪ @܀ ܆ ܟܕ ܡܐܨܕ ܐܢܬ ܒܫܡܫܟ + ܘܒܐܣ̈ܝܟ ܕܢܝܼܚ ܧ ܠܐ ܟܠܝܢܐܝܬ. ܗܕܐ ܙܘ ܗܝ ܬܫܡܫܬܐ :܀ ܕܓܒܝܐ ܕ ܠܬܐ ܕܢܫܿܡܫ ܀
knowledge. 6 When your heart becomes burning, that is where you must fix your eyes; when your members disjoin under the effect of the fervor, fall there on your face. Love to cry out simply: Father! Father! more than to shed more of these tears of children born of the Spirit: (this exclamation expresses) then the confident freedom of the perfect, these (tears) being also mingled there without one wishing it. And when it is sweet for you to chant, you are seized with stupor and wonder when you fix your eyes on your Sun that shines without obstacle in you and in the walls of your cell. That is the service that pleases Him who came to serve.
ܘܐܢ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܛܒ̈ܢ ܣܦܝܼܩ ܐܢܬ ܘܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ :ܐ ܫܓܝܫܝܢ ܘ ܆ ܨܿܠܐ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܟܣܝܐܝܬ ܘܕܠܐ ܦܣܿܩ. ܐܠܗܝ ܘ ܚܶܟܡܝܢܝ ܘܚܿܝܠܝܢܝ. ܗܕܐ ܒܟܠܝܙܒܢ ܣܿܦܩܐ ܀ܒܗܠܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܐ ܒܢܝܼܫܐ ܗܢܐ ܕܒܠܒܐ ܠܝ̇ܕܥ ܟܠ ܡܬܚ̈ܘܝܐ. ܚ̇ܟܡܢܢܝ ܡܪܝ ܕܐܕܥ ܨܒܝܢܟ ܟܠܗ ܆ ܘܚܿܝܠܝܢܝ ܕܐܐܓܡܪܝܘܗܝ. ܘܚ̇ܟܡܢܢܝ ܕܐܕܥ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܕܫ̈ܐܢܐ ܘܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܆ ܘܚܿܝܠܝܢܝ ܕܐܙܟܐ ܠܟܠܗܝܢ. ܗܕܐ ܘܘ ܨܠܘܬܐ ܚܒܘܫܬܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܬ̇ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܣܓܝܐܬܿ ܘܘ ܣܘ̈ܟܼܠܐ ܠܝ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܗܕܐ ܫܟܝܼܚܐ ܿ ܀
7 If you are empty of all good, and if the adverse forces rise up, say this prayer secretly and without interruption: "My God, instruct me and strengthen me!". It suffices at every moment in this life, for in the heart appears to the Omniscient the following meaning: "Instruct me, Lord, so that I may know all your will, and strengthen me so that I may accomplish it. Instruct me so that I may know the artifices of the demons and everything that is adverse, and strengthen me so that I may conquer all of that". This condensed prayer, small in words but rich in meaning, I say myself in preference to any other.
ܠܙ ܕܬܠܬܝܢ ܘܫܒܥ ܥܠ ܓܠܝܼܙܘܬ ܒܘܝ̈ܐܐ
